# Lapidary: Implementation Plan

This file is the design log, written for the coding agents that build the app. Users read `README.md`; developers read `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

Written 2026-09-08. This plan is meant to be executed by a Claude Code session phase by phase. Read `CLAUDE.md` first for repo rules.

## 0. What we are building

A Windows desktop app (Electron) for reviewing Claude-suggested copyedits to novel chapters written as Markdown in an Obsidian vault. The author sees their original text double-spaced, with each suggested change floating above the words it would replace. Each change can be accepted or dismissed at any time, in either direction. Dismissed changes stay visible but faded. Accepted changes swap places with the original, which becomes the faded ghost. Paragraph-level comments (continuity, plot, flow) appear in a side panel. Claude is called from inside the app using the author's own Claude subscription; since Phase 5c OpenAI can answer instead, on an API key the author pastes into Settings (section 5.0). The author can also write their own edits, which become suggestions like any other. Nothing is ever written into an existing manuscript file except through an explicit Save to chapter, which first copies the version it replaces into the app's data folder (6.5); nothing saves to the chapter by itself. The only other writes to the manuscript folder create a file that is not there yet, a new chapter or the guidelines file on first use, and cannot touch one that is. The one exception is a reviews folder the author keeps inside the manuscript folder (6.1): there the app saves over, moves and removes its own review files and the small records it keeps beside them (6.4) as it would anywhere else, and never a chapter (CLAUDE.md rule 1).

## 1. Locked decisions

| Decision              | Choice                                                                                                                                                                      | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| --------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Shell                 | Electron 44 + electron-vite 5 + Vite 7 + React 19 + TypeScript (see Appendix C.1)                                                                                                              | The Node main process hosts the provider layer (the user's Claude Code run as a child process, the `openai` package), file I/O, and the watcher with no friction.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Claude access         | The Claude Code each user installs and signs in to themselves, found on their computer (5.6 item 1) and run from the main process as `claude -p` over stream-json: every built-in tool removed, no settings files, the app's system prompt and its JSON schema for structured output (11.3 q, C.42). The app ships nothing of Anthropic's; `cli.1` replaced `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk`, which bundled a `claude.exe`. | The author accepts no Anthropic terms and ships nothing of Anthropic's (Appendix D 8 and 21). A Claude Code from Anthropic's installer keeps itself current (a WinGet or Homebrew copy is updated by hand), so new models need no release of the app. Signed in with a Claude plan, `claude -p` draws on the plan's usage limits (Help Center, 2026-10-07, Appendix A); with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the environment, which Claude Code always uses in `-p` mode when it is there, reviews are billed per token to the key's Console account. |
| Auth handling         | The app never reads, stores, or proxies credentials, and never starts a sign-in. The user's own Claude Code reads the login it wrote itself (C.42; the app ships no Claude Code since `cli.1`). Setup (5.6) shows the commands, `claude auth login` among them, for the user to run in their own terminal. Apart from reviews, the `initialize` exchange of each check and Test the connection, which run `claude -p` (11.3 q), the app runs only `--version` and `auth status --json` (`runClaudeCli` refuses anything else), keeping only whitelisted fields, and reads the account from Claude Code's `initialize` reply (CLAUDE.md rule 2 (a); Appendix C "No in-app sign-in launcher"). | Required by Anthropic's policy: third-party developers may not offer Claude.ai login in their own applications, and sign-in to a Claude account must complete through Anthropic's own flow (legal page, re-read 2026-10-19, Appendix A).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Claude setup          | A launch check that sends no model request (`--version`, `auth status --json` and the `initialize` exchange), read into one of nine views; a Setup screen in place of the main view that says what to do in each, with commands to copy, and checks again as the window comes back to the front; Test the connection, the one request Setup sends, only when pressed; usage notices in Claude Code's own figures, from 80% of a limit (5.6, `setup.2` to `setup.9`). | For most people the first run starts with Claude Code not installed or not signed in. The old check said ready when signed out and spent two model requests on every launch (11.1). |
| First run             | A welcome guide while `onboardingDone` is false: Connect Claude, Choose your manuscript folder and Fill in your style sheet, each saying when it is done; Start once a folder is chosen, Skip at any time, and Show the welcome guide again in Settings (section 7, `setup.11`). Review with AI's Guidelines row says when the style sheet is still empty (5.5, `setup.10b`). | The friend sets the app up alone, from the README and the app (11.0), and a reviewer without the style sheet "fixes" names, invented words and the spelling variant. |
| Primary Claude output | A change list: `{changes: [{paragraph, before, after, category, reason}], comments: [...]}`                                                                                 | The author's edits are a few words at a time. Short exact quotes anchor reliably, output is tiny, and every change carries a reason and category. Larger issues become comments by design.                                                                                                                                                |
| Secondary input       | Full-text diff (a corrected copy of the open chapter, pasted or loaded from a file)                                                                                          | Needed for "Submit manual review" (7.5) and for imports. Same hunk model, same UI.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Folders               | User picks a manuscript folder and a reviews folder, anywhere on disk                                                                                                       | Inside or outside the vault does not matter. Reviews are `.json`, which Obsidian does not index.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Sync                  | File watcher + content hashes + text-anchored hunks that re-anchor on external change                                                                                       | Obsidian and this app can both touch the file; neither may clobber the other silently.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Writing the chapter   | Explicit Save to chapter only: the toolbar button, Ctrl+S and the Save in the review dialogs and the leave and close prompts all run one `saveChapter` (6.3). No autosave: decisions are kept automatically in the review, which is not the chapter (6.4), and nothing writes the chapter by itself. Before a save that changes the file, the bytes it replaces are copied to `<userData>/backups`; no backup, no save (6.5). Main refuses any target but a chapter in the manuscript folder or opened from it (6.1, CLAUDE.md rule 1). | The author decides when the chapter changes, and a write in the background would race Obsidian's own autosave and a sync tool (11.0). The friend may not use git, so a copy outside the vault, never listed, indexed or synced, is what undoes a save. |
| Model                 | Setting, default `default`: no `--model` flag, so Claude Code uses the account's own default (`modelArgs`, `setup.1`). A settings file that names a model keeps it, and a new user on a Pro plan gets `sonnet` preselected once, when a check finds Claude ready while the first welcome guide is open; Start or Skip before that leaves Default, and the guide brought back from Settings never preselects (Appendix D 12, C.67). List populated from Claude Code's `initialize` reply, each entry labelled with its version and grouped by family (`shared/ai/claudeModels.ts`, round 5). Effort setting, default `high`. | What Default means is Claude Code's to decide, by its version and the account. Since Claude Code 2.1.280 it is Opus 5.5 on every plan, Pro included, where it was Sonnet 5 before; an `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_MODEL` in the environment or an organisation's default model changes it, while the user's own `/model` choice, kept in their settings file, does not reach the app (Appendix C "Default is Opus 5.5 on Pro since CLI 2.1.280"). Opus uses a Pro plan's limits fastest, so on a Pro plan the review dialog notes that Opus models, Default included, use the limits fastest, and the first welcome guide preselects Sonnet. |
| OpenAI access (5c)    | Official `openai` package (7.15.x), Responses API, strict `json_schema` text format, streamed. API key stored encrypted with `safeStorage`, `OPENAI_API_KEY` as fallback.   | OpenAI's documented route for a third-party app is an API key billed per token (Appendix A). The key is the one credential the app stores, so it is encrypted, main-process only, and never shown again (CLAUDE.md rule 2).                                                                                                               |

## 2. Repository layout

```
ai-grammar-checker/
  package.json
  electron.vite.config.ts
  tsconfig*.json
  src/
    main/                 Electron main process (Node)
      index.ts            window creation, app lifecycle
      ipc.ts              IPC handlers, typed against src/shared/ipc.ts
      files.ts            list .md and .txt, read (EOL/BOM detection), what Save may write, atomic write, exclusive create, sha256
      backups.ts          the copy Save makes before it replaces a chapter, under <userData>/backups (6.5)
      watcher.ts          chokidar on the manuscript folder, own-write suppression
      reviews.ts          review store: create, load the current review (6.4), save decisions, follow a moved chapter
      drafts.ts           open paragraph boxes kept under <userData>/drafts (7.4, 11.3 j)
      closeGuard.ts       holds a close that would put work at risk and asks the page (7.7)
      providers/          the AI provider layer (5.0)
        types.ts          ReviewProvider interface, hooks, ProviderResult
        registry.ts       provider lookup by id, no Electron
        index.ts          the real registry, in-flight requests for Cancel
        claude.ts         the Claude provider: runs the user's Claude Code (11.3 q, C.42), progress, error mapping, account info
        claudeBinary.ts   finds the user's `claude`, runs `--version` and `auth status --json`, and supplies the review's `spawn`
        claudeCli.ts      the stream-json exchange with `claude`: `initialize`, the chapter, the result
        claudeOptions.ts  the child's flags and environment (`claudeArgs`, `buildClaudeEnv`, CLAUDE.md rule 6)
        claudeRuntime.ts  the launch check: installed, new enough, signed in (5.6, 11.3 f)
        openai.ts         Responses API call, streaming, error mapping, model list
      secrets.ts          the OpenAI key: safeStorage-encrypted secrets.json
      settings.ts         settings JSON in app.getPath('userData')
    preload/
      index.ts            contextBridge exposing a typed `api` object only
    renderer/             React UI, no Node access
      index.html, main.tsx, App.tsx
      components/         FileList, ReviewPane, HunkSpan, HunkTooltip, MarginNotes, SidePanel, Toolbar,
                          EditPopover, ReviewDialog, NewChapterDialog, ManualReviewDialog,
                          SettingsDialog, SetupScreen, StatusBar
      state/              store (zustand), selectors, keyboard bindings
      styles/
    shared/               pure TypeScript, no Node or DOM imports, fully unit-tested
      types.ts            Hunk, ReviewComment, Review, Settings, enums
      branding.ts         APP_NAME and APP_DATA_DIR, the only place the name is written
      edit.ts             page layout of a span, paragraph-edit planning, supersede, manual hunks
      ipc.ts              channel names and payload types
      text/
        paragraphs.ts     split into paragraphs with offsets, frontmatter detection
        eol.ts            detect and restore CRLF/LF, BOM, trailing newline
      diff/
        wordDiff.ts       full-text -> hunks (paragraph align, then word diff)
        anchor.ts         change list -> hunks (exact, neighbor, fuzzy, stale)
        compose.ts        base text + hunk decisions -> output text
        rebase.ts         re-anchor an existing review onto changed text
      prompt/
        system.ts         static instructions + guidelines assembly
        schema.ts         zod schema for Claude's output and JSON Schema export (draft-07)
        chapter.ts        render numbered paragraphs for the user message
      ai/
        openai.ts         model filter and default, effort map, key wording (no zod: the renderer imports it)
        openaiReply.ts    strict-schema transform and reply parsing (carries the zod schema)
        pricing.ts        OpenAI price table for the cost estimate
  resources/
    GUIDELINES.default.md copied into the manuscript folder on first use if absent
  test/                   vitest, mirrors src/shared
  PLAN.md  CLAUDE.md
```

Dependencies as installed on 2026-09-08: electron 44.3.0, electron-vite 5.0.0, vite 7.3.6, @vitejs/plugin-react 5.2.0, react 19.2.8, zod 4.5.4, diff 9.0.0 (jsdiff), diff-match-patch 1.0.5, chokidar 5.0.0, @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk 0.3.266 (bundles a 219 MB native win32-x64 binary as an optional dependency; upgraded to 0.3.283 on 2026-09-27, whose bundled CLI knows Opus 5.5; removed by `cli.1`, C.42). Add zustand, electron-builder, vitest, eslint, prettier, fast-check. See Appendix C.1 for why Vite is 7 rather than 8.

## 3. Data model (`src/shared/types.ts`)

```ts
export type ChangeCategory =
  | 'spelling'
  | 'typo'
  | 'homophone'
  | 'grammar'
  | 'agreement'
  | 'tense'
  | 'punctuation'
  | 'word-choice'
  | 'redundancy'
  | 'awkward'
  | 'consistency'
  | 'formatting'
  | 'other';

export type CommentCategory =
  | 'consistency'
  | 'plot'
  | 'flow'
  | 'pacing'
  | 'clarity'
  | 'repetition'
  | 'pov'
  | 'style'
  | 'suggestion'
  | 'other';

export type HunkStatus = 'pending' | 'accepted' | 'dismissed';
export type AnchorQuality = 'exact' | 'fuzzy' | 'ambiguous' | 'stale';

export interface Hunk {
  id: string;
  start: number; // char offset in review.base.text (LF-normalized)
  end: number; // exclusive
  baseSide: 'original' | 'replacement'; // which text currently occupies [start,end) in base
  original: string; // may be '' for a pure insertion
  replacement: string; // may be '' for a pure deletion
  paragraph: number; // paragraph index at anchor time, for display and navigation
  category?: ChangeCategory;
  reason?: string;
  source: 'claude' | 'diff' | 'manual'; // 'manual' is an edit the author typed (7.4)
  status: HunkStatus;
  anchor: AnchorQuality; // 'stale' hunks are listed in the side panel, not inline
  edited?: boolean; // a model suggestion whose wording the author rewrote
  supersededBy?: string; // id of the manual hunk whose span swallowed this one
}

export interface ReviewComment {
  id: string;
  paragraph: number | null; // null when Claude gave no usable anchor, or when its paragraph left the file
  wasParagraph?: number; // the paragraph it had when a change on disk took that paragraph away (ui.7, C.60)
  quote?: string; // short excerpt Claude referenced, if any
  category: CommentCategory;
  text: string; // may include a suggested rewrite
  status: 'open' | 'resolved';
}

export interface Review {
  version: 1;
  id: string; // <chapter-slug>--<ISO timestamp>
  sourcePath: string; // absolute path of the chapter; the review belongs to exactly this file (files.1, C.45)
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
  decidedAt?: string; // files.1: last decision (suggestion, comment, save); picks the review a chapter opens with only while none is recorded as last on screen (apply.26, 6.4)
  base: { text: string; hash: string; eol: 'lf' | 'crlf'; bom: boolean; trailingNewline: boolean };
  generator: {
    // 'manual' was called 'paste' until Phase 5b; migrateReview renames it on load.
    // For a model-written review the kind is the provider id (Phase 5c).
    kind: 'claude' | 'openai' | 'manual' | 'file';
    provider?: 'claude' | 'openai'; // Phase 5c; withGeneratorProvider fills 'claude' on load when missing
    model?: string; // model that actually answered (modelUsage key, or the Responses API's model)
    effort?: string;
    guidelinesHash?: string;
    costUsd?: number; // Claude: Claude Code's own estimate (`total_cost_usd`). OpenAI: this app's price table, only for known models
    costEstimated?: boolean; // true when costUsd is the app's own arithmetic; the status bar says "estimate"
    usage?: unknown; // result.modelUsage or response.usage, stored verbatim
    durationMs?: number;
    sessionId?: string;
    fallbackNote?: string; // set when a safety classifier moved the request to a fallback model (5.1)
    paragraphBase?: 0 | 1; // Phase 5d: what the paragraph numbers in `raw` count from (5.2); absent on older reviews
  };
  raw?: unknown; // untouched structured_output from Claude, never edited
  hunks: Hunk[];
  comments: ReviewComment[];
}
```

Settings (`src/shared/types.ts`; every key normalised with its default in `src/shared/settings.ts`, 11.3 d):

```ts
export interface Settings {
  manuscriptFolder: string | null;
  reviewsFolder: string | null; // null: <userData>/reviews (6.4)
  guidelinesPath: string | null; // null: <manuscriptFolder>/GUIDELINES.md (10)
  provider: 'claude' | 'openai'; // default 'claude'; the review dialog's Provider list (5.0, 5.5)
  model: string; // the Claude model; default 'default', passed as no --model flag (section 1, setup.1)
  openaiModel: string; // default '': the newest gpt-5 the key can see
  effort: EffortLevel; // default 'high', shared by both providers
  zoom: number; // 1 is normal size (section 7 Zoom)
  theme: 'system' | 'light' | 'dark'; // default 'system' (11.3 d and e)
  onboardingDone: boolean; // the welcome guide was finished or skipped (section 7, setup.11)
  dismissedHints: string[]; // 'pro-model-preselected' (setup.1, C.67), 'save-to-chapter' (apply.12)
  checkForUpdates: boolean; // default true
  updateDownloadConsent: string | null; // the version the author agreed to download
  sideSplit: number | null; // the side panel's split; null is the usual one (ui.6)
}
```

`model` stays the Claude model under its old name so every settings.json on disk still reads correctly; `modelFor(settings, provider)` picks the right one, so neither provider can be sent the other's model. `effort` is shared. `onboardingDone`, when the key is missing, is true for settings that already name a manuscript folder, so an install from before 1.0 never sees the welcome guide unasked. The OpenAI key is not a setting and is never in settings.json (CLAUDE.md rule 2).

What the setup screens read (5.6, section 7), in `src/shared/ipc.ts` unless named:

```ts
interface RuntimeStatus {
  // ai:runtime: what the check of a provider found; nothing is cached across launches
  provider: 'claude' | 'openai';
  state: 'ready' | 'missing-binary' | 'not-signed-in' | 'error';
  executablePath?: string; // the Claude Code found (5.6 item 1)
  cliVersion?: string; // its `--version`
  minCliVersion?: string; // MIN_CLAUDE_CODE
  credential?: 'subscription' | 'api-key' | 'cloud' | 'auth-token'; // what Claude Code signs in with (C.71)
  authMethod?: string; // from `auth status --json`
  planWarning?: string; // the free plan, which cannot run Claude Code
  checkedAt?: string;
  email?: string; // shown, never logged (11.3 m)
  subscriptionType?: string; // 'pro', 'max', ...
  apiKeySource?: string; // such as ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, never the key
  message?: string; // the check's own words when it is not ready
  keySource?: 'stored' | 'environment' | 'none'; // OpenAI
  keyLast4?: string; // OpenAI: never the key itself
}
interface RateLimitInfo {
  // a usage notice from Claude Code's rate_limit_event (setup.9, C.64)
  status?: string; // allowed, allowed_warning or rejected
  resetsAt?: string;
  kind?: string; // five_hour, seven_day, seven_day_opus, ...
  utilization?: number; // 0 to 1, sent only with allowed_warning
  usingOverage?: boolean; // extra usage, which may be billed, is paying
}
interface ConnectionTestResult {
  // ai:test, Test the connection (setup.8, C.63): answered, never thrown
  ok: boolean;
  model?: string;
  durationMs: number;
  error?: AppError;
  rateLimit?: RateLimitInfo;
}
interface GuidelinesStatus {
  // guidelines:status, read-only (10, setup.10b, setup.11)
  path: string | null;
  exists: boolean;
  styleSheet: 'blank' | 'filled' | 'missing';
}
interface LastFailure {
  // src/shared/ui/setup.ts, page only: the last review or test failure that bears on setup (setup.6)
  code: ErrorCode;
  executablePath?: string;
  cliVersion?: string;
  signedOutSince?: boolean;
  planLimit?: boolean; // a usage limit that is one of the plan's own (setup.9 review)
}
```

Invariants:

- Hunks never overlap. Sorted by `start`. Anchoring rejects the later of two overlapping hunks and records it as `stale` with a note in `reason`.
- An edit the author types is a hunk like any other (`source: 'manual'`), so the composed output stays a pure function of base plus decisions and every edit is reversible and re-anchorable. A hunk carrying `supersededBy` stays `stale` through a rebase: its words often still exist inside the edit that replaced them, and re-anchoring it there would undo a decision the author already made.
- `base.text` is the file content normalized to LF with BOM removed. All offsets refer to it. EOL, BOM, and trailing-newline flags are restored on write.
- YAML frontmatter at the top of the file (`---` ... `---`) is never inside any hunk and is never sent to Claude as an editable paragraph.
- The composed output text is a pure function of `base` and `hunks`. See `compose.ts`.

## 4. Text algorithms (`src/shared`)

### 4.1 Paragraphs (`text/paragraphs.ts`)

Split `base.text` on runs of blank lines (`/\n[ \t]*\n+/`), keeping exact `start`/`end` offsets for each paragraph and preserving the separators. A paragraph index is stable for the life of a review. Frontmatter is returned separately with `isFrontmatter: true` and excluded from numbering sent to Claude.

### 4.2 Compose (`diff/compose.ts`)

For each hunk in offset order, the desired side is `replacement` when `status === 'accepted'`, otherwise `original`. If `desiredSide !== baseSide`, splice the other text into the span. Because hunks do not overlap, a single left-to-right pass with an offset delta is enough. Restore EOL/BOM/trailing newline last.

### 4.3 Change list to hunks (`diff/anchor.ts`)

Input: Claude's `changes[]` and the paragraph list.

1. Look for `before` as an exact substring of paragraph `p`. One match: `exact`. Several matches: choose the first and mark `ambiguous`.
2. No match: try paragraphs `p-2..p+2` (Claude occasionally miscounts). Found once: `exact` with corrected paragraph index.
3. Still nothing: fuzzy match with diff-match-patch `match_main` against the whole text near the paragraph's start offset, `Match_Threshold` 0.3. Accept only if the matched span has small edit distance relative to `before` length (for example, Levenshtein ratio under 0.2). Mark `fuzzy` and set `original` to the actual matched text.
4. Otherwise `stale`. The hunk keeps `before`/`after` for display in the side panel.
5. Normalize: if `before` and `after` share a common prefix and suffix, trim both so the hunk covers only the differing words. This makes the floating suggestion short and precise.
6. Drop any hunk where trimmed `original === replacement`.
7. Resolve overlaps as in the invariants.

### 4.4 Full-text diff to hunks (`diff/wordDiff.ts`)

1. Split both texts into paragraphs. Align them with jsdiff `diffArrays` on paragraph strings (exact equality first; if a paragraph was edited it shows as remove+add).
2. For each removed/added paragraph pair in sequence (pair by position within the changed block; unpaired ones become whole-paragraph hunks), run `diffWordsWithSpace` and group consecutive changed tokens into one hunk: `original` = concatenated removed tokens, `replacement` = concatenated added tokens. Adjacent hunks separated only by whitespace are merged.
3. Offsets are computed in the original text. `source: 'diff'`, no category or reason.

### 4.5 Rebase (`diff/rebase.ts`)

When the file changes on disk (or before a Save when the disk hash differs from what the app last knew):

1. New base = disk text, normalized.
2. For every hunk, the text expected in the new base is `baseSide === 'original' ? original : replacement`. Also try the other side: if the user already applied the change by hand in Obsidian, the replacement is what exists now. Search near the old offset first (window of a few hundred characters), then the whole text, then fuzzy. Update `start`, `end`, `baseSide`, `anchor`. A side that holds no text, the empty side of a deletion or an insertion, is a zero-width place found by the text that surrounded the hunk (48 characters either side together, then 24 and 12, then either side alone), never inside a word, and it competes with the other side's words (`apply.23`, C.52). Candidates rank by word boundaries, then by how much of the 40 characters either side still reads as before (where both texts begin or end, the edge counts as reading as before), then the side the base holds, then distance. The other side wins, and the decision follows it (C.5), only where the file plainly shows the change made or undone there: an empty place whose surroundings read as before in full on one side, or for at least 12 characters of whole words on both (a side with less old text than that says nothing), once copies of the hunk's own words within 40 characters of it have been weighed against it in whole words (`settleCopies`, the `apply.23` review): a copy that reads further takes the place's claim away; words whose surroundings read as before on both sides, or on one side when they did not stand beside a copy of themselves (the twin of a doubled word); or, for a hunk whose own side has words, the only such words in the file. A decision Save would write stays only where its own side is plainly in place: words with one side of their surroundings as before, or the only such words when they did not stand beside a copy of themselves, and not tied with a place beside them; an empty place with one side as before in full and the other for at least 12 characters of whole words. Only words are placed by the fuzzy search: a deletion or an insertion with no place found exactly goes to Unanchored.
3. Hunks found nowhere become `stale` and drop out of the inline view, and so does a hunk whose other side seems to be in the file but not plainly, or whose unsaved decision would otherwise be written at a guessed place. A deletion or an insertion that is stale stays stale: only its surroundings could place it, and a stale hunk has none.
4. Comments keep their paragraph index if the paragraph text still exists (match by first 40 characters, then by the quote), otherwise `paragraph: null`, with the index they had in `wasParagraph`, so the side panel can say where they were (`ui.7`, C.60). A comment already unanchored keeps that number, and nothing anchors it again.
   Property tests should confirm: compose(rebase(review, compose(review))) reproduces the same output, and status is preserved across a round trip.

### 4.6 EOL and BOM (`text/eol.ts`)

Detect `\r\n` presence, a leading UTF-8 BOM, and a trailing newline on read. Normalize to LF internally. Restore all three on write so git diffs and Obsidian see only the intended changes.

## 5. AI integration (`src/main/providers/`, `src/shared/prompt`, `src/shared/ai`)

### 5.0 The provider layer (Phase 5c)

Every service that can answer "Review with AI" implements one interface in `src/main/providers/types.ts`:

```ts
interface ReviewProvider {
  id: 'claude' | 'openai';
  label: string;
  runtime(): Promise<RuntimeStatus>; // can it run now, and under whose account or key
  models(): Promise<ModelInfo[]>; // in the order the dialog shows them
  review(request: ReviewRequest, hooks: { onProgress; onRateLimit?; signal }): Promise<ProviderResult>;
  test?(hooks: { onRateLimit?; signal }): Promise<{ model? }>; // Test the connection (setup.8); throws when it fails
}
// ProviderResult: { output: ReviewOutput (the shared zod schema), raw, model, usage, costUsd?, costEstimated?, durationMs, ... }
```

- **IPC is provider-neutral.** `ai:runtime`, `ai:models`, `ai:review`, `ai:cancel` and `ai:test` each name the provider in the payload (`ai:test`, `setup.8`: one very small request, answered with `{ok, model?, durationMs, error?, rateLimit?}` rather than a thrown error, after at most 60 s; `testConnection` in `providers/connectionTest.ts` times it, aborts it at the limit or on quitting, and answers a provider without `test` with "has no connection test"; OpenAI's is one unstreamed Responses call with the same system prompt to the model a review would use when none is chosen, `store: false`, tested against the mock but offered by no button yet); progress and rate-limit notices arrive as `ai:progress` and `ai:rate-limit`. The IPC layer (`src/main/ipc.ts`) owns request ids, Cancel (an `AbortSignal` per request) and the broadcasts; providers only call their hooks, so no provider imports IPC. The key has three channels of its own, `secrets:openai-status`, `secrets:openai-set` and `secrets:openai-clear`, and none of them ever returns the key.
- **Everything downstream is shared.** Both providers get the same system prompt (5.4) and the same numbered chapter (5.2), and both replies go through the same zod schema (5.3), the same anchoring, hunks and comments. A review records `generator.provider` and the model that answered; the status bar and the history badges show both.
- **Claude** (`providers/claude.ts`) is 5.1 and 5.6 unchanged, moved behind the interface. Since `cli.1` it runs the user's `claude` instead of the SDK (11.3 q, C.42).
- **OpenAI** (`providers/openai.ts`) uses the official `openai` package's Responses API:
  - `instructions` = the system prompt, `input` = the numbered chapter, `text.format` = `{type: 'json_schema', name: 'manuscript_review', strict: true, schema}`, `reasoning.effort` from the app's effort, `store: false`, `stream: true`, and the request's `AbortSignal`.
  - The schema is the draft-07 export rewritten by `toStrictJsonSchema` (`src/shared/ai/openaiReply.ts`) into the strict subset: every property required, `additionalProperties: false` everywhere, optional properties made nullable, and unsupported constraint keywords dropped. On the way back `stripOptionalNulls` removes exactly the nulls that transform introduced (a comment's `quote: null`), keeps legal ones (`paragraph: null`), and the shared zod schema validates the result. `raw` keeps what the model sent.
  - Streaming: `response.output_text.delta` accumulates the text and drives the progress label with an estimated token count (four characters per token) until `response.completed` supplies the real usage. A stream that ends without `response.completed`, or with `response.incomplete` or `response.failed`, is an error.
  - Models: `models.list()`, filtered by `isTextModel` (drops embeddings, speech, images, moderation, realtime, transcription, search and fine-tunes), newest first by `created`; the default is the newest id starting with `gpt-5`, else the newest text model. The list is fetched when the dialog first shows OpenAI, not at launch.
  - Effort: `OPENAI_EFFORT_FOR` maps low/medium/high/xhigh/max. The installed SDK's `ReasoningEffort` accepts all five, so the map is the identity (C.25); the dialog says that not every model accepts every level.
  - Errors: 401/403 -> "OpenAI rejected the API key" with an Open Settings button on the card; 429 -> "rate limiting this key, or the account is out of credit"; 5xx, connection failures and a garbled stream -> a retry hint; 400/404/422 -> the API's own message plus "the model or the effort level may not be available"; a reply that fails to parse -> a message naming the model that answered and what was wrong. Cancel maps to `cancelled` like Claude's.
  - Cost: token usage is always stored. USD is shown only when the model is in `src/shared/ai/pricing.ts` (standard-tier prices copied 2026-09-11), and then with the word "estimate". An unknown model gets no cost rather than a guessed one.
  - `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_OPENAI_BASE_URL` (formerly `STET_OPENAI_BASE_URL`), a development switch that packaged builds ignore (C.39), points the SDK at another base URL. Otherwise the client is always built with `https://api.openai.com/v1` and a null organization and project, so the SDK never takes them from `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `OPENAI_ORG_ID` or `OPENAI_PROJECT_ID`. It exists so the path can be driven against `test/helpers/mockOpenAi.ts`; nothing sets it in normal use.
- **The key** (`src/main/secrets.ts`): Settings has a password field with Save key and Remove key. Main encrypts with `safeStorage.encryptString` and writes base64 ciphertext plus the last four characters to `<userData>/secrets.json`. If `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` is false, it refuses to store and Settings says why. `OPENAI_API_KEY` is used only when nothing is stored, and Settings and the dialog say which source is active. The key is decrypted only inside `providers/openai.ts`'s client factory, per call.

### 5.1 The request

_Superseded by C.42 and 11.3 (q): there is no SDK. The app runs the user's `claude` with the flags in `claudeOptions.ts`, sends the system prompt and the schema in the `initialize` request, and `claudeCli.ts` reads the same messages listed below from its stream-json output; the usage-limit prefixes are copies in `src/shared/ai/claudeStatus.ts`. The SDK call is kept as the record of what those flags replace._

```ts
import { query } from '@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk';

const q = query({
  prompt: renderChapter(paragraphs), // see 5.2
  options: {
    systemPrompt: buildSystemPrompt(guidelinesMd), // static instructions + GUIDELINES.md
    model: settings.model, // alias such as 'opus'
    effort: settings.effort, // 'low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'xhigh' | 'max'
    outputFormat: { type: 'json_schema', schema: reviewOutputJsonSchema }, // draft-07
    maxTurns: 1, // raise if structured output needs more, see risks
    settingSources: [], // do not load the user's ~/.claude settings or CLAUDE.md
    persistSession: false,
    includePartialMessages: true, // progress from message_delta usage
    disallowedTools: ALL_BUILTIN_TOOLS, // see risks: verify the current tool names in sdk.d.ts
    cwd: app.getPath('userData'), // never the vault
    env: { ...process.env },
    abortController,
    stderr: (line) => log.debug(line)
  }
});
```

Message handling while iterating `q`:

- `stream_event` with `event.type === 'message_delta'`: read `usage.output_tokens` for a progress counter.
- `system` / `model_refusal_fallback`: note the fallback in the review's generator info.
- `system` / `model_refusal_no_fallback`: surface `api_refusal_explanation` to the user.
- `rate_limit_event`: read its `rate_limit_info` into `RateLimitInfo` (`readRateLimit` in `providers/claudeResult.ts`, `setup.9`): the status, the reset, which limit (`rateLimitType`, kept as `kind`), the share of it used (`utilization`, a fraction, which Claude Code 2.1.302 sends only with `allowed_warning`, while it warns) and whether extra usage is paying (`isUsingOverage`, kept as `usingOverage`, set on a `rejected` notice). The status bar words it with `describeRateLimit` (section 7), and the card for a review that stops at a usage limit names the limit from it (5.6 item 4, C.64).
- `assistant` with `error` set (`authentication_failed`, `rate_limit`, `billing_error`, ...): map to a user-facing message. `authentication_failed` opens the sign-in instructions.
- `result`: success requires `subtype === 'success'` and a present `structured_output`. Parse with zod. Any other outcome is an error shown with the SDK's message. Record `total_cost_usd`, `modelUsage`, `duration_ms`, `session_id`.
- The `for await` loop can throw after an error result. Wrap it.

Exported constants `USAGE_LIMIT_ERROR_PREFIXES`, `USAGE_WARNING_PREFIXES`, and `USAGE_TRANSITION_PREFIXES` from the SDK identify usage-limit text in results. Use them.

### 5.2 Chapter rendering (`prompt/chapter.ts`)

Send paragraphs numbered so the model can reference them. **Numbers start at 1** (Phase 5d), the same numbers the author sees in the rail beside each paragraph (7), so a comment that says "see paragraph 12" means the paragraph labelled 12:

```
<chapter file="Chapter 12.md" paragraphs="57">
[1] It was the third night of rain, and the river had began to climb the steps.

[2] "You should of told me," Mara said.
...
</chapter>
```

The app itself keeps 0-based indices everywhere (`Paragraph.index`, `Hunk.paragraph`, `ReviewComment.paragraph`). `paragraphNumber` and `paragraphIndexFromNumber` in `prompt/chapter.ts` are the only crossing between the two, and `fromPromptNumbering` converts a model's reply once, in the renderer, before `createAiReview` anchors it; a model that answers 0 anyway is clamped to the first paragraph. The system prompt tells the model the numbering and to cite paragraph numbers, never line numbers. Reviews record `generator.paragraphBase: 1`, which says what the numbers in `raw` count from; older model reviews, prompted from 0, have none. Their stored hunks and comments were always 0-based and display correctly; only the free text of an old comment ("see paragraph 37") still counts from 0.

Markdown is left exactly as written. Frontmatter is omitted. The parser strips nothing on the way back because Claude returns quotes, not the chapter.

### 5.3 Output schema (`prompt/schema.ts`)

```ts
export const ChangeSchema = z.object({
  paragraph: z.number().int().min(0),
  before: z.string().min(1), // exact text copied from that paragraph; shortest span that is unique in the paragraph
  after: z.string(), // '' means delete
  category: z.enum(CHANGE_CATEGORIES),
  reason: z.string().max(240)
});
export const CommentSchema = z.object({
  paragraph: z.number().int().min(0).nullable(),
  quote: z.string().max(200).optional(),
  category: z.enum(COMMENT_CATEGORIES),
  text: z.string().max(1200)
});
export const ReviewOutputSchema = z.object({
  changes: z.array(ChangeSchema),
  comments: z.array(CommentSchema)
});
export const reviewOutputJsonSchema = z.toJSONSchema(ReviewOutputSchema, { target: 'draft-7' });
```

Keep it shallow. The SDK validates against draft-07 and retries on mismatch.

### 5.4 System prompt (`prompt/system.ts`)

Static part written by us, then the contents of `GUIDELINES.md`. The static part states: you are a copyeditor for fiction; return only the structured output; `before` must be copied verbatim from the numbered paragraph, minimal but unique; never quote across paragraph boundaries; edits of more than about eight consecutive words are forbidden and must become comments; do not restate the chapter; if there is nothing to fix, return empty arrays. The guidelines file (see `resources/GUIDELINES.default.md`) carries the author-editable rules and style sheet. The same system prompt string for every chapter lets the SDK's automatic prompt caching apply.

### 5.5 The review dialog

"Review with AI" opens a modal rather than firing a request, because a review costs real money and minutes and the settings that shape it were sitting in the toolbar all day for a decision made a few times a day. The dialog asks, in one place:

- **Provider**, a select backed by `Settings.provider`: "Claude" or "OpenAI (API key)" (Claude's label was "Claude (subscription)" until `opensource.6`). Choosing OpenAI swaps the model list, adds an **API key** row (saved in Settings / from OPENAI_API_KEY / missing, with Open Settings), a line saying it is billed per token, and a note under Effort saying what reasoning effort is sent. Start review stays disabled until a key exists. Opening the dialog with OpenAI chosen and no key also raises a card with an Open Settings button; the Claude Setup screen only stands in front of the dialog when Claude is the provider.
- **Model** and **Effort**, persisted on change. The model select writes `model` or `openaiModel` depending on the provider.
- **Guidelines**, the effective path with **Open in Obsidian** and **Open** (`guidelines:open` with `via` `obsidian` or `default`; section 10). Under it, while the style sheet is still empty, an orange note: "Your style sheet is still empty: spelling variant, character names, invented words. Without it the reviewer may "fix" them." (`styleSheetNote` in `src/shared/ui/guidelines.ts`: `blank`, or a file the review has still to create from the template). The dialog reads `guidelines:status` as it opens, after either open and whenever the window gets the focus back, so the note goes once the author fills the style sheet in and returns (`setup.10b`).
- **Chapter stats**: paragraphs and words, from `chapterStats` in `src/shared/text/stats.ts`, so the size of the job is visible before it is paid for.
- **The previous review**, if there is one: when it was made, how many suggestions it holds, how many are still pending (both counted from the page when it is the review on screen, as History counts it: `historyCounts`, 6.4, `apply.5`), and a sentence saying that a new review replaces the working set while the old one stays in History.
- **Start review** and **Cancel**. Escape cancels.
- **No stranded decisions** (`apply.20`). A new review replaces the working set, and the model reads the chapter file, so changes on the page that the file does not have yet (`unsavedDecisionCount`) would stay behind in the old review, out of sight in History, and the dot in the chapter list, which follows the review the chapter opens with, would go. With such changes the dialog says so ("2 changes are not saved to the chapter yet. The review reads the chapter file, so save them first to have them included.", `reviewStartNote`) and its buttons are Cancel, "Review without them" and the blue "Save 2 changes, then review" (`reviewStartLabels`, singular "Save 1 change, then review" and "Review without it"); with none they are Cancel and "Start review", as before. The blue button runs Save to chapter (6.3) and starts the review only on `saved` or `nothing-to-save`, so the review on screen then stands on the bytes just written and the review is made from the saved text; a save that is cancelled, blocked or fails starts nothing and leaves the dialog open, and a dialog closed while the save runs starts nothing (`saveThenReview` in `src/renderer/state/reviewStart.ts`). The save's cards sit in the corner of the manuscript pane, under the dialog's backdrop, where they can be neither read nor closed, so the dialog says why itself: the warning gives way to a red box (`role="alert"`) that reads "The review has not started, because the changes were not saved." and then the text of every card the save put up, in order (`notSavedLines` in `src/shared/ui/save.ts`, handed over by `onNotSaved`); after Cancel to applying paragraph boxes, which the author answered, it adds nothing. The box goes when the blue button is pressed again, and the cards stay behind for when the dialog has closed (`apply.20` review). "Review without them" asks first, "Review without saving?" with Cancel focused (`reviewWithoutPrompt`): the review will not see them and may suggest some again, and they stay in the review from <date> (History's date), which the chapter stops opening with once the new review comes back, so it is reopened from History to save them. Both buttons are disabled while a save runs and when Start would be. The review reads the provider, model and effort when it starts, after the save, not when the button was pressed.

The toolbar keeps only the button, the progress label and Cancel while a review runs.

### 5.6 Claude's setup: the check, the Setup screen and the ways to it

How the app finds out whether Claude can review, says so, and tells the author what to do about it, without ever signing anyone in itself (`setup.2` to `setup.9`, `setup.11`; the tables are in 11.3 f, the shapes in section 3, and what Anthropic's pages say about each step in Appendix A, re-read for `setup.13`). The pure parts are `src/shared/ai/claudeStatus.ts`, `src/shared/ui/setup.ts` and `src/shared/ui/usage.ts`; the screen is `src/renderer/components/SetupScreen.tsx`.

1. **Binary.** The app runs the user's own Claude Code; nothing of Anthropic's ships (C.42). `src/main/providers/claudeBinary.ts` (`setup.3`) looks, in order, where Anthropic's installer puts it (`%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\claude.exe` on Windows, `~/.local/bin/claude` elsewhere), in each `PATH` folder, then in `/opt/homebrew/bin` and `/usr/local/bin` on macOS or `/usr/bin` and `/usr/local/bin` on Linux. On Windows only `claude.exe` counts, never npm's `claude.cmd` shim, which cannot run without a shell, so a copy installed only with npm reads as not installed (the npm package now carries the same native binary, but inside its package folder, which is not on PATH: setup page, re-read 2026-10-19). Development and packaged builds look the same way. Troubleshoot-install says an older Claude Desktop can put a `Claude.exe` in the `WindowsApps` folder (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WindowsApps`) ahead of Claude Code on PATH (on this machine the folder comes before WinGet's `Links`, where a WinGet copy is found), and Windows matches `claude.exe` to `Claude.exe` (Appendix A, 2026-10-19). The lookup never takes such an alias, on two counts (`setup.17`). `claudeCandidates` leaves out every PATH folder that ends in `Microsoft\WindowsApps` (`isWindowsAppsDir`), on Windows only. And the entries in that folder are App Execution Aliases, reparse points that `fs.stat` refuses with EACCES, so `canRun` says no to them anyway (measured by the `setup.13` review on 2026-10-19 on this machine's `winget.exe`, `python.exe` and the current Claude Desktop's `claude-desktop.exe` aliases, under Node 24.21.0 and under Electron 44.3.0's own Node 24.20.0; this machine has no older Desktop's `Claude.exe` to try). Leaving the folder out costs nothing, since `canRun` already passed over everything in it, and it means that a Node or Electron whose `fs.stat` followed an alias still could not find Claude Desktop there and run it with `--version` (`fs.access` already succeeds on an alias, measured 2026-10-19). `test/main/claudeBinary.test.ts` pins both: the folder left out of the candidates however PATH spells it, and, on Windows, `canRun` false for every alias in this machine's `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WindowsApps` (every `.exe` there that is not a folder, 50 here, each only stat'ed, never run; Node lists them as symbolic links, but the test does not ask for that, so a Node that listed them as plain files would still test them rather than skip; skipped where the folder holds no `.exe`) and no candidate in that folder on this machine's own PATH. A terminal does run the alias when it comes first on PATH, so there `claude` may start Claude Desktop, and the full-path form is the way round it, as Troubleshooting says (item 5). The same module is the only one in `src` that imports `child_process`, and its `runClaudeCli` runs nothing but `--version` and `auth status --json`, without a shell or a window.
2. **The check.** On every launch, on Check again, and as the window comes back to the front (item 6), `src/main/providers/claudeRuntime.ts` asks three things side by side and sends no prompt, so it uses none of the plan: `--version`, `auth status --json` and the `initialize` exchange (`setup.4`, C.46; until then a one-word prompt was sent). The one request Setup ever sends is Test the connection (item 5), and only when it is pressed. `classifyClaude` reads the answers into `ready` (with the credential: a Claude plan, an API key, a cloud provider or the bearer token in `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, which outranks an API key and the sign-in but not a cloud provider (C.71), and a warning for the free plan), `missing-binary`, `not-signed-in`, or `error`: a check that failed or ran out of time, or a Claude Code older than `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE`. Nothing is cached across launches. A review that fails says why through `classifyClaudeFailure` (`setup.5`, C.47), and the last failure that bears on setup is kept beside the check (`nextLastFailure`).
3. **Views.** `setupView` reads the two into one of nine: checking (no answer yet), not installed, signed out, sign-in ended, could not check, out of date, usage limit, ready with an API key, and ready, in the order 11.3 (f) gives. The status bar names it (`runtimeLabel`), and so does the Setup screen's status card (`setupStatus`).
4. **The ways to Setup** (`setup.7`).
   - **The launch card.** Once the settings and the first check are in, and Claude is the provider and neither Setup nor the welcome guide is open, a view with something to do (not installed, signed out, sign-in ended, could not check, out of date) puts up a card under the key `claude-runtime` that says so in a line (`runtimeCard`), with Open Setup. While it is up, later checks keep its words current; a check that leaves nothing to do withdraws it by its key, and so does opening Setup, which says the same at length, and the welcome guide showing, whose first step does (section 7, Notification cards; `setup.11`). Closed with its X, it stays closed: only the first check of the run may put it up. With OpenAI as the provider there is no card, and switching to OpenAI, in the review dialog's Provider list, withdraws one that is up; switching back does not bring it back. One pure rule decides all of this after every check, `runtimeCardAction` (raise, update, withdraw or nothing; `setup.7` review).
   - **The status bar.** The provider's readiness on the right of the status bar is a button: Setup for Claude, Settings for OpenAI, its tooltip saying which (`runtimeButtonTitle`).
   - **Settings.** The Claude account row has Setup, as before.
   - **The welcome guide.** Its first step, Connect Claude, has Set up Claude, which opens Setup over the guide; Setup's Close goes back to it (section 7, `setup.11`).
   - **Review with AI** with Claude as the provider opens Setup in place of the dialog only when Claude Code is missing or definitely signed out (`reviewGate`). A check that failed or ran out of time, a Claude Code too old for the app, or a sign-in or usage failure that the last review reported and the check no longer finds, opens the dialog with a warning in a line and Open Setup beside it (`reviewDialogWarning`), since the review may still go through and says why if it does not. Before the first check has answered, the dialog opens, its model list loading. The dialog can still be open when a check finds Claude Code missing or signed out: the first check answering, its Provider list switched to Claude, its Check again or the window coming back to the front. The warning then says what that check found, "Claude Code is not installed, so this review cannot run yet.", "Claude Code is not signed in, so this review cannot run yet." or, after a review said the sign-in had ended, "Your Claude sign-in has ended, so this review cannot run until you sign in again.", never what the last review said (`setup.7` review). Start review stays enabled: a review that then fails opens Setup, as below, and a check that is out of date by then does no harm.
   - **A review that fails** because Claude Code is missing, too old, not signed in or its sign-in has ended checks again and opens Setup (`setup.5`), its card showing over it.
   - **A review that stops at a usage limit** opens nothing: its card gives Claude Code's own words, which name the limit and when it resets, then what to do (`usageLimitCard` in `src/shared/ui/usage.ts`, `setup.9`). The advice is only for one of the plan's limits (`isPlanLimit`, `setup.9` review): the last usage notice says a plan limit was reached, or Claude Code's words name one ("You've hit your session limit", "weekly limit", "Opus limit", "Sonnet limit" or "Fable limit", or "You've hit your limit" alone); not when the notice says extra usage was paying or was itself the limit reached, nor when Claude Code's words already say to switch model. Any other usage limit, such as a team's shared budget, a spend limit, usage credits with no plan limit reached, or a seat without usage, keeps Claude Code's words alone, which say which limit and what to do about it. When a plan limit and the extra usage behind it run out together, Claude Code's words give the plan's reset ("You're out of usage credits · resets 3pm") and the notice names the plan's limit, so the advice is given. The advice comes from the notice, which says which limit was reached, on the plan the check found: for a limit that holds one model, the weekly Opus, Sonnet or model limit, the models it leaves free, "Sonnet and Haiku do not count against the weekly Opus limit: choose one under Model in Review with AI to go on now, or wait until the limit resets.", and for the weekly model limit, which Claude Code names after its model ("Fable limit"), "Other models do not count against this weekly model limit: choose another under Model in Review with AI to go on now, or wait until the limit resets."; for any other, "Wait until the limit resets, then try again. Sonnet and Haiku use your limits more slowly than Opus models (including Default), so choosing one under Model in Review with AI makes them last longer." (`usageLimitAdvice`). On a Pro or Enterprise plan Claude Code counts the weekly Sonnet limit as the account's weekly limit and says "You've hit your weekly limit", so there the app names it and advises on it as the weekly limit (C.64). The notice is read from `useAi`'s `latestRateLimit`, kept as each notice arrives, since the page may not have drawn the one that came just before the failure (C.64). The status bar then says "Claude: usage limit reached" and, in its usage notice, which limit and when it resets (section 7). Setup's usage-limit view says what the card said: `useAi` records the failure with the card's decision, from the same message and notice (`failureSetupEvent`, `LastFailure.planLimit`), so for one of the plan's limits Setup gives the same advice under its text, and for any other it says the limit is not one of the plan's, with no advice; the review dialog's warning follows it.
5. **The screen.** It takes the place of the chapter list, the prose and the side panel; the toolbar and the status bar stay, the notification cards sit at the top of its column, before it in the flow, so that they never cover its Close or Check again and stay in view as it scrolls (section 7), and the dialogs, Settings among them, and the close question open over it as over the main view, the page inert under them. The screen scrolls across the window's whole width under the cards, its page a centred column 720 CSS px wide at most, so the wheel scrolls it over the margins too and its scrollbar is at the window's edge (`.setup` and `.setup__page`; `setup.7` review: the column alone scrolled, and the cards came after it, at its foot). The suggestion editor is not drawn while Setup is open, but its words are kept for when Setup closes (7.4). From the top:
   - "Set up Claude", with Close. Escape does nothing here, as in the dialogs that have no Cancel of their own (section 7, Interactions).
   - **The status card**: the view in a few words, what the check found (the version, the account and plan when ready, the source of an API key or the `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` in use and how to use the plan instead (C.71), the check's own words when it failed, the free-plan warning; in the usage-limit view, what to do about the limit, from the last usage notice, as the review's card says it: `usageLimitAdvice`, item 4, `setup.9`; for a usage limit that is not one of the plan's, which the card gives no advice for, "The last review stopped at a usage limit that is not one of your Claude plan's own limits, such as usage credits, a spend limit or a team's shared budget. Claude Code's message said which. Nothing here needs setting up." in place of the plan's text, with no advice: `LastFailure.planLimit`, `setup.9` review), with its state's colour at its edge, Check again (blue while there is something to do, "Checking…" while a check runs, and held rather than disabled while it does, so that pressed from the keyboard it keeps the focus: `heldButton`, below), Test the connection (below) and, with a chapter open where the dialog would open anyway (`canTryReviewAnyway`), Try the review anyway, which closes Setup and opens the dialog.
   - **Test the connection** (`setup.8`) sends one very small request through the same Claude Code, flags, environment and folder as a review (Haiku, one turn, the system prompt "Reply with the single word OK.", no schema; `runCliTest`), so it goes through wherever a review would and fails wherever one would. It sits in the status card after Check again wherever Claude Code has been found and the check has not said for certain that it is signed out (`canTestConnection`: a ready check, the ended sign-in that only a test or a review clears, and a check that failed, where the test can say more); not before the first check, for a missing Claude Code or a definite "signed out", where the steps say what to do. Under the buttons, across the card, a note on what the request costs, in the terms the card itself uses for what Claude Code signs in with (`connectionTestNote`, `setup.8` review): on a Claude plan the task's "Sends one very small request to Claude, which counts against your plan like any other."; with an API key "Sends one very small request to Claude, which is billed to your API key like any other.", since the card says a few lines higher that reviews are billed to the key "not to a Claude plan"; through a cloud provider "Sends one very small request to Claude, which is billed through your cloud provider like any other."; with the token "Sends one very small request to Claude, which is billed to the account behind your ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN like any other." (C.71); and where the check failed and so did not say, "Sends one very small request to Claude, which counts against your plan or API key like any other." Then one line: while the test runs "Testing: waiting for Claude to answer…", with the button held but keeping its label, so the buttons keep their width and the card's text does not reflow. Held, not disabled (`heldButton` in `src/renderer/state/heldButton.ts`, for Check again too): the button is `aria-disabled`, drawn as a disabled one, and has no click handler, so a click, Space or Enter does nothing until the work ends, but it keeps the keyboard focus, which Chromium takes from a focused button that becomes `disabled` and leaves on the page, so a keyboard or screen-reader user had to Tab in again from the top of the window (`setup.8` review); then how it went, "Claude answered the test at 18:35:37, in 1.7 s (claude-haiku-5-5)." in `--accept` or "The test at 18:35:37 did not go through: <the reason, as a review would give it>" in `--danger` (`connectionTestLine`), kept for the rest of the run and shown again when Setup opens again. The card is the live region, so the line is read out. A test that went through clears the last failure, as a review that went through does, and one that did not is remembered as a review's failure is (`testSetupEvent`, `nextLastFailure`'s `success` and `failure`); one that fails for a reason Setup can fix (missing, too old, not signed in, sign-in ended) checks again, as such a review does. Its rate-limit notice goes to the status bar, as a review's does. The test stops after 60 s, from the app's side (`testConnection`), with "Claude did not answer within 60 seconds, so the test was stopped. Try again in a minute."; quitting ends it as it ends a review. The signed-out and sign-in-ended views with a ready check say "test the connection or try the review: this clears once either goes through" (`SIGNED_IN_AGAIN`).
   - **The out-of-date note** of the review dialog (`setup.6`): a Claude Code older than `RECOMMENDED_CLAUDE_CODE` may not offer the newest models, with `claude update` and, on this machine's own tab, its full-path form. It shows here in every view but out of date, whose steps are the update, and not installed; the review dialog keeps it, from the same `UpdateNote`, below its model list.
   - **Steps** for the view (`setupSteps`), under a heading, on the system chosen in three tabs, Windows, macOS and Linux, the user agent's preselected: open a terminal (`openTerminalHint`), run one command, come back. Not installed: the one line that installs Claude Code and signs in through the path it installs to (`installAndLoginCommand`), since that is the first run for most people; through the full path, because Anthropic's installer does not add its folder to PATH itself, only says how in its output when the folder is missing (troubleshoot-install, re-read 2026-10-19, Appendix A), and a terminal opened before the install would not see it anyway. Signed out and sign-in ended: `claude auth login`. Out of date: `claude update`. Each command has a Copy, and the sign-in and update commands have their full-path form under them whenever the path is known, for a terminal that does not find `claude` (the terminal's PATH need not be the app's), on the tab of the system this machine runs only (`pathForTab`, the user agent's system): the path is this machine's, and on another system's tab it would be quoted in that system's syntax, such as a Windows path quoted for a Mac terminal, which runs nowhere (`setup.7` review). Not installed also lists other ways to install that put `claude.exe` or `claude` where the app looks: WinGet on Windows and Homebrew on macOS, which do not sign in, so `claude auth login` follows them. npm is not offered: on Windows its `claude.cmd` reads as not installed (item 1), and Troubleshooting says so.
   - **Copy** puts the command on the clipboard and says "Copied"; a copy the clipboard refuses says "Copy failed" and, under the command, "Could not copy. Select the command and copy it yourself." A click on a command selects all of it, and a long one wraps rather than scrolling.
   - **Troubleshooting** for the view and the tab (`setupTroubleshooting(view, runtime, os, host)`), each question opening on its answer: the terminal does not recognise `claude`; on the Windows tab, the terminal starts Claude Desktop instead of Claude Code; no browser window opened; signed in, yet the app says not signed in (its Claude Code runs without the user's Claude Code settings, so a sign-in kept there does not reach it, 11.3 c); which plans work, and the API key in their place; installed with npm (Windows); `claude update` refusing a copy a package manager installed; a check that keeps failing. The two terminal answers follow the steps (`setup.17`). Where the steps show a full-path form (signed out, sign-in ended and out of date, on this machine's tab once the path is known, `pathForTab`), they lead with it: "Use the full-path form of the command, shown under it: it names the Claude Code that <APP_NAME> found, so the terminal does not have to find claude by name.", then that a new terminal helps only once Claude Code's folder is on PATH, which Anthropic's installer does not arrange. Where the steps show none, the answers never point at one. On another system's tab they name the folder the installer uses there (`%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin`, `~/.local/bin`) and the view's command run from it, such as `~/.local/bin/claude auth login` (`installerPathCommand`, from which the install line is built too), and say that a WinGet or Homebrew copy is found by name in a terminal opened after the install. In the not-installed view, whose install line signs in through the full path already, they say that line needs no PATH, and that a terminal opened before a WinGet or Homebrew install may not see where it put Claude Code, so open a new one for `claude auth login` (on Linux, which offers no other install, how to put `~/.local/bin` on PATH). The Claude Desktop answer says an older Desktop can put a `Claude.exe` in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WindowsApps`, which a terminal runs for `claude` when that folder comes first on PATH (item 1), then the way round it: the full-path form shown under the command, the installer's path on another system's tab, and, not installed, the install line, even after a WinGet install. `test/shared/setup.test.ts` walks every view, tab and host: an answer says "shown under it" exactly where the steps show a full-path form. Against Anthropic's pages as re-read on 2026-10-19 (Appendix A): the browser answer matches what `claude auth login` does, printing the link and reading a pasted code; the plans answer matches the setup page (Pro, Max, Team, Enterprise or Console; not the free plan); WinGet and Homebrew copies do not update themselves, as the update answer assumes. Until `setup.17` the first answer told everyone to close the terminal and open a new one, which helps only once the installer's folder is on PATH, and then to use a full-path form that the not-installed view and another system's tab do not show (the `setup.7` review saw the second gap).
   - **Advanced**, folded: the Claude Code found and its version, the versions needed, what it signs in with, when it was last checked, and where the app looks (`advancedRows`).
   - **The privacy footer**: "<APP_NAME> never sees your Claude password or sign-in token. You sign in with Anthropic, in Claude Code and your browser.", with a link to Anthropic's setup guide, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup.
6. **Checking again as the window comes back.** The author runs the command in a terminal and comes back. While the view has something to do and Claude is in use (the provider, or Setup open), the window coming back to the front checks again, at most every 3 s from the start of the last check, whatever started it, and never over a check that is running (`focusRecheckDue`, `recheckOnFocus` in `useAi`). A ready view, a usage limit, or OpenAI with Setup closed, starts nothing.
7. **No sign-in of the app's own.** The app never offers a sign-in form or a button that signs in, never starts a sign-in (`runClaudeCli` refuses anything but its two probes), never reads or writes Claude Code's credentials, and never runs a check with the vault as `cwd` (CLAUDE.md rules 2 and 6). `test/renderer/setupScreen.render.test.ts` renders every view and finds no button labelled like a sign-in.
8. **Policy note for the README:** this app runs the unmodified Claude Code each user installs and signs in to themselves, with their own subscription or API key (C.42). Every user signs in with their own account through Anthropic's flow, in their own terminal; the app never intermediates credentials and never starts a sign-in (item 7, Appendix C "No in-app sign-in launcher"). The README's Connecting Claude and First run follow this section and section 7's welcome guide (written by `setup.13`, handed to `opensource.11`).

## 6. Files, watcher, review store (`src/main`)

### 6.1 Files

- List `*.md` and `*.txt` recursively under the manuscript folder, skipping dot-folders and the reviews folder if nested. Both extensions are read, saved and reviewed the same way; the empty state says "No .md or .txt files in that folder".
- Read: bytes -> refuse anything over 4 MiB or not UTF-8 (`decodeText`, `files.3`, 11.3 l) -> detect BOM/EOL/trailing newline -> LF text -> sha256 of bytes. The refusal (`not-utf8`) says what the file most likely is (UTF-16, an older encoding, a few damaged characters, binary) and how to fix it, and ends "The app has not changed the file." Save re-reads through the same path, so a file swapped to another encoding after it was opened is refused rather than overwritten.
- Write, for Save to chapter only (`writeChapter`, 6.3, 11.3 h): compose -> restore EOL/BOM -> stop with nothing written when the bytes equal the file's -> copy the bytes being replaced into the backups, or stop with nothing written (6.5) -> write `<file>.<random>.tmp` in the same folder -> `rename` over the original. Remember the hash of what was written so the watcher can ignore the echo. A rename refused with EPERM, EACCES or EBUSY (a scanner, OneDrive or Obsidian holding the file for a moment) is retried after 50, 100, 200, 400 and 800 ms; before each retry the target is hashed again, and if someone else changed it the save stops as `stale` and their bytes stay (`files.4`). When the retries run out, `explainWriteError` says in plain words what Windows refused, including the Controlled folder access way out, and that nothing was written.
- **A read that fails is explained as a read (`apply.24`).** `toAppError` in `src/main/errors.ts` takes the operation, `read` or `write`, and these read paths in main throw `readFailure(err, name, options)`, whose words reach the page unchanged: opening or reloading a chapter (`readChapter`), the reads Save to chapter makes before it writes (steps 1 and 5 of 11.3 h, and the read after a `stale`), a review opened from History (`reviews:load`), the guidelines file (before a review, and its status), the drafts file (11.3 j) and Show backups (6.5). So does the review a chapter opens with (`reviews:load-latest`, `apply.25`): a review file in the chapter's folder that is there but cannot be read, or the folder itself, stops the open with a card that names the chapter before main's words ("Could not open Chapter 12.md: Windows would not let Lapidary read the review file ..."), and the page stays on what was open (6.4). `explainReadError` says what may be holding the file, what to try, and ends "Lapidary has not changed anything." On Windows EBUSY, EPERM and EACCES give "Windows would not let Lapidary read Chapter 12.md. Another program may have it open, such as a sync, backup or antivirus tool, or your Windows account may not be allowed to read it. Close it there, or wait a moment, and try again."; elsewhere "Lapidary is not allowed to read Chapter 12.md. Check that the file and its folder can be read, and that no other program is holding it, then try again." ENOENT and ENOTDIR give "Lapidary could not find Chapter 12.md. It may have been renamed, moved or deleted; if it was renamed or moved, open it again from where it is now." for a chapter (`readChapter` and Save's reads pass `reopen`), and stop at "It may have been renamed, moved or deleted." for any other file, which cannot be opened again from somewhere else ("Lapidary could not find the review file 2026-10-17T12-00-00-000Z.review.json. It may have been renamed, moved or deleted. Lapidary has not changed anything."); EISDIR "... because it is a folder, not a file.", and any other code "Lapidary could not read Chapter 12.md (EIO). If it is on a network, USB or cloud drive, check that the drive is connected, then try again." Controlled folder access blocks only changes, so a read never mentions it. A file is named by its own name, a review file as "the review file <name>", the drafts file as "the file they are kept in" (its own name is a hash, and the card puts the words after "could not be read back:") and a chapter's backups as "the backup folder of <chapter>"; the name never starts a sentence, so a file name is shown exactly as it is. An error the IPC bridge receives unexplained is worded as a write that replaces a file, as before, and a refused rename keeps those words. The chapter list's read is explained too (`apply.25`): `listChapters` throws `readFailure` naming "the manuscript folder <name>" when the manuscript folder itself cannot be read or is not there, and the list shows those words where "No .md or .txt files in that folder." would be, with Try again (section 7); a subfolder it cannot read is still skipped. Held by another program on Windows: "Windows would not let Lapidary read the manuscript folder Novel. Another program may have it open, such as a sync, backup or antivirus tool, or your Windows account may not be allowed to read it. Close it there, or wait a moment, and try again. Lapidary has not changed anything."
- **A write that makes something new says what it was making (`apply.25`).** Only a refused rename is retried (files.4), so only its words say "even after trying for a second and a half". `toAppError` takes the kind of write as well (`write`: `replace`, the default, `create`, `copy` or `folder`, the type `WriteKind` in `src/main/errors.ts`), and `writeFailure(err, kind, name)` turns a refusal into an `AppFailure` whose words reach the page unchanged, as `readFailure` does for a read; an error it has no words for (`EEXIST`, or `ENOENT` for a new file or a copy) is left as it is, so a caller can still test its code. `create` is a new chapter (`createChapter`'s `wx` write) and set-aside text (`setAsideDrafts`), `copy` the guidelines file made from its template (`ensureGuidelinesFile`), and `folder` the folder made before each of them, the drafts folder (`saveDrafts`), a chapter's review folder (`saveReview`, adoption) and the reviews folder itself (`makeReviewsFolder`, which every review operation runs first). Their words say what was being made, claim no retries, keep the Controlled folder access way out, since it blocks creating files too, and say "try again", never "save again", since none of them is Save to chapter: "Windows would not let Lapidary create Chapter 13.md. Another program may be holding its folder, or your Windows account may not be allowed to add files to it. Wait a moment and try again. If that keeps happening, Windows Security's Controlled folder access may be blocking Lapidary: allow it under Windows Security > Virus & threat protection > Ransomware protection > Allow an app through Controlled folder access. Nothing was written."; "... create GUIDELINES.md from the template. ..."; "... create the review folder of Chapter 12.md. Another program may be holding the folder it goes in, or your Windows account may not be allowed to add folders there. ..."; elsewhere "Lapidary is not allowed to create Chapter 13.md. Check that its folder is writable, then try again. Nothing was written."; a full disk "There is no space left on the disk for Chapter 13.md. Free some space and try again." (part of a new file may be left, so it does not say nothing was written) and a read-only drive "Lapidary cannot create Chapter 13.md, because the drive is read-only. Nothing was written.". A folder is named by what it is for: "the manuscript folder Novel", "the folder for GUIDELINES.md", "the folder they are kept in" (the drafts, after "could not be kept:"), "the folder for set-aside text", "the review folder of Chapter 12.md", "the reviews folder <path>". A folder is made with everything on its path, so `ENOENT` means its drive, or the share it is on, is not there, and `ENOTDIR` that a file is in its way, and a folder has words for both (`apply.25` review): the reviews folder can be on a USB, network or cloud drive, and while that drive is not connected no chapter opens (6.4), where Node 24 on Windows reports the missing drive as `ENOENT` with the path `\\?` and the card had shown only `ENOENT: no such file or directory, mkdir '\\?'`. Now "Lapidary could not create the reviews folder Q:\Reviews, because the drive or the folder it goes in is not there. If it is on a network, USB or cloud drive, check that the drive is connected, or choose another reviews folder in Settings, then try again. Nothing was written." and "... because a file has the name of one of the folders it goes in. Move or rename that file, or choose another reviews folder in Settings, then try again. Nothing was written."; only the reviews folder, which the author chooses in Settings, offers Settings (`makeReviewsFolder` passes `setting: 'reviews folder'`, `ErrorContext.setting`), and every other folder stops at "then try again". A new file and a copy keep `ENOENT` and `ENOTDIR` as they are. The temp file `atomicWriteFile` writes first is part of replacing a file, so it keeps `replace`, but a refusal to create it, which Controlled folder access gives in a protected folder before any rename, is never retried, and `explainWriteError` tells it from a refused rename by the error itself (a temp file's path and no `dest`): "Windows would not let Lapidary write the new version of Chapter 12.md into its folder. Another program may be holding the folder, or your Windows account may not be allowed to add files to it. Wait a moment and save again. If that keeps happening, ... Nothing was written; the file is as it was." Show in folder's own folders in the app's data folder keep the default words.
- **Main decides what Save may write (`files.2`).** `files:save` first runs `assertSavableChapter`: the file must be `.md` or `.txt`, must not be the guidelines file, and must lie inside the manuscript folder or have been read or created from inside it earlier in the run (`OpenedChapters`, so a chapter open while Settings changes folder still saves). Anything else is refused with `invalid-input` before a byte is read or written.
