- **Create (`files:create`).** The New chapter dialog (7.5) creates a file in the manuscript folder through `createChapter`, which writes with the exclusive-create flag (`wx`): it can fail, but it can never overwrite. `checkChapterName` is the gate in front of it and refuses a path separator, a traversal, a reserved Windows device name, an illegal character and anything resolving outside the manuscript folder; `files:check-name` runs the same function plus an existence check, so the dialog can say "A file with this name already exists" while the author is still typing and keep Create disabled. A new file is written LF, no BOM, with a trailing newline; after that Save preserves whatever shape the file has.
- **The review folder name includes the extension** for anything but `.md` (`chapter-01` versus `chapter-01-txt`), so two files that differ only by extension cannot show each other's reviews. `.md` keeps the bare slug, which is what every review written before `.txt` support already uses.

### 6.2 Watcher

chokidar on the manuscript folder with `awaitWriteFinish: { stabilityThreshold: 300, pollInterval: 100 }`. On change to the open chapter: hash the file; if it equals the last written or last loaded hash, ignore; otherwise send `file:changed` to the renderer, which triggers rebase (4.5) and shows a card, one that each reload replaces (section 7, Notification cards): "Reloaded from disk. N suggestions re-anchored, M could not be found.", followed by "K decisions changed to match the file." when the rebase changed decisions to follow the file (C.5; `rebaseCardText` in `src/shared/ui/rebase.ts`, which words all four rebase cards: this one, a chapter opened on a review made before the file changed, an older review opened from History, and Save to chapter finding the file changed, `apply.23`). Obsidian saves on a debounce while typing, so this can fire repeatedly; that is fine because rebase is idempotent.

`createWatcher` (`src/main/watcher.ts`, `files.5`) takes chokidar, `broadcast`, the file reader and the log as dependencies, so it is tested with a stand-in and fake timers; `npm run test:slow` runs the real-chokidar timing tests in `test/slow/`, which `npm test` leaves out. Only .md and .txt files count: a change is reported unless its bytes are ones the app wrote or loaded (compared by `pathKey`), a chapter or folder appearing or disappearing refreshes the file list once per 300 ms burst, an added chapter is also hashed so an editor that saves by replacing the file still reloads the open chapter, and watcher errors are logged.

### 6.3 Save flow

`saveChapter` in `src/renderer/state/saveChapter.ts` (`apply.3`) is the one path from the page to the chapter file. The toolbar's Save to chapter, Ctrl+S, the review dialog's "Save N changes, then review" and Submit manual review's "Save N changes, then build" (`apply.20`, 5.5, 7.5), the leave prompt's Save first (`apply.7`, 7.7) and the close prompt's Save and close (`apply.9`, 7.7) all call it and can wait for how it ended: `saved` or `nothing-to-save` (the file now reads like the page), `cancelled` (Cancel to applying paragraph boxes), `blocked` (no chapter, a review of it running, or a box that cannot be applied) or `failed` (a card says why; a dialog that saved first, Review with AI or Submit manual review, also shows the card's words inside itself, 5.5 and 7.5). It reaches main through `SaveDeps` that App passes in (`files:save`, the review writer, a refresh of History and the list), so it is tested in node. Saves run one at a time, and `pendingSave()` hands a caller that must let one end first the save under way.

0. Nothing unsaved (`unsavedDecisionCount` is 0 and no paragraph box has changed text): a card says "The chapter already matches the page." and the file is not read or written. Paragraph boxes with changed text are applied first as accepted suggestions, after a question unless the caller already asked (`applyDrafts: 'apply'`, for a prompt whose Save reads "Apply and save first"); if one cannot be applied, nothing is written. A box still as it opened needs no question and is closed when something is written, since the save can renumber the paragraphs under it.
1. Re-read the file and hash it.
2. If the hash differs from the review's `base.hash`, rebase first and tell the user what changed, then try once more.
3. Compose. When the bytes to write are the bytes already on disk, nothing is written (`SaveResponse.unchanged`, 11.3 h step 3), so the file keeps its modified time and Obsidian and sync tools see no change; the card then says the chapter already matches the page. Otherwise back up the bytes being replaced (6.5; no backup, no save), check once more that the file has not changed, and write atomically. Then update `base` to the written text (accepted hunks now have `baseSide: 'replacement'`, `savedReview`) and save the review. Comments and hunk paragraph labels follow their paragraphs into the new base (`remapAfterCommit`, C.44), so a save that splits, joins or deletes a paragraph does not shift them. While the file is being written the store holds back decisions (`chapterSaving`): the new base folds in exactly the decisions that were written, so a click in that moment does nothing rather than being recorded as saved without being in the file. Opening a chapter or a History entry waits for a save under way to end (`pendingSave()`), and of several clicks made meanwhile only the last goes ahead. If the review on screen is replaced during the write all the same, by a load that began before the save: a copy of the same review on the same base whose decisions compose the same text is folded like the review itself (`isWrittenReview`); another chapter or review is left alone, the save is recorded off screen in the review it belongs to, and History is refreshed only for the chapter on screen; a copy of the same review that reads differently, such as one rebased onto other bytes, is left alone too, and the card says the review was reloaded while saving and to save again.
4. Obsidian notices external changes and reloads the file when it is not being edited there. Document that the same chapter should not be edited in Obsidian while accepting changes; the app cannot prevent it, only detect it.

**Ctrl+S**, or Cmd+S, runs the same save wherever the focus is (`saveShortcutAction` in `src/shared/ui/save.ts`, handled first in `state/keyboard.ts`, before the early return for modified keys). It does nothing behind a dialog, a question, Settings, the Setup screen or the welcome guide, on a held key's repeats, while a review of the chapter runs or a save is under way, or when there is nothing to save; from inside a paragraph box a card says to press Done first, and while the suggestion editor is open a card says to press that editor's Save first. Ctrl+Shift+S and Ctrl+Alt+S pass through.

### 6.4 Review store

`<reviewsFolder>/<chapter-slug>/<ISO-timestamp>.review.json`. The folder is named after the chapter's file name only, so same-named chapters in different folders share it; a review belongs to the chapter its `sourcePath` names, compared as the file system compares paths (`samePath`), never to whatever its folder suggests (files.1, C.45). The current review, the one the chapter opens with, is the one last on screen for it (`apply.26`, C.57), which is almost always the newest. Main records it in the chapter folder's `.last-open.json`, `{version: 1, chapters: [{sourcePath, file}]}`, one record per chapter path, since same-named chapters in different folders share the folder: when the page shows a review from History or the review selector (`reviews:shown`, sent once it is on screen, `recordShown`), and when `saveReview` starts a new entry, which is a new review arriving, whether the author is on its chapter or not, or the review on screen being written for the first time. A record is written only when it changes, never touches the review file or its times, and is honoured only for a `.review.json` directly in the chapter's folder that belongs to the chapter; a record that cannot be written is logged (`reviews.last-open-failed`) and the save it came with stands. While nothing is recorded for a chapter, or the file recorded is gone, it opens with the belonging review last decided in (`decidedAt ?? createdAt`): a decision is made only on screen, and that is the review every chapter opened with before `apply.26`, so upgrading switches no chapter to another review. When reviews follow a renamed or moved chapter, the record goes with them (`carryLastOpen`, run with the journal). `currentOf` in `src/main/reviews.ts` decides it for `reviews:load-latest` and for the chapter list's marks (`reviews:index`) alike. **A review file that cannot be read is not skipped like one that is not a review (`apply.25`).** `readEntry` tells a file that is there and that the system will not let the app read (another program holds it with no sharing, or the account may not read it) from a file that is gone, a folder, or json that is not a review, which are still skipped. Whose such a file is, and when it was decided in, cannot be told without reading it, so unless the record names a review that could be read, `reviews:load-latest` throws `readFailure` naming it ("Windows would not let Lapidary read the review file 2026-10-17T12-00-00-000Z.review.json. ..."), and it does the same for a chapter folder it cannot list ("the review folder of Chapter 12.md") and for the review chosen when that file was read before, its entry cached, and is held since; nothing is adopted in its place. `openChapter` shows any failure of `reviews:load-latest`, a reviews folder that cannot be made included (6.1), as a card and stays on what was open: no older review and no blank one comes on screen, so no decision can make a new review current over the one that could not be read. Main's words name only a review file or a folder, so the card names the chapter first (`chapterNotOpenedText` in `src/shared/ui/open.ts`, `apply.25` review): "Could not open Chapter 12.md: Windows would not let Lapidary read the review file 2026-10-17T12-00-00-000Z.review.json. ..."; a chapter file that cannot be read is shown in main's words alone, which name it. **A review behind the page does not open either (`apply.25` review).** A decision whose write failed is kept by the writer, not in the file ("Two save points" below), so a review read back from disk would come on screen without it, and the writer's next try would then put it back over what the page showed. Opening a chapter, or a review from History or the review selector, therefore first queues every failed review write again (`retryReviewWrites`) and then flushes, so a review whose file another program has let go gets its decisions before anything is read; if the review about to be shown still has a failed write (`reviewWriteFailureOf`, by the key of chapter path and review id), the open stops before anything on screen changes, with "Could not open Chapter 12.md, because your latest decisions in its review are not in the review file yet, and it would open without them. The review could not be saved: ..." or, from History or the selector, "Could not open the review from <date as History shows it>, because your latest decisions in it are not in its file yet, ..." (`unwrittenReviewText`). Another chapter, or another review of the chapter, still opens while one review cannot be written. A review file read before and unchanged since counts by its cached entry, held or not. History and the list's marks still leave such a file out. `saveReview` neither writes over such a file nor writes the review on screen into a new entry instead, which would become current: the write fails with the read words, and the writer tries again later ("Two save points" below). History lists every belonging review, the current one too, newest first by `createdAt`, and highlights the one on screen (`historyCounts`, `apply.5`); the review selector at the top of the side panel lists the same reviews (section 7). `raw` is never modified. A small `index.json` per chapter folder is not needed; list the directory, with summaries cached by modification time and size.

**Reviews follow their chapter.** When nothing belongs to a chapter that opens, the reviews of a chapter path that no longer exists are taken over if they plainly are this chapter's: the vault moved (the last two path segments match) or the chapter was renamed or moved in Obsidian (a review of exactly its current text, at least 500 characters). The whole history moves to the new folder with the new `sourcePath`, through a journal so a crash cannot split it, and a card names the old path (11.3 g). `saveReview` writes only a `.review.json` directly in the folder the review belongs in and holding no other chapter's review; any other path it is handed gets a new entry in the right folder instead.

**The reviews folder always resolves.** When Settings names no folder, reviews go to `<userData>/reviews`, created on demand (`resolveReviewsFolder` in `src/main/reviews.ts`). No review operation ever fails with "choose a reviews folder first": a generated review costs real money and minutes, and losing one because Settings was incomplete is the worst outcome the app can produce. Settings shows the folder actually in use, marks it as the default when it is, and offers Change and Show in folder.

**Two save points.** A review is written immediately after Claude answers, and again, debounced by 700 ms, after every decision. Both write through `reviews:save`; passing the known path overwrites that history entry rather than starting a new one. Each review has its own queue (`createKeyedWriter` in `src/renderer/state/persist.ts`, used by `reviewPersistence.ts`, `apply.1`): moving to another chapter or another History entry never cancels a write still waiting, one write per review is in flight at a time and the last one written is the newest, and opening a chapter, or a review from History or the selector, first queues failed writes again and then flushes what is waiting, so it never reads a review file that is behind the screen; a review whose write still fails does not open (above, `apply.25` review). The status bar shows "Decisions kept HH:MM" (local time, 24-hour clock) after each write of the review on screen, and only for that review: the time is stored with the file it was written to and shown while that file is the one open (`reviewKeptAt`), so a chapter whose review has not been written yet, or another review, shows no time. The tooltip is "Your decisions are kept automatically in the review. The chapter file changes only when you press Save to chapter." (`keptPhrase`, `KEPT_TOOLTIP`, `apply.4`; it said "Review saved", which read as the chapter). A failure raises a notification card once per distinct message rather than once per keystroke, and the review that failed is kept until a later write of it succeeds, so closing the window asks first and tries it again (`apply.9`, 7.7), as every open does (`apply.25` review). The card stays after a later write works, until its X is clicked.

**Writing the chapter is separate.** The toolbar button is called **Save to chapter** because it runs the only action in the app that changes the manuscript file, which Ctrl+S and the Save in the review dialogs and the leave and close prompts run too: it composes the page's decisions, copies the version it replaces into the backups (6.5) and writes atomically (6.3). Review json persistence needs no button, and nothing writes the chapter by itself. The button counts what the chapter file does not have yet (`unsavedDecisionCount`): "Save 3 changes to chapter", "Save 1 change to chapter", plain "Save to chapter" when the file already reads like the page; a dot in `--unsaved` before the chapter name and "3 not saved to chapter" in the status bar say the same (`saveButtonState`, `unsavedPhrase`, `apply.4`). The chapter list puts the same dot, with the same tooltip, before every chapter whose current review holds decisions its file does not (`ReviewSummary.unsavedCount`), and before the open chapter while the review on screen does, so the dot outlasts leaving the chapter and a restart (`chapterListMarks` in `src/shared/ui/chapterList.ts`, `apply.5`). It goes by the review the chapter opens with, the one last on screen (`apply.26`), and so do Save, the status bar and the leave and close questions: changes left in another review of the chapter, which the author moved away from without a question, are not marked by the dot but counted on that review's entry in the review selector ("2 not saved to chapter", as last written; section 7). The list's marks are read (`reviews:index`) whenever the file list is (at start, after Save to chapter or a new review, when chapters appear or go) and also each time a chapter opens, after the reviews waiting to be written are on disk and before the new chapter shows, so the chapter just left is marked from its review as written; only the newest answer is kept. History counts the same way (`historyCounts` in `src/shared/ui/history.ts`, `apply.5`): the row of the review on screen, the one it highlights, takes its pending badge from the page, as the status bar does, and every other row its count as last written. History is read when a chapter opens, after Save to chapter and after a new review, and also when a History entry opens, after the reviews waiting to be written are on disk, so the row of the review just left keeps the count it showed on screen. There is no other unsaved flag: `store.dirty`, which also counted a decision made and turned back, is gone.

### 6.5 Backups

Before Save to chapter replaces a chapter file, the bytes it replaces are copied into the app's data folder (`apply.10`, 11.3 h, Appendix D question 10). The author may not use git, and the data folder is never synced or indexed. `writeChapter` in `src/main/files.ts` runs the copy as its `beforeReplace` hook, which main fills from `chapterBackupHooks` in `src/main/backups.ts`, after the file has been read and found to be the one the review is based on, and only when the save changes bytes: a save that would write what is already there writes and backs up nothing (6.3 step 3). **No backup, no save:** if the copy cannot be made, nothing is written and the card says "Could not back up the chapter before saving, so nothing was written:" and why, in plain words (no space left, a folder that cannot be written, and so on). After the copy the file is read once more, and if it changed meanwhile (Obsidian or a sync wrote it during the copy) the save stops as `stale` and rebases, as in 6.3, so bytes that are not in a backup are never replaced.

- **Where.** `<userData>/backups/<chapter slug>-<first 8 hex of sha256(pathKey)>/`, one folder per chapter file: the slug is the one the reviews folder uses (6.4), and the hash of the chapter's path key keeps two chapters with the same file name in different folders apart. A `chapter.json` holding `{sourcePath}` is written the first time and never replaced, so a person restoring by hand can tell which file a folder belongs to.
- **Never in the vault.** The backups root must lie outside the manuscript folder and outside the folder of the chapter being saved (`assertOutside`). That fails only when the manuscript folder contains the app's data folder, or a development userData override points into the vault, and the save is then refused with that reason, so no backup can be listed as a chapter, indexed by Obsidian or synced.
- **Names.** The local time of the save, then the chapter's file name: `2026-10-14 16.04.33 Chapter 12.md`, which opens in any editor as the chapter it copies and sorts by time. A second backup in the same second gets `-2` after the time, then `-3`. Each file is created exclusively, so no backup is ever overwritten, and holds the old bytes exactly: BOM, line endings and all. A copy that fails part way is removed. The names are made and read by `formatLocalStamp`, `backupFileName` and `parseBackupFileName` in `src/shared/backups.ts`.
- **Duplicates.** When the newest backup already holds exactly the bytes being replaced, as when the author put the old text back in Obsidian and saved again, no copy is added and that one stands for the save.
- **What is kept.** After every write, `afterWrite` prunes that chapter's folder: the newest 20 backups stay, and so does the oldest backup of each local calendar day from today back through the 29 days before; the rest are deleted (`backupsToPrune`). A name that is not a backup's, `chapter.json` and anything the author put there included, is never deleted. Pruning is best effort: the chapter is already written by then, so a failure is logged (`backups.prune-failed`) and the save stands.
- **The answer.** `SaveResponse.backup` gives the backup's `name` and whether this save made it (`created`), for the save card and Settings to point at (`apply.11`, which also records restoring a backup by hand). The log gets a line when a backup fails (`backups.failed`) or a prune does, and never a backup's contents (11.3 m).
- **Finding them** (`apply.11`). While a chapter is open, a footer under the side panel's two regions holds **Show backups**, which opens that chapter's backup folder in File Explorer with its newest backup selected. The "Saved to the chapter file." card carries the same button whenever the answer names a backup, for the chapter it saved, whose newest backup is the one that save made or the one that stood for it (a later save of the same chapter replaces the card). Both send `shell:reveal` `backups` with the chapter's `sourcePath`, never a folder: main works the folder out itself (`chapterBackups` in `src/main/backups.ts`, through `backupFolderFor`), refuses a path that is not absolute and any folder that is not directly under the backups root (`assertChapterBackupFolder`), and answers `not-found` while there is no folder for the chapter's path. That is so until a Save to chapter first changes the file at that path, and also after the chapter was renamed or moved in Obsidian, or the whole vault was moved: the folder is named by the path, so the chapter gets a new one, and unlike its reviews (6.4, "Reviews follow their chapter") its backups do not follow it. Those made before stay in the old path's folder, whose `chapter.json` names the old path; after a vault move its name differs from the new one only in the hash. The page shows `not-found` as an information card, "No backups of Chapter 03 at this path yet. Save to chapter makes one each time it changes the file. If the chapter or its folder was renamed or moved, its earlier backups are in another folder, named after its old file name." (`noBackupsYet`), with **Show all backups** (`reveal-all-backups`), which selects the backups root as Settings does; it opens no other window. The card's key is `SHOW_BACKUPS_CARD`. A Show backups that works closes it, and so does a Save to chapter whose answer names a backup, made or standing for that save (the "reloaded while saving" card included, and whichever chapter the card named), so the card never stays above "Saved to the chapter file." saying there are none; a save that writes nothing or is refused leaves it. A folder whose backups were deleted by hand opens with nothing selected. Settings has a **Backups** row: the backups root (`EffectivePaths.backupsFolder`), Show in folder, which selects it in the app's data folder as the other rows do, and the note "Before every Save to chapter, the version it replaces is copied here. The last 20 saves and the first save of each day for 30 days are kept. Backups stay on this computer." The words are in `src/shared/ui/backups.ts`.
- **Restoring by hand.** The app has no restore command. A backup is the chapter file as it was, byte for byte, so it opens in Notepad or any text editor; Obsidian opens only files inside a vault, so it cannot open a backup where it is. Do not drag a backup into Obsidian: that copies it into the vault, where a backup must never be (see "Never in the vault"), listed as a chapter, indexed and synced. Each chapter's backup folder holds a `chapter.json` naming the file its backups were made from, which tells apart two chapters of the same name, and a renamed or moved chapter's folder from its new one. To take back a passage, open the backup and copy the words into the chapter in Obsidian. To take back the whole chapter, first copy the chapter as it is now somewhere outside the vault, because the version on disk goes into a backup only when the next Save to chapter replaces it; then paste the backup's whole text over the chapter's in Obsidian, or replace the chapter file with a copy of the backup given the chapter's file name. A copy left in the manuscript folder under a backup's name is listed as a chapter and indexed by Obsidian. The app sees the changed file and moves the open review onto it (6.2), as for any change made in Obsidian, so suggestions the restored text no longer has go to Unanchored; the next Save to chapter backs up the restored text like any other.

## 7. UI specification (`src/renderer`)

Layout: left column file list, each chapter with the unsaved dot before its name while its file lacks some of its changes and a badge after it (a tick for a review with nothing pending, else the pending count), and a header carrying a folder button and a "+" for a new chapter (the open chapter's dot and badge come from the review on screen, the others' from their current review as last written: `chapterListMarks`, `apply.5`); with no rows the list says why (`chapterListNotice` in `src/shared/ui/chapterList.ts`, `apply.25`): "No manuscript folder chosen yet." with Choose folder, "No .md or .txt files in that folder.", or, when the manuscript folder could not be read, main's words for it (6.1) in the text colour with Try again, which reads the folder again; center review pane with a comment gutter; right column with, at its very top, the review selector, then the focused suggestion in a box of fixed height (below, `ui.5`), the suggestions list, the comments under one heading (open, then unanchored, then resolved; One comments list, below, `ui.7`), stale/unanchored suggestions, and review history below (each review's provider or kind, its model, and its pending count when there is one; the row of the review on screen is highlighted and counts from the page, the others as last written: `historyCounts`, `apply.5`). The right column does not scroll as a whole: under the box, the suggestions list, its heading pinned, takes what it needs up to about 55% of the height the box leaves and scrolls on its own, and everything below it scrolls as a second region, with a handle between the two that moves the line between them (The split, below; `ui.6`), so the box stays in view while j and k step through the list; when the focus moves, a row already in view stays where it is. In a window less than 540 CSS pixels high inside, which zoom changes and a chapter does not, the column scrolls as one instead, the box at its top and the list at its full length: the smallest window, 900 by 600, from 110% zoom, and the default 1440 by 940 from 175% (C.49). Under both regions, and under the one scroller too, a footer that never scrolls holds Show backups (6.5, `apply.11`). Category filter chips sit under the toolbar. Status bar: a "?" legend, pending/accepted/dismissed counts, the count not saved to the chapter ("3 not saved to chapter", in `--unsaved`, only when there is one), the time the review on screen was last written ("Decisions kept 14:02", 6.4; nothing for a chapter whose review has not been written), provider and model used ("OpenAI · gpt-5-mini-..."), cost (with "estimate" when it is the app's own arithmetic), Claude's usage notice in `--warn` (`describeRateLimit` in `src/shared/ui/usage.ts`, `setup.9`, C.64: "5-hour limit 85% used, resets 15:45" from 80% of a limit, when Claude Code sends the share, which it does only while it warns itself (C.64), "Weekly Opus limit reached, resets Sat 09:00" once one is reached (the weekly Sonnet limit named as the weekly limit on Pro and Enterprise, as Claude Code names it), "Using extra usage (may be billed)" while extra usage pays, and nothing otherwise; a reset that is not today gives its day, "tomorrow 09:00", "Sat 09:00" or "29 Oct 09:00"; its tooltip says it in full with the time left, "85% of your Claude plan's 5-hour limit is used. It resets at 15:45, in 2 hours and 10 minutes." (`rateLimitTitle`); worded again every 30 s while it shows, so the time left keeps up and it goes once its limit resets; only while Claude is in use, the provider or Setup open), the active provider's readiness, a button that opens Setup for Claude and Settings for OpenAI (5.6, `setup.7`), and the zoom control. Its items never break in two; zoomed in, the bar wraps onto a second row instead. In a window narrow in CSS pixels, as zooming in makes it, the file list and the right column shrink and then fold to strips that open them as drawers (Narrow windows, below; `ui.3`).

**The review selector** (`apply.26`). Above both regions, and above the one scroller too, so it never scrolls, a select lists the open chapter's reviews as History does, newest first by `createdAt`: History's date, the provider (or the kind, "manual" or "file", for a review no model wrote), the model, "N pending" when there are any, and, unlike History, "N not saved to chapter" when the review holds changes the chapter file does not have, in the status bar's words (`unsavedPhrase`), joined by " · " ("17/10/2026, 14:02:11 · Claude · claude-haiku-4-5 · 3 pending · 1 not saved to chapter"; `reviewChoiceLabel`), the review on screen selected and counted from the page as its History row is, the others as last written (`ReviewSummary.unsavedCount`). The unsaved count is there because the chapter list's dot, Save and the leave and close questions follow only the review on screen, and changing reviews asks nothing, so the selector is where changes left in another review show (6.4, `apply.26` review). Its accessible name is "Review shown", and its tooltip is the selected entry's label, which a narrow panel cuts off. When the review on screen is not the newest by `createdAt`, a warning mark stands before the select and its frame takes `--warn`; the mark's tooltip, spoken name and the select's description are "Not the newest review. The newest is from <date>." (`notNewestText`). Choosing another entry opens it as a History row does, with no question (7.7): the chapter opens with it from then on. A choice hands the focus back to the page (the select's `blur`), as a History row's click leaves it on a button, so j, k, a and the arrows act on the suggestions at once; a focused select takes them as typing (`isTypingTarget`). While its list is closed and no modifier is held, the select holds back the arrows, Home, End, Page Up and Page Down (`pickerHoldsKey`), which on Windows change a closed select at once and so would open another review at every press, the arrows being review keys besides; Alt+Down, Alt+Up, F4 and Space still open the list, where the arrows only move the highlight until Enter or a click chooses. Type-ahead is left to the select: a digit typed into it, the start of a date, picks the next review whose date starts with it, once, and the focus then goes back to the page. The review on screen is listed even before History has it (a new review before its first write, or before History is read again), and History's entries of another chapter, still listed for a moment after a chapter opens, are left out. A chapter with no review shows a disabled select reading "No reviews yet". The pure part is `reviewPicker` in `src/shared/ui/history.ts`; the side panel draws it, and it fades and goes inert with the rest of the panel while a review of the chapter runs (7.6). The focused suggestion's box is directly under it (`ui.5`, below).

**The focused suggestion** (`ui.5`). Directly under the review selector and above the list, the focused suggestion is a box of fixed height, 139 CSS px, that fits its heading line, the category (with the anchor when it is not exact), Original and Suggested on a line each, and two lines of explanation. Each line in it has a line height of its own, so no font's metrics change the sum (`.side__focus` in app.css). A longer suggestion or explanation scrolls inside the box, below the heading line, which stays in view; each newly focused suggestion shows from its first line. Nothing below the box moves when the focus does: the list and the comments start at the same y whatever is focused. Until `ui.5` the details sat between the list and the comments, at the height of their text, so the comments region grew and shrank as the focus moved and often showed one or two comments (the author, with the `1c0877c` build on a 130-chapter manuscript). The heading line reads "Focused suggestion" and ends in the pencil the list rows use, an icon button named "Edit suggestion" with the tooltip "Edit suggestion (e)", which opens the suggestion editor as `e` does; Tab goes from the selector to it and then to the list. When the box has more text than it shows, its scrolling part is a Tab stop of its own between the pencil and the list, as Chromium makes every scroller with nothing focusable in it, so a keyboard user can reach the rest; there the arrows scroll it, as do Space, Shift+Space, Page Up, Page Down, Home and End, and `j` and `k` still step through the list (`arrowsScrollBox` in `src/shared/ui/scroll.ts`, asked by keyboard.ts before the review keys). The arrows were review keys there at first and moved the focus to the next suggestion, which also sent the box back to its top, so only Space scrolled it (`ui.5` review). A click in the box does not focus it, and a box that shows its whole text is no Tab stop, so the arrows step through the list from everywhere else as before. With nothing focused there is no pencil, and the box keeps its height and says "Press j or click a row to focus a suggestion." The category keeps its own line: on the heading line it would cut the heading or itself short in a narrow panel. The pencil is enabled while the suggestion's paragraph is open for editing, as the Edit button was, and a card says why nothing opens (7.4). In the short window's one scroller the box is the first thing, at the same height, and scrolls away with the list: pinned above it, it would leave the default window at 200% 63 of the scroller's 202 px, and a box that grew with its text would move the list under the pointer at a click; so there a long explanation's scrollbar is the one scroller inside another the panel has, beside the panel's own. The price is height: the list and the region below share what the box leaves, so in the smallest window at 100% the list's cap falls from 191 to 115 px (about three and a half rows) and the comments region has 94 px in view at all times, where the details used to take most of its 157; `ui.6`'s split lets the author move the line between the two (below).

**The split** (`ui.6`). Between the list and the region below, where a line divided them, a handle 7 CSS px tall with a grip at its middle moves that line up or down, so that once the suggestions are dealt with the comments can take most of the panel (the author, 2026-10-17). It is a control of its own: a focusable `role="separator"` named "Split between suggestions and comments", its value the list's share of the height in per cent ("Suggestions 23% of the height"), its tooltip "Drag to give the suggestions or the comments more room. Double-click it, or press Enter, for the usual split.", with the row-resize pointer, which keeps its shape over whatever a drag crosses (`.splitting` on the root while it lasts), and a focus ring in `--accent` drawn inside its band, where neither region covers it. The grip is in `--ink-faint`, which has 3:1 on the panel in both themes, and darkens under the pointer. A drag follows the pointer with the pointer captured; a press on the handle does not take the focus, as a click in the focused suggestion's box does not, so the arrows go on stepping through the list after a drag. Tab reaches it after the list's last row, wherever it can move (below), and there the up and down arrows move it 24 CSS px, Home and End to the limits, and Enter puts back the usual split, as a double-click does; j and k still step (`splitTakesKey` in `src/shared/ui/split.ts`, asked by keyboard.ts before the review keys). Where the panel has room for it, each region keeps three rows in view whatever the split (`SPLIT_MIN_ROWS`): the list its heading and three of its rows, the region below three list rows' height of what it scrolls, which is the Comments heading and the top of the first comment; in the default window at 100% the list then has 103 to 475 of the 568 CSS px the two share, and the handle moves by that height less 196 px, the two least heights (103 and 86) and the handle's 7. A shorter panel has no room for both (C.59, from the `ui.6` review). There the list has its heading and three rows or 55% of the height, whichever is less (`splitMin`), and the region below the rest, so a split set never leaves the region below less than the usual split leaves it under a full list: in the smallest window, 900 by 600, at 100%, with the chips and the status bar on two rows each, the 180 CSS px under the box give the list 99 and the region below 54 px of what it scrolls, about two and a half rows, whatever the split; a 1000 by 900 window at 150%, in the drawer, with the chips on three rows, has 136 px, which give 75 and 34. Where the handle could move less than one arrow step, under 220 px at 100%, it is no control (`splitMovable`): it has `aria-disabled`, leaves the Tab order, and is drawn as the plain line, with no grip and the usual pointer, its tooltip "The panel is too short to move this line. Make the window taller, or zoom out, to give it room."; a press, a drag, a double-click and its keys do nothing, and should it have the focus when the window shrinks, the arrows step through the list again. In the smallest window at 100% that is any chapter whose chips take two rows or whose status bar takes two; with both on one row, 228 px, the handle moves 32 px. A move that would go against the pointer or the key does nothing, so a list of one or two rows under the usual split, shorter than its least, stays where it is on a move up and grows to three rows on a move down (`splitMove`). The split is kept in settings (`sideSplit`, 11.3 d) as the list's share of the height the list, the handle and the region below share, which is the panel under the focused suggestion's box and above the footer, so the line keeps its place as the window, the zoom or the chips change that height; null is the usual split, where the list takes what it needs up to 55% (C.59). A split set keeps the list at its share however many rows it holds. app.css draws a share as `clamp(least, share * 100%, 100% - room below)`, with both limits measured by the page (`splitRoom`; `useSplit` in `src/renderer/state/splitHandle.ts`), so a resize needs no script. The page takes a new split at once and writes it through `settings:set`, one write after another, a run of key presses ending in one write of the last, and a close waits for the write with the others (`state/split.ts`). In the short window's one scroller there is no handle and the split is ignored (C.49); in a drawer (Narrow windows, below) it applies as in place wherever the window is tall enough for two regions.

**Zoom** (round 5). The whole window zooms, prose and controls together, from 80% to 200% in fixed steps (`shared/ui/zoom.ts`): the status bar's minus and plus, a click on the percentage to reset, Ctrl+plus / Ctrl+minus / Ctrl+0, and Ctrl+wheel. `src/main/zoom.ts` is the one place that decides it: it applies the level to the window, keeps it in settings (`zoom`), puts it back after every load, and tells the page (`view:zoom-changed`) so the label follows. The keys are caught in `before-input-event`, so they work wherever the focus is, and Ctrl+wheel arrives as Electron's `zoom-changed` event. The side columns give way to the prose when the window is narrow in CSS pixels, which is what zooming in does (Narrow windows, below).

**Narrow windows** (`ui.3`). The chapter list and the side panel give way so that the centre column keeps at least 480 CSS px (`PROSE_MIN`) while the window allows it. `columnLayout` in `src/shared/ui/columns.ts` works the layout out from the page's own width, which zoom changes and nothing on the page does, so it cannot feed back on itself. It takes the first of four steps that leaves the centre column its 480 px: (1) both columns at the widths they had before `ui.3`, the chapter list 18% of the window between 170 and 230 px and the side panel 24% between 240 and 320 px; (2) both shrink toward their floors, 150 and 200 px, each in proportion to what it can give, so neither reaches its floor first; (3) the chapter list folds to a strip 32 px wide at the window's left edge, and the side panel takes its usual width, or less down to its floor; (4) the side panel folds too, to a strip at the right edge, and the centre column takes the rest. When even that is under 480 px (`body--narrow`), the review pane's side padding narrows from 40 to 16 px (`NARROW_PANE_PADDING`); the margin column never shows there. Column widths are whole pixels, rounded down. In the smallest window, 900 by 600, at 100% both columns shrink a little (168 and 236 px, centre 480); at 110% the chapter list folds (centre 532); from 125% both do, which leaves the centre column 643, 525, 441 and 378 px at 125%, 150%, 175% and 200%, and the prose's text 487, 371, 337 and 275 px. In 1100 by 700 the list folds at 150% and both from 175%. In the default 1440 by 940 nothing changes up to 150%; the list folds from 175%, and the side panel never folds (200 px at 200%). A folded column is one button the height of the column (`ColumnStrip`): a chevron at the top pointing the way its drawer goes, the column's name, "Chapters" or "Suggestions", written down it and cut off where a short window leaves no room, the tooltip "Show the chapter list" or "Show suggestions, comments and history" ("Hide" while open), and `aria-expanded` (`stripText`). That is how the author gets the column back: a click, Enter or Space opens it as a drawer beside its strip, over the edge of the prose, at the column's usual width, less what the window cannot spare (both strips and 48 px of the centre column stay uncovered, `DRAWER_MARGIN`), with a shadow; one drawer at a time, and Tab goes from the strip into its drawer. The drawer closes from its strip; with Escape, first when the focus is in the drawer or on its strip, which then takes the focus, and otherwise after the suggestion editor and the find bar, never behind a dialog, and never with something else on the same press: an Escape something else has already taken (`defaultPrevented`), such as one in the find bar's box, which closes the bar before keyboard.ts sees it, leaves the drawer open, and so does one pressed while the Bulk menu or the status bar's legend is open, which close on it themselves (`popupTakesEscape`; `ui.3` review, where one press in the find box closed the bar and the drawer); with a press anywhere in the prose's scroller, which also does what it would have done; and, for the chapter list, once a chapter is chosen in it or made with its +. Choosing a review in the side panel's drawer leaves it open. Widening the window or zooming out until the column has room puts it back in place, and a drawer left open is forgotten then (`shownDrawer`). With no chapter open the side panel's strip has no button, as the panel is empty. Each column's wrapper (`.column`, `display: contents` while the column is in place) stays in the tree whatever the layout, so the list and the panel keep their state as the window changes; a closed drawer is hidden and `inert`. The drawer is above the page (z-index 10) and under the first-use hint (11), the notification cards (12), the lock card, the menus, the dialogs and the hover card, so neither a hint nor a card is ever lost behind it; while open it covers the edge of the prose and the find bar's end there. Its background is the page's (`--bg`), which is what lies under the column in place, so the side panel, faded while a review of the chapter runs (7.6), shows no prose through it (`ui.3` review). The page measures `.body` with a ResizeObserver and renders the new layout before the frame is painted (`flushSync`), so a zoom step never shows a frame of the old layout, and the first layout comes from the window's width (`useColumns` in `src/renderer/state/columns.ts`); the widths reach app.css as custom properties on `.body` (C.58).

**Blue buttons** darken on hover and press (`--accent-hover`, `--accent-active`) rather than taking the generic near-white hover, which had left their white text unreadable.

**Toolbar order**, left to right: the unsaved dot (only when something is not saved to the chapter) and the chapter title, "Review with AI" (progress and Cancel while it runs), "Submit manual review", "Bulk" with a dropdown holding Accept all, Dismiss all and Reset, "Save to chapter" with its count ("Save 12 changes to chapter", 6.4), "Copy result", "Settings". It has to fit a 1100 px window on one row, at 125% too while no review runs, which is why model and effort moved into the review dialog (5.5) and the three bulk buttons became one. The chapter title takes the room the buttons leave (there is no spacer) and is what gives way first: it ends in an ellipsis, with the full name in its tooltip, down to about 8em (`flex: 1 1 8em`). Past that, zoomed in or in the smallest window from 125%, the row wraps as the status bar does: the last buttons move to a second row on the right, and no button label ever breaks onto a second line (`apply.4`). While a review runs, its progress label (at least 15em, about 250 CSS px at "Writing suggestions (2345 tokens) (1m 12s)") and Cancel take about 200 CSS px more than Review with AI, so at 1100 px and 125% Copy result and Settings move to the second row until it ends. The label itself is free to wrap: the row wraps first, so it keeps one line, and if it is ever given less room than its words they break inside its own box rather than spilling over the name; with `white-space: nowrap` it had been drawn leftwards over the dot and the name, and cut off at the window's edge.

**First-use hint** (`apply.12`). The first time the page holds a change the chapter file does not have, a note hangs under the toolbar, over the top of the side panel, its arrow on Save to chapter: "Your decisions are kept in this review, not in your chapter yet. Press Save to chapter (Ctrl+S) to write them into Chapter 01 - The Flood.md. The version it replaces is backed up first.", with the chapter's own file name and extension, and a Got it button (`saveHintText` in `src/shared/ui/saveHint.ts`, drawn by `SaveHint.tsx`). Got it, or any Save to chapter that reaches the file, puts it away for good, whether or not it was showing: the toolbar's button, Ctrl+S, the leave and close prompts' Save, "Save N changes, then review" and "then build" (App's `onSaved`, which also runs for a save that found the file already reading like the page). Its id, `save-to-chapter`, goes into `dismissedHints` (11.3 d) through `settings:set` (`dismissHint` in `src/renderer/state/hints.ts`), one write after another, each from the settings as they then stand; the hint leaves the screen at once, before the write, and a close waits for that write with the review and paragraph writes, so Save and close puts it away too. A write that fails keeps it away for the session, and the next start shows it again. Until it is put away, it shows whenever the review on screen has a change not saved to the chapter (`saveHintVisible`), and nothing else dismisses it: turning the change back, opening another chapter and a restart only hide it until a change is unsaved again. It waits while a review of the chapter runs or a save is under way, since Save to chapter cannot be pressed then, and with the side panel folded it gives way to the prose (below). It also waits while the Setup screen or the welcome guide is up, and shows again when they close: neither has a side panel, and hung over them across the toolbar's width it covered the guide's Skip and Setup's Close in the smallest window (884 by 561 CSS px inside) until Got it (`saveHintVisible`'s `singleScreen`, `setup.11` review). It sits inside the toolbar, `top: calc(100% + 9px)`, so it hangs below the toolbar's last row and never covers one of its buttons, whichever row Save has wrapped onto. It keeps to the side panel's column, 300 px wide or the column less 8 px at each side, lined up 8 px in from the column's right edge, and moves left within the column to bring its arrow over Save (`saveHintPlacement`, the column measured from `.body .side`), measured again whenever the toolbar, the button, the side panel or the centre column changes size; so it never meets the notification cards, the find bar or the chapter's text, which are all in the column beside it. With the side panel folded to a strip (Narrow windows, above), its column is where its drawer opens, beside the strip, whether the drawer is open or not: the hint stays clear of both strips and hangs over the top right of the prose instead, and there it gives way to the prose (`ui.3` review). It waits while a paragraph is open for editing (7.4), whose text and Done it would cover, and once it has shown, the author's next press anywhere but on the hint, review key (j, k, the arrows, a, u, d, e, n) or drawer opened sets it aside for the rest of the session while the panel stays folded. The press or key still does what it does, nothing is written, and with the panel drawn in place again the hint shows over it as before. The press, key or drawer that makes the first change comes before the hint shows, so it never sets it aside (`saveHintVisible`'s `sideFolded`, `editingParagraph` and `setAside`; `saveHintGivesWay` in `src/renderer/state/hints.ts`, called by a capture-phase `pointerdown` listener that `SaveHint.tsx` adds while it hangs over the prose, by keyboard.ts after a review key, and by a strip opening its drawer). A full-width row under the toolbar that pushed the prose down instead would have covered nothing, but in a 900 by 600 window with five chip categories it is 91 to 111 CSS px tall, where the prose has 287, 166, 112 and 68 px at 125%, 150%, 175% and 200%, so from 175% it would have left no prose at all. What it may cover: the top of the side panel (the review selector, its warning mark included, and the top of the focused suggestion's box: its heading line with the pencil, the category and the top of the Original line, as the hint ends at 179 CSS px in the default window at 100%, so the list's heading and rows stay clear, seen in the `ui.5` window check; until `ui.5`, the suggestions heading and first rows, seen in the `apply.26` window check, where Got it uncovered them; before `ui.3`, at 200% in a 900 by 600 window, the whole panel and two counts on the status bar's first row), the chips at the right-hand end of their strip when they run that far (with five categories, at about 880 CSS px wide or less: a 1100 px window from 125%), and the find bar's last buttons where the centre column is too narrow for the bar and they spill into the side panel's column (1100 by 720 at 200% before `ui.3`). With the side panel folded (900 by 600 from 125%, `ui.3`), until the author goes on: the top right of the prose and of the find bar, at 200% the right part of the prose over its whole height and two counts on the status bar's first row, the side panel's drawer's top, the review selector included, as in place, when a change made in the open drawer brings the hint up over it, and at 200% the right end of an open chapter list's drawer, its + included; never the paragraph editor, as it waits while one is open. Got it is the way out, and the keys, Escape for the find bar among them, go on working meanwhile. The arrow points at Save's middle when it can reach it, else at the nearest point of the button it can, and only at a part of the button with no toolbar item under it, 12 px clear of the button's ends and of anything below (`SAVE_HINT_ARROW_CLEAR`); the hint hangs below the last row, so an arrow under Copy result would point at Copy result. When Save has wrapped onto a row above Copy result and Settings and every part of it has one under it (about 720 CSS px wide or less: 900 by 600 at 125%, 1424 by 901 and 1280 by 800 at 200%), the hint has no arrow (`data-arrow="none"`) and its words name the button. It sits under the notification cards (z-index 11 against 12), so a card wins should the two ever meet, above the page's unpositioned content, and under every dialog's backdrop, the menus and the hover card, and takes no focus, so the review keys go on working while it shows. When `apply.21` calls for a sentence about Obsidian, it goes in `SAVE_HINT_OBSIDIAN_NOTE`, and the hint adds it after its own three. The empty page ("Nothing open") and Accept all's question, a chip's Accept all included, end with "Nothing reaches the chapter until you press Save to chapter." (`NOTHING_UNTIL_SAVE`), the question on a line of its own.

**The Setup screen** (`setup.7` to `setup.9`; 5.6 items 4 to 7 give what it says and does). Claude's setup is a screen, not a dialog: "Set up Claude", with Close, takes the place of the chapter list, the prose and the side panel, while the toolbar and the status bar stay, the notification cards sit at the top of its column, and the dialogs and the close question open over it with the page inert under them. Its status card says what the last check found, with Check again, Test the connection where a test can tell something, and Try the review anyway where the dialog would open anyway; under it the steps for the view on the system's own tab, each command with Copy, then Troubleshooting, Advanced and the privacy footer. The ways to it: the launch card's Open Setup, the status bar's Claude label (a button: Setup for Claude, Settings for OpenAI), Setup beside Claude account in Settings, the welcome guide's Set up Claude, Review with AI when Claude Code is missing or signed out, and a review that fails for a reason Setup can fix. The review keys, Ctrl+S and Ctrl+F do nothing behind it, and Escape does nothing on it, as it has no Cancel of its own. No button on it reads like a sign-in, and it starts none (5.6 item 7).

**The welcome guide** (`setup.11`). A fresh install has no settings file, so `onboardingDone` is false (11.3 d), and once the settings are in, the welcome guide takes the place of the chapter list, the prose and the side panel, in the Setup screen's layout (5.6 item 5): the toolbar and the status bar stay, the notification cards sit at the top of its column, it scrolls across the window's width with its page a centred column 720 CSS px wide at most (`.setup`, `.setup__page`), and the dialogs and the close question open over it, the page inert under them. Settings from before 1.0 that name a manuscript folder count as done, so an existing install never sees it unasked. The Setup screen comes first: the guide's Set up Claude opens Setup over it, and Setup's Close goes back to it. `welcomeShown`, `welcomeSteps` and `welcomeStart` in `src/shared/ui/welcome.ts` decide what it says, and `src/renderer/components/WelcomeScreen.tsx` draws it: "Welcome to Lapidary" with Skip at the head, a line saying that three steps get the app ready, that each says when it is done, that they can be done in any order and that Settings has Show the welcome guide again, then the three steps, each a card with its number, or a tick once it is done, at its side, its title, where it stands in a few words (read out as it changes, `aria-live`), what it means, and its buttons. The blue button is what the step still needs; a step that is done has none.

1. **Connect Claude.** Where it stands is the Setup screen's own title for the last check (`setupStatus`: "Checking Claude Code…", "Claude Code is not installed", "Claude Code is not signed in", "Claude is ready" and the rest), so it follows every check, the focus re-check included (5.6 item 6). It is done when Claude can review: ready, with an API key, or at a usage limit, which resets by itself, but not on a plan that cannot run Claude Code. Set up Claude opens Setup (5.6), and "Use OpenAI instead (experimental, pay per use)" makes OpenAI the provider (`settings:set`). With OpenAI chosen, the step says what OpenAI's runtime says ("OpenAI: no API key", "OpenAI ready (key ends abcd)"), that reviews are billed per token to the account that owns the key and that OpenAI in the app is experimental, with Open Settings, for the key, and Use Claude instead; it is done once there is a key.
2. **Choose your manuscript folder.** Where it stands is the folder's path, or "No folder chosen yet"; it is done once one is chosen. A line says it is the folder that holds the chapters, the vault or a folder inside it, and four points say what that means: the app lists every .md and .txt file in it and its subfolders as a chapter, leaving out folders whose names start with a dot (6.1); it changes a chapter file only when Save to chapter is pressed, after copying the version it replaces into a backup in its own data folder (6.3, 6.5); it adds one file of its own there, GUIDELINES.md, from a template the first time it needs it, and never changes it after that (section 10), or, when Settings names a guidelines file, it names that one instead; and reviews are kept in the app's own data folder, given by its path, not in the manuscript folder, or in the reviews folder chosen in Settings (`settings:effective`, 6.4). Choose folder, then Choose another folder, is the chapter list's folder picker, which reads the chapter list again.
3. **Fill in your style sheet.** It is done when the guidelines file is there and its style sheet is `filled` (`guidelines:status`, section 10). Otherwise it says "GUIDELINES.md is not there yet", "Still empty", with Review with AI's note (`STYLE_SHEET_EMPTY`, 5.5), "No Style sheet section", "Not checked yet" before the status is in, "Could not read GUIDELINES.md" with main's words, or "Choose a manuscript folder first", its buttons disabled. The buttons are `setup.10b`'s: Open in Obsidian, which reads "Create and open in Obsidian" while the file is missing, Open with default app and Show in folder, through `guidelines:open` and `shell:reveal`, each creating the file from the template first when it is missing. Open in Obsidian is disabled for a `.txt` file, the tooltips are the ones Settings has (`openGuidelinesTitle`), and a line under them says what the last one did, or why it did nothing (`openedGuidelinesNote`). The status is read as the guide opens, whenever the manuscript folder, the guidelines file or the reviews folder changes in the settings, after each button, and whenever the window gets the focus back, as when the author returns from filling the style sheet in; only the newest answer counts.

Under the steps, **Start**, blue, is disabled until a manuscript folder is chosen, since the app has nothing to open without one, and beside it a line says what is left: "Choose a manuscript folder to start, or skip the guide for now.", or "Still to do: Connect Claude. You can start now and come back to it with Show the welcome guide again, in Settings.", or nothing once every step is done (`welcomeStart`). Start and Skip both put the guide away at once and write `onboardingDone: true`; a write that fails says so in a card, as the guide then shows again at the next start (`welcomeNotKept`). The guide stays away for the rest of the run whatever the settings say, by the store's `welcomeClosed` (`closeWelcome` in `src/renderer/state/welcome.ts`): after a write that failed, main still holds `false`, and the next settings reply from anywhere (Got it on the Save hint, the split, a change in Settings, the review dialog's model) brought the guide back over the chapter mid-session until the `setup.11` review. Settings' **Show the welcome guide again** writes `onboardingDone: false`, with `pro-model-preselected` added to `dismissedHints` in the same write so the guide shown again never changes the model (`welcomeAgainPatch`, `reopenWelcome`; C.67), and closes Settings and Setup, so the guide comes back, once that write is in, in place of whatever was open; a chapter open behind it stays open and is on screen again after Start or Skip. While the guide shows, the launch card (5.6 item 4) is not put up, and one that is up goes, since the first step says the same; the review keys, Ctrl+S, and Escape for the find bar and the suggestion editor do nothing behind it (`isModalOpen`), and Escape does nothing on the guide itself, as on Setup. No button on it reads like a sign-in.

Messages, progress and confirmation:

- **Notification cards.** Messages appear as floating cards anchored to the top-right corner of the manuscript pane, sized to their text and stacked when several are open; on the Setup screen, which has no manuscript pane, they sit at the top of its column, before it in the flow, so they never cover its Close or Check again and stay in view while it scrolls (5.6, `setup.7` and its review). A card closes only when its X is clicked: no click-away and no timeout, because a save error that vanished before it was read is worse than a stale card. The one card the app takes away itself is the launch card about Claude, under the key `claude-runtime` (5.6): once a check leaves Setup nothing to do, OpenAI becomes the provider, or Setup is opened or the welcome guide shows, what it says is no longer true or no longer needed, so it is withdrawn by its key (`setup.7`, `runtimeCardAction`). Tones: info, warn, error. Cards about a recurring event (the watcher reloading the chapter, a save) pass a key, and a new one replaces the old, so a long typing session in Obsidian cannot bury the screen. Save errors use this path like everything else: every card that says how a Save to chapter ended, failures included, shares the key `SAVE_CARD`, so a save refused again and again (a backup that cannot be made, 6.5) leaves one card, and a save that then works replaces it with "Saved to the chapter file.". The card Save to chapter shows when it finds the chapter changed on disk and rebases has no key and stays, because it counts the suggestions lost. A chapter that could not be opened, its file or the review it opens with unreadable (6.4, `apply.25`), says why under the key `open-chapter`: the next try replaces the card, and an open that works closes it. When the review is at fault the card names the chapter, "Could not open Chapter 12.md: ...", since main's words name only the review file or folder; a review whose latest decisions could not be written does not open either, from the chapter list, History or the review selector, and since `apply.25`'s review History and the selector use the same key for every open that fails, where each failure used to add a card of its own (6.4, `src/shared/ui/open.ts`). Cards sit under a dialog's backdrop (z-index 12 against 20), so a dialog that runs a save, Review with AI's "Save N changes, then review" and Submit manual review's "Save N changes, then build", shows inside itself what the save's cards said when it did not reach the file (5.5, 7.5); the cards were not raised above dialogs, because in the top-right corner they would cover the dialog itself, and at 200% there is no room beside it (`apply.20` review). Control glyphs are inline SVG rather than characters (see C.18).
- **Progress.** While Claude works the label cycles "Thinking", "Thinking.", "Thinking..", "Thinking..." every 500 ms with the elapsed time beside it, then switches to "Writing suggestions (N tokens)" once output tokens arrive. Cancel stays next to it. `src/shared/ui/progress.ts` owns the wording.
- **Confirmation.** "Accept all", "Dismiss all", "Reset" and the per-chip bulk actions open a small in-app modal, never `window.confirm`. It states the exact count and what is left alone: accept and dismiss only ever move pending suggestions, reset only clears decisions. Escape and Cancel back out; when nothing would change, a card says so instead of asking. `src/shared/ui/bulk.ts` computes both the affected ids and the wording. The same dialog asks the three-way questions before leaving a chapter or closing the window (11.3 i, `apply.6`): Cancel, the middle choice and the blue Save, left to right, in a dialog 460 px wide instead of 420, with the question's own default focused (Keep for later, or Cancel when only something would be lost). A question that cannot save hides Save, and an answer it does not show counts as Cancel. Labels never break inside a button; when the row does not fit, zoomed in, the last button moves to a second row on the right. `src/shared/ui/confirm.ts` decides the buttons and the focus. A question can be asked from inside another dialog or with the suggestion editor open, so the dialog sits above every other surface (`.modal-backdrop--confirm`, rendered last), takes Escape for itself, and shows the focus ring on its default however it was opened, since Enter answers that button. It holds the keyboard too: while a question is open everything else in the window is `inert`, the page (`.app-shell__page`) and the four other dialogs (`.app-shell__dialogs`, `ui.4`), both wrappers with `display: contents` and so laying out nothing, so Tab and Shift+Tab go round its buttons only, and when it closes the focus goes back to the element that had it when it opened, if that is still on the page (`apply.7` review). A Bulk action's menu item goes with the menu as the question opens, so Bulk takes the focus first and gets it back (`ui.4`).
- **Find.** Ctrl+F opens a find bar as a strip across the top of the manuscript pane, above the scroller, so it is in view wherever the author is in the chapter (C.33); pressing Ctrl+F again returns to the box with the query selected. It shows a match count ("3 of 12"); Enter and Shift+Enter or the arrows step, Escape closes. Matches are painted with the CSS Custom Highlight API over the existing text nodes, so hunk spans, ghosts and offsets are untouched (C.15). Ghost text and the paragraph rail are excluded from the search. The pane scrolls to the active match only when the author asks to go there, by typing or stepping (`find.seek` in the store); a redraw, such as accepting a suggestion with the bar open, repaints the matches without moving the page.
- **Scrolling.** Every scroll the app makes for the author (keyboard focus, a list click, a find match, a comment, a paragraph opened for editing) is animated by `state/scroll.ts` with the timing in `shared/ui/scroll.ts`: quick for a short hop, proportionally faster for a long one, and never more than 320 ms. A new scroll cancels the one in flight, so rapid j/k or Enter always lands on the last thing asked for. Reduced motion makes it instant. Every element that scrolls has an opaque background in a colour token, so it keeps a composited layer of its own with LCD text while it moves (C.48).

Review pane rendering:

- **Paragraph rail** (Phase 5d). Every body paragraph has, in a rail to its left, its number (from 1, matching the prompt, 5.2) and a faded pen that opens it for editing in place (7.4). A paragraph with an open comment has its number in the comment colour, where a dot used to be. The rail sits in padding added to the measure, is never selected or copied with the prose, and the find bar skips it. A block that spans two paragraphs (a deletion straddling the blank line) is labelled "12-13". Frontmatter has no rail. The rail is 39 px wide and ends 11 px short of the text, where the margin marks sit (`small.8`): a bar, 3 px by 0.9em in the category colour, beside each line with a pending small change, and an ink arrowhead beside the line where the focused suggestion starts, whatever its size. A bar is a button 11 by 39 CSS px outside the prose: a click selects its change without scrolling and without taking the keyboard focus, so a, d, u and e then act, and hovering shows its card under the change, for reading. The arrowhead is the same button, which takes no pointer, so a bar that turns into it under the pointer keeps its element and its card closes as the pointer leaves it; a mark that goes away under the pointer, decided or made again by a save, closes the card it opened (`markHover` in `src/renderer/state/hover.ts`; the `small` review, C.68). The marks are children of the paragraph, placed by CSS anchor positioning on their change's live text (`marginMarks` and `markAnchorName` in `src/shared/ui/marks.ts`), so they follow reflow and zoom with no measuring; a paragraph join's bar sits beside the blank line it would remove. Decided small changes get no bar, so a worked chapter goes quiet. A merged number such as "12-13" stays on one line.
- Prose font (Georgia or Literata with a serif fallback), `line-height: 2.3`, `white-space: pre-wrap`, max width about 70 characters. Text is rendered character-for-character from `base.text` so offsets stay valid; Markdown emphasis markers stay visible but are styled (italic for `*...*`, bold for `**...**`, headings larger). No Markdown-to-HTML conversion.
- **The manuscript does not scroll sideways** (`ui.2`), wherever the centre column is wider than the pane's own side padding, which since `ui.3` is every window at every zoom (the narrowest centre column is 378 CSS px, a 900x600 window at 200%; Narrow windows, above). Every ghost stops inside the pane's side padding, or short of the margin column (below). A word longer than the line, such as a web address, breaks inside the word (`.prose { overflow-wrap: break-word }`; ghosts are `white-space: pre` and never wrap). `.pane` clips anything else at its own edge, the outside of its side padding (`overflow-x: clip`, which does not make it a scroller), and has `min-width: 0`, so in a narrow pane it gives the position strip its 14 px instead of staying as wide as its longest word and pushing the strip out, as it did in a 900x600 window at 150%. What the pane clips is hidden and cannot be clicked, so anything in it wider than a narrow column has to wrap: the paragraph editor's Cancel and Done share a row with its hint where there is room and otherwise wrap below it, on the right (7.4; at 900x600 and 150% the text column was 25 px before `ui.3`, and Done had been cut off, `ui.2` review). Before `ui.3` the side columns kept their widths whatever the zoom: in a 900x600 window from 175% the centre column (87 CSS px, then 25) was narrower than the pane's 80 px of side padding and the 14 px position strip, so it still scrolled sideways, and there, as in a 1100x700 window at 200%, the text column was 0 px, so the pane showed no text at all and the paragraph editor's buttons were out of reach; before `ui.2` those windows drew the chapter a letter to a line, past the pane, behind the scrollbar. Now the text column is at least 275 px (900x600 at 200%).
- Each hunk is an inline `<span class="hunk">` carrying its own `data-hunk-start` and `data-hunk-end`, with the live text in the line and a ghost row absolutely positioned above it (`top: -1.15em`, smaller font). The extra line height leaves room for the ghost. Ghost text is `user-select: none` so it can never join a selection.
  - **Colour means undecided.** Pending live text gets a 2 px underline in the category colour and a faint tint of it. Accepted gets a 2 px solid underline in the grey of the "was" ghost (`--ink-faint`) and no tint: the green underline it used to have read as "still active", which is the opposite of what it meant. Dismissed gets a 1 px dotted light-grey line. The hover tint follows the same rule, category-coloured only while pending, and so do a small change's capsule and an insertion's caret (`small.1`, `small.6`). The "?" legend and the suggestions-list status markers say the same thing, and the strip beside the scrollbar marks pending hunks only.
  - **The dismiss "x" is part of the ghost row**, immediately after the ghost text with a 0.4em gap, so it trails the ghost whatever the two widths are. Pinned to the span's corner it landed on top of the ghost's last letters whenever the ghost was wider than the live text. The row counts as part of the suggestion for the pointer and reaches down to the line of text, a strip joins the top of the word to it, and the x keeps its room while invisible, so moving from the word to the x never loses it; the hover grace period covers a diagonal path to an x that sits past the word (C.34). Clicking the ghost toggles the suggestion, as clicking the word does.
  - **Ghosts that would collide, or run out of the pane, are clipped.** Two suggestions close together on one line can have overlapping ghosts even when the words below them do not, and a long suggestion's ghost, one line of text, can run far past the right edge of the prose (a 40-word inserted sentence gave the pane a horizontal scrollbar, `ui.2`). No CSS rule can know either: both depend on where the line broke. `clipGhosts` in `src/renderer/state/ghosts.ts` measures the ghost rows after layout, and `ghostMaxWidths` in `src/shared/ui/ghosts.ts` gives any ghost that would run into the next one on the same line, or past the room its row has, a max width and an ellipsis, leaving room for the x that trails it; no ghost is cut below 22 px, nor short of the right edge of its key (`small.7`). That room (`ghostEdge`) is the pane's side padding, up to 6 px short of the pane's edge, where `.pane` clips (`GHOST_EDGE_MARGIN`), or, while the margin column shows, up to 10 px short of it (`GHOST_GAP`), so a row never covers a note. It is not the edge of the prose's text: a short ghost near the end of a line has always run a little past that, into the padding, with no scrollbar, and cutting it there made "-> watermill" read "-> water..." and an accepted insertion's lone "added" label read "ADD", sliced through a letter (`ui.2` review). The label is `inline-block` so that a replaced original's line-through does not strike it, which is also why a cut inside it ends without an ellipsis. Ghosts are clipped, not wrapped: a wrapped ghost would climb into the line above. The full text stays in the tooltip and in the suggestions list. Clipping changes no geometry the prose depends on, so it cannot loop. The pass runs after every change to the hunks, the scroller's size or the prose's, and as the margin column comes or goes.
  - The ghost is styled by **role, not position**, because the swap makes position meaningless: an original the author has replaced is always grey and struck through and is introduced by a small-caps "was"; a suggestion still on offer always carries the category colour and is introduced by an arrow. `ghostRole` in `src/shared/render.ts` decides which, and `ghostContent` what the row shows. The one exception: a small original (Small changes, below) is drawn bigger as an inline-block, which a line-through does not reach, so a replaced hyphen reads plainly as a hyphen; its "was" says it was replaced.
  - insertion (`original === ''`): live = a thin caret glyph; ghost = inserted text. Once accepted, the ghost reads "added", having no original to show (it used to read "was keep", which said the opposite), followed by what was added when that is small ("ADDED ,", `small.7`). The caret is inline at 0.8em, 2 by 15 CSS px, so it never leaves the word before it, as an inline-block one did at some window widths, and it follows the status: the category colour while pending, `--ink-faint` for an accepted deletion and `--border-strong` once dismissed (`small.1`).
  - deletion (`replacement === ''`): ghost shows a small "delete" tag, followed by the name of what it takes out when that cannot be seen ("-> DELETE SPACE").
  - **Small changes** (11.7, `small`). A change whose text is a comma, a space, a one-letter word or nothing is drawn bigger, never stored bigger: no hunk, offset or text in the line changes, so Save to chapter, Copy result, find and copy read exactly what they did (`isSmallText`, `isSmallLive` and `isSmallChange` in `src/shared/ui/small.ts`; Appendix C.68). In the line, a capsule sits under the live text, 0.75em wide or the text's own width, beneath its letters (every paragraph is isolated): the category colour at `--tint-small` with a 2 px category line at its foot while pending, a 2 px grey cup once accepted, a 1 px dotted line once dismissed, and focused, `--focus-bg`, with theme.8's hairline in the active comment's paragraph; the ghost above is not filled, as a word-sized change's is not (C.68). A transparent hit zone 0.9em wide lies over it, so the click target grows from about 5 by 22 to 16 by 23 CSS px. On a side where another change touches it, or text under two characters stands before the next, both pull back to the change's own edge (`flushBefore`, `flushAfter` in `segmentsIn`). A change whose live text holds a line break, such as a paragraph join, gets no capsule. Above the line, the ghost's small text is drawn bigger, the glyph at 1.25 times the ghost size and not struck through, and whitespace is named in small caps in a keycap, a 1 px box in the ghost's colour at 60% ("-> SPACE", "WAS PARAGRAPH BREAK"); punctuation keeps no box, as a box on every key piled the rows up on a busy line of dialogue (`small.7`). Everywhere a side is written out, the list, the hover card, the focused suggestion's box, Unanchored and the spoken name, a space, a break or small punctuation is named: "- (hyphen) -> (space)", with "(nothing)" only for the empty string (`sideText`).
  - A "?" popover in the status bar explains the three states in words, then the unsaved dot, and ends with the keys and pens (`e` rewrites the focused suggestion; the pen beside a paragraph edits it) and that nothing reaches the chapter before Save to chapter or Ctrl+S (`apply.4`). It scrolls when the window is too short for it.
  - A thin strip down the right edge of the scroller marks where the pending suggestions are; clicking a mark jumps to one.
- **Hover is not focus.** Hovering a suggestion opens a custom tooltip within 100 ms (category, reason, "Original: ... / Suggested: ...", and a pencil) and adds a hover tint. It never moves focus and never scrolls, because focus scrolls the pane and a suggestion that slid out from under the pointer is unusable. The tooltip is positioned below the words so it cannot cover the ghost. Only keyboard navigation and clicks in the suggestions list scroll the pane. Because the card now carries a control, leaving the words schedules its removal after a short grace period instead of doing it at once, and entering the card cancels that (`src/renderer/state/hover.ts`).
