- Interactions: click the ghost or hunk = toggle accepted; the "x" beside the ghost, right-click, or the `d` key = toggle dismissed; `j`/`k` and the arrows move focus, starting from the paragraphs on screen when nothing is focused or the focused change has been scrolled out of sight, and Shift+J and Shift+K go to the next and previous pending change, passing over decided ones and wrapping round (`stepFocus` in `src/shared/ui/navigate.ts`, `small.9`; letters are read in lower case and Shift from the event, so Caps Lock does not stop them); a click on a margin bar selects its change without scrolling (except that the arrows scroll the focused suggestion's box when Tab has put the focus there, `ui.5`, and move the split's handle when it has the focus, `ui.6`); `a` accept; `u` back to pending; `e` edit the focused suggestion's text; the pen in the rail edits a paragraph; `n` next paragraph with a comment; Ctrl+S saves to the chapter, under the rules in 6.3. None of these keys, the arrows and Ctrl+F included, acts while a dialog, a question, Settings, the Setup screen or the welcome guide covers the page, or while the suggestion editor is open (`reviewKeysActive` and `isModalOpen` in the store): behind Review with AI, `a` had accepted a suggestion out of sight, which changed the dialog's count and its blue button, `e` had opened the suggestion editor over the dialog, and Ctrl+F had put the focus in the find bar behind it (`apply.20` review). Escape behind a dialog is the dialog's as well: Settings and Review with AI close on it, a question cancels, and New chapter, Submit manual review, the Setup screen and the welcome guide do nothing with it, so it no longer closes the find bar or the suggestion editor out of sight behind them (`ui.1`). Nor does the suggestion editor act behind a dialog: one opened while it is open covers it, and it is inert until the dialog closes (7.4, `ui.1` review). Bulk actions in the filter chips: accept all or dismiss all of a category, behind a confirmation. Everything is reversible until Save to chapter, and after it too: a saved change turned back is one more change for the next Save to write, and each save first copies the version it replaces into the backups (6.5).
- **Suggestions list.** The right column lists every anchored hunk in document order, filtered by the category chips: a category dot, "original -> suggested" clipped to one line, a status marker, and edit, accept and dismiss icon buttons that act exactly as the pane does. A small change's row shows two words of its line on either side of a chip holding the change, each side named, "...a double [- (hyphen) -> (space)] standard, and..." (`changeInContext`, `small.5`), and its spoken name is the named summary. The list and the keys sort by start, end and id (`compareHunks`). A hunk the author wrote or rewrote shows a small "you" marker in place of the category dot. Clicking a row focuses that hunk and scrolls it to the centre of the pane; the focused row is highlighted and kept visible when focus moves by keyboard, by scrolling the list region only. Stale and unanchored hunks keep their own section below. The focused hunk's category, reason and both texts stay visible in the box of fixed height above the list, with the pencil on its heading line (The focused suggestion, above; `ui.5`).
- **Comments as margin notes.** Each comment with a paragraph is a card in a gutter to the right of the prose, aligned with the top of its paragraph and pushed down when it would overlap the card above (`stackCards` in `src/shared/ui/stack.ts`). Clicking a card highlights its paragraph; resolve and "Edit text" live on the card. The sidebar list stays, and both views share one `activeCommentId`, so clicking either highlights the same paragraph. When the pane is too narrow for a gutter the cards fall back to the sidebar alone. Everywhere comments are listed (sidebar, margin, the `n` key) they are in reading order: by paragraph, ties in the order they came, chapter-wide comments last (`sortComments`); in the sidebar, within each of its three runs, an unanchored comment by the paragraph it had (`ui.7`).
- **Resolving a comment does not remove it.** A resolved comment stays in the margin as a greyed card collapsed to one line, its category and the first few words (`commentDigest`), and expands again when clicked. It is measured at a small height so it stays out of the open comments' way. In the sidebar it moves down the one comments list, after the open and the unanchored ones (`ui.7`, below). Both views offer Unresolve, and both stay in sync as before. A resolved note that vanished read as a lost note.
- **One comments list** (`ui.7`). The sidebar lists every comment under one heading that counts the resolved ones, "Comments (5 of 8 resolved)" ("Comments (0)" with none, and "No comments." under it), so it cannot be read as 5 open of 8 (`commentsHeading`). The list runs the open comments first (those on a paragraph, then those about the whole chapter), then the unanchored, then the resolved, each run in reading order (`commentList` in `src/shared/ui/comments.ts`). An unanchored comment is one whose paragraph left the file when it changed on disk (4.5, C.60): where the paragraph number goes it reads "unanchored (was paragraph 12)", the number it had (`commentPlace`), and it is listed by that number, since one usually belongs near where it was; it has no margin card, the `n` key passes it by, and its "Edit text" says "That comment's paragraph is no longer in the chapter, so there is no one paragraph to edit." (`commentEditBlocked`, which gives a comment about the whole chapter its own words). A resolved comment keeps its quieter look and Unresolve wherever it was anchored. Under three headings, Comments, Resolved comments and Unanchored, the open ones sat under a near-empty heading once most were resolved. The Unanchored heading below still lists the suggestions that lost their place, and the status bar's counts are as they were.
- "Copy result" puts the composed text on the clipboard for pasting back into Obsidian.
- Settings dialog: manuscript folder, reviews folder (showing the effective path, whether it is the default, Change and Show in folder), backups (the backups root, Show in folder and the note in 6.5; `apply.11`), guidelines file path (default `<manuscript>/GUIDELINES.md`, created from the template if missing, with Change and Show in folder beside it and, on a row of their own under them so the path keeps its width, Open in Obsidian and Open guidelines file, the system's app for the file; Open in Obsidian is disabled for a `.txt` file, and Settings' note says "Asked Obsidian to open" and the path; `setup.10b`), Claude model, effort, Appearance (Match system, Light or Dark, saved through `settings:set`, with the note "Match system follows your computer's light or dark setting."; 11.3 d and e), Claude account (`claudeAccountLine` in `src/shared/ui/setup.ts`: when the check found Claude ready, the account's email, or "signed in", and its plan, then ", API key from <source>" when Claude Code signs in with an API key, or ", token from ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN" with the token (C.71), and nothing more for a plan's sign-in, whose `apiKeySource`, `none` where a check keeps one, names no key (`setup.17`; it read "ann@example.com (Claude Max) key from none"); otherwise what the check found, or "not checked") with Setup, which opens the Setup screen and its steps (5.6), the OpenAI API key (status line, password field, Save key, Remove key, where to get one; 5.0), and a Welcome guide row with Show the welcome guide again, which closes Settings and brings the guide back (`setup.11`, above). The shell actions go through `guidelines:open` and `shell:reveal`, which take a target name rather than a path (with a chapter's path for that chapter's backups, from which main works out the folder itself, 6.5), so the renderer can never ask the shell to open an arbitrary file. Settings is a modal like the others (`ui.1`): Done or Escape closes it, wherever the focus is, except that a question open over it takes Escape for itself, as any dialog over it would (`inOtherDialog`). The page's keys do nothing behind it (Interactions, above), and the suggestion editor, if it was open, waits under it, inert, until it closes (7.4). The focus goes into its box as it opens, so Tab starts inside it and the arrow keys and Page Down scroll it, and when it closes the focus goes back where it was, which is the toolbar's Settings button when that opened it, or to that button when where it was has gone, as after Open Settings in Review with AI (`useDialogEscape` and `useDialogFocus` in `src/renderer/state/dialog.ts`; Review with AI takes its Escape from the same hook).
- **Every dialog holds the keyboard** (`ui.4`). Settings, New chapter, Review with AI and Submit manual review are drawn outside the page, in `.app-shell__dialogs`, and while one of them is open the page is `inert`, as it is under a question (Confirmation, above), so nothing behind the backdrop can take the focus, a click or a screen reader. Tab and Shift+Tab, finding nothing else in the window that can take the focus, go round the dialog's own controls, from the last to the first and from the first to the last; there is no Tab handler of the app's own. Under a question the dialogs are inert too. `shellInert` in `src/shared/ui/dialog.ts` says which, and App feeds it the dialogs it actually draws, so the page is never inert with no dialog on screen. Each dialog takes the focus as it opens, on its box (`tabIndex={-1}`), or New chapter on its name field, and when it closes hands it back to where it was, or to its opener when that has gone: the toolbar's Settings, Review with AI or Submit manual review, or the + in the chapter list's header (`useDialogFocus`; the openers carry `data-opens`, which the `*_BUTTON_SELECTOR` constants in the same file name). Submit manual review's Build review stays enabled while the question it asks is open, since the dialog is inert under it anyway, so that Cancel can hand the focus back to it; while the save that may follow runs, `saving` disables it.
- No application menu. `Menu.setApplicationMenu(null)` removes it entirely, so Alt does nothing over the manuscript. Development keeps DevTools (F12, Ctrl+Shift+I) and reload (Ctrl+R) through a `before-input-event` handler that exists only when the app is not packaged.

### 7.4 Editing inside the app

Accepting a suggestion used to be the only change the author could make without leaving for Obsidian. They can now write their own words, and **every edit goes through the hunk model**, so Save to chapter stays the only write path (CLAUDE.md rule 1) and an edit is as reversible and as re-anchorable as anything a model suggested. The rules are pure and tested in `src/shared/edit.ts`.

1. **Edit a suggestion.** A pencil in the tooltip, on the heading line of the focused suggestion's box ("Edit suggestion", `ui.5`), and on every row of the suggestions list (and the `e` key) opens a small floating editor prefilled with the suggestion's replacement: Save and Cancel, an "Accept now" checkbox, Ctrl+Enter and Escape. It floats over the page and its menus but under every dialog: Settings, Review with AI, New chapter or Submit manual review opened while it is open covers it (`.modal-backdrop` is drawn above `.editor`), and it is `inert` until the dialog closes, when it is back as it was, words and all (`ui.1` review). The Setup screen, which takes the main view's place (5.6), takes the editor off the page with it, so the words typed are kept in the store (`EditorRequest.text`, `setEditorText`) rather than in the panel: closing Setup brings the editor back with them, and while it is open the close question still counts them (`hasDialogText` reads them from the request; `setup.7` review, as the status bar's button reaches Setup with the editor open). The pencil again on the suggestion being edited moves the editor and keeps them; another suggestion starts afresh. Until then it was drawn over them and could still be typed in and saved with Ctrl+Enter, which rewrote and accepted the suggestion behind the dialog. Saving updates `replacement`, sets `edited: true`, keeps source, category and reason, and accepts it unless "Accept now" is unticked. The new words are always a change against what the chapter file says over the span (`editSuggestion(base, ...)`, `apply.22`, C.51). A suggestion already saved into the file (`baseSide: 'replacement'`) takes the saved words as its `original` and its base side, and the new words as its replacement: ticked, it is accepted and counts as one unsaved change, and Save to chapter writes the new words; unticked, it is pending, the page keeps the saved words, and Save writes nothing for it rather than putting the model's original back. A saved change the author had turned back shows the saved words again the same way. New words that read exactly as the file does leave nothing unsaved: both sides become the file's words, and a suggestion whose two sides are the same never counts as unsaved and always shows the file's words, whatever is decided on it afterwards (`changesBase`, `decidedText`), so rewriting a saved suggestion and then back to the saved words is a no-op, and turning it back or dismissing it later still is.
2. **Edit a paragraph in place** (Phase 5d, replacing editing a mouse selection, which caught stray spaces and fought with clicking a word). The faded pen in the rail swaps the paragraph for a text box in the same typeface, prefilled with the paragraph as the page reads it, every accepted change applied. The box grows with its text; Done or Ctrl+Enter applies, Cancel or Escape discards, after asking first when the box's text has changed ("Discard your changes to paragraph 12?", with Keep editing focused; `apply.16`). Below the box a hint, Cancel and Done share a row; where the column is too narrow for that, Cancel and Done wrap below the hint, still on the right, since the pane hides whatever reaches past its side padding (section 7, `ui.2` review). **Several paragraphs can be open at once**, each with its own Done. When a hunk straddles the blank line after a paragraph, the page already shows the two as one block, and the box takes the whole block. On Done, `planBlockEdit` word-diffs the page text against the box:
   - every changed run becomes its own accepted manual hunk (`source: 'manual'`, `category: 'other'`, `reason: 'Edited by author'`), so fixing two words far apart leaves two small hunks, not one that owns the paragraph; runs separated only by whitespace join, and each is trimmed to whole words, as the full-text diff does (4.4);
   - a run that touches a suggestion, overlapping it or merely adjacent to it, grows to take the suggestion whole: a model suggestion is superseded (dismissed, unanchored, `supersededBy`, reason kept behind "Superseded by your edit: "), and one of the author's own earlier edits is simply replaced, with anything it had superseded now pointing at its replacement;
   - typing exactly the fix a suggestion offered accepts that suggestion, keeping its category and reason;
   - typing the file's own words back needs no hunk: the suggestions in the way settle on the file's words (dismissed, or accepted when a save already wrote them in);
   - emptying the box deletes the paragraph with one blank line beside it, the one after if there is a next paragraph, as the full-text diff stores a deletion;
   - newlines at either end of the box are dropped; an unchanged box closes with nothing done.

   A property test checks that, for generated chapters, suggestions, saved states and edits, the composed chapter always equals the text before the paragraph, then exactly what was typed, then the text after, with no overlapping hunks.

   While a paragraph is open, its suggestions are frozen (their list rows fade and their buttons are disabled) and Bulk is disabled, because the box started from the page as it was and must commit against the same page. If the chapter file changes on disk while a box is open, the watcher still reloads the review and the box stays open; Done then refuses, says the paragraph changed, and keeps the author's text in the box. Opening another chapter asks first when a box's text has changed, in the same question as changes not saved to the chapter (7.7, `apply.7`): Save first applies the boxes as accepted suggestions and saves, Keep for later keeps them for when the author comes back, and a box left as it opened is kept without a question. Another review of the chapter, from History or the review selector, asks nothing (`apply.26`): the boxes are kept, and go back on that review where their paragraph reads the same, the others set aside (C.53). Save to chapter with boxes whose text changed asks to apply them first, as accepted suggestions, and saves nothing if any cannot be applied; boxes left as they opened are closed without a question when something is written (6.3). Review with AI and Submit manual review ask the author to finish or cancel them first.

   **Open boxes are kept on disk** (`apply.16`, 11.3 j). Every box open in a chapter is written to that chapter's drafts file 700 ms after the last keystroke, and the file goes when the last box closes, so closing the app, an update or a crash loses nothing typed in a box. Leaving the chapter keeps them too (Keep for later, 7.7, `apply.7`); until then leaving asked to discard open boxes, and Discard emptied the drafts file. Whenever a review of the chapter comes on screen, each box goes back where its block still reads exactly as when the box opened, with a card when typed text came back ("Paragraph 12 is open for editing again, as you left it."). The card goes when another chapter or review comes on screen, or once every box it names is applied or closed (`apply.7` review). A box holding typed text that cannot go back is set aside as a .txt in the app's data folder, and its card's Show the text opens it. A drafts file that cannot be read when the chapter comes on screen (another program holding it open, say) is never written over unread: each write for that chapter reads it first and writes nothing while it still cannot be read, and once it can, the boxes it held are kept after those on screen and come back the next time the chapter opens (C.53).
3. **From a comment.** "Edit text" on every comment card, in the margin and in the sidebar, opens the comment's paragraph as in case 2, with the comment's quote selected when it can be found. This replaces the "Insert as suggestion" idea from the original Phase 5 list.

### 7.6 Read-only while a review runs (Phase 5d)

A review's result replaces the working set, so a decision made while it runs would vanish when it lands, and nothing used to stop the author making one. Now, while a review of the open chapter runs:

- the prose, the margin notes, the filter chips and the side panel are faded and `inert`, so no click, key or hover reaches them; the review-pane keys are ignored; Submit manual review, Bulk and Save to chapter are disabled; the store refuses every decision and edit for that chapter whatever the caller (`isLocked` in `state/store.ts`);
- a status card floats at the bottom of the manuscript pane, outside the scroller so it is always in view, with the progress label and Cancel review;
- other chapters stay open to read and edit. If the author has moved to another chapter when the result arrives, it is saved as that chapter's newest review and a card says so, instead of pulling the author back; opening the chapter shows it. If the chapter file changed on disk while the model worked, the result is rebased onto the text the chapter has now before it is shown.

The review dialog also warns when the page holds decisions the chapter file does not (`unsavedDecisionCount`): the model reads the file, and its result replaces the working set, so changes that were never saved would drop out of view (they stay in History) and be suggested again. Since `apply.20` its blue button is "Save N changes, then review", which saves and then starts, and starting without them takes "Review without them" and a question (5.5); until then the warning carried a Save to chapter button that left the dialog open.

### 7.5 New chapter, and manual reviews

Paste mode was one dialog doing two unrelated jobs. It is now two:

- **New chapter**, from a "+" beside the Chapters heading (the folder button next to it opens the manuscript-folder picker). File name, an extension radio (`.md` default, `.txt`), and a textarea for the text. The name is checked against the folder as it is typed and Create stays disabled while it is taken or unusable; the file is created through `files:create` (6.1), then the list refreshes and the new chapter opens.
- **Submit manual review**, from the toolbar. Paste a corrected version of the chapter that is open, or load it from a file, and the full-text diff (4.4) turns every difference into a suggestion. The review is saved to the reviews folder immediately, like a generated one, and carries `generator.kind: 'manual'`. When the page has changes the chapter file does not (`apply.20`, `buildManualReview` in `src/renderer/state/reviewStart.ts`), Build review asks "Replace the open review?" with three answers (`manualBuildPrompt`): Cancel, "Build without them", and the blue "Save N changes, then build", which is focused, so Enter never leaves them behind. Save then build runs Save to chapter (6.3) and builds only on `saved` or `nothing-to-save`, against the review on screen as it is then, so the text is compared with the saved chapter; a save that does not get there builds nothing and leaves the dialog open, with a red box between the pasted text and the buttons: "The review was not built, because the changes were not saved." and what the save's cards said, as in 5.5; where the pasted text was made from the chapter without those changes, the new review suggests putting their old words back, which the question says. Build without them compares with the file as it is and leaves them in the review from <date>, in History. When the open review has decisions but none the file lacks (all saved, or only dismissed), the older two-way confirmation still says that the working set is being replaced and that the old review stays in History (`replaceReviewPrompt`); with no decisions it builds at once. A dialog closed while the save runs builds nothing.

### 7.7 Leaving a chapter and closing the window

**Leaving** (`apply.7`, 11.3 i). A chapter clicked in the list, the chapter New chapter has just made, and another review of the open chapter, chosen in History or the review selector, each replace the review on screen, so each goes through `beforeLeaving` in `src/renderer/state/leave.ts` first. It lets a save under way end (`pendingSave()`, 6.3), then, for another chapter, asks `leavePrompt`'s question when the page holds changes the chapter file does not have, or paragraph boxes whose text changed:

- **The question.** "Leave <chapter>?". It says how many changes are not saved to the chapter and that they are kept in this review: the chapter opens with the review last on screen (6.4, `apply.26`), so they are where the author left them. Open paragraph boxes are kept as they were left and open again when the author comes back to the chapter (C.53).
- **Another review of the chapter asks nothing** (`apply.26`, the author's decision replacing the "Open the review from <date>?" question of `apply.7`'s review). Decisions live in each review and are written as they are made, the chapter opens with whichever review was on screen last, and open boxes are kept for the chapter and go back on the review that comes on screen where their paragraph reads the same, the others set aside as a .txt (C.53). Once it is on screen, the page tells main (`reviews:shown`), which records it as the review the chapter opens with. Changes not saved to the chapter in the review it replaces stay there, and the dot, Save and this question then follow the new one, so the selector's entry for the old one counts them ("2 not saved to chapter", section 7).
- **The answers.** Cancel, Keep for later (focused, so Enter keeps) and the blue "Save N changes first", or "Apply and save first" when a box changed. Save first runs `saveChapter` with the boxes applied without a second question and goes on only when the file then reads like the page (`saved` or `nothing-to-save`); a save that fails or is blocked stays, and its card says why. Keep for later goes on: the decisions are in the review and the boxes in the chapter's drafts file, both written before anything else is read, and the list's dot marks the chapter meanwhile (6.4). Cancel, Escape, or another question replacing this one stays.
- **No question** when nothing would be left unsaved, while a review of the chapter runs (the page is read-only and its decisions are written), or for what is already on screen: re-clicking the open chapter, whichever of its reviews is on screen (the chapter opens with that one, since it is the last on screen), or the review on screen in History, only writes what is waiting and reloads nothing, so the focus, the filters and the find bar stay as they were (`isOpenTarget`; it used to reload the review and reset them). It no longer reads the chapter file again either, so a change on disk that the watcher missed, on a synced or network folder say, shows once the author opens another chapter and comes back. Until `apply.26` the open chapter clicked from an older review opened the review last decided in and asked "Open the review from <date>?" (`openedBy`, now gone). Of several clicks made while a save runs or a question is open, only the last goes ahead.
- **The question holds the keyboard.** While it is open the rest of the window is inert, so Tab stays among its buttons and cannot reach Save to chapter, the chapter list or History behind it; when it closes, the focus goes back to where it was, a list row or a paragraph box, if that is still on the page (section 7, Confirmation).
- **New chapter** creates the file before asking, so Cancel leaves the new chapter in the list, unopened.

Before `apply.7`, leaving asked only about open boxes, as "Discard paragraph edits?", and Discard threw them away.

**Closing the window** (`apply.8`, main; `apply.9`, the page; 11.3 i). The page pushes what a close would put at risk whenever it changes (`CloseGuardState` over `app:guard-state`), and the close guard in main (`createCloseGuard` in `src/main/closeGuard.ts`; `src/main/ipc.ts` builds the app's one guard and `createWindow` attaches the window) decides at the window's `close` event from the last state it has, without asking the page then, since a page that has stopped responding could not answer (C.54):

- **At once, with no IPC:** a close Restart now has allowed (`allowNextClose('update')`, `release.8b`) or the page has agreed to; a close after the page's process has gone (`onRendererGone`, from the one `render-process-gone` handler); any close once Windows has said the session is ending; and a close with nothing at risk (`needsAttention`). A page that has pushed nothing since it loaded counts as nothing at risk; the page pushes as soon as it has loaded (`apply.9`), so this is only the moment before.
- **Otherwise the close is held.** The window is restored if minimised, shown and focused, and when it was not in front its taskbar button flashes too, until the close ends. A quiet test window (`dev.1`) is shown without the foreground, a minimised one is left minimised, and its button does not flash; the native box below is outside that switch and may raise the window and take the foreground (C.61). The page is sent `app:close-requested` once, with `quitting` when the whole app was asked to quit, and reports on `app:close-status`: `received` within 3 s, then `prompting` within 10 s of that, once its question (`closePrompt`, or its "Still writing your decisions") is on screen.
- **The native box.** When the page misses either time, or the author closes again before its question is up, main shows a box over the window (`unresponsiveDetail`, through `messageBoxOptions`): "<APP_NAME> is not responding" or "<APP_NAME> is still finishing", what the last state says is at risk, Wait and Close anyway. Wait is the default, and Escape answers Wait. Wait leaves the page its time and sets no new timer, so a page that is slow but working does not bring the box back every few seconds; another close opens it again at once. Close anyway destroys the window, which does not wait for the page, and quits the app when the close came from a quit. When the page answers after all (`received`, `prompting` or its decision), the box is taken away through its AbortSignal and whatever it was answered is ignored, since the page's own question is then on screen; `received` starts the 10 s. Once the question is up, a further close only brings the window forward. A box that fails to open counts as Wait.
- **The decision** (`app:close-decision`). True closes the window on the next tick, so the reply reaches the page first; when the close came from a quit it calls `app.quit()` again instead, since holding the close cancelled the quit, and that close goes through. False, or anything but a plain true, keeps the window and clears the quitting flag, so the next close asks afresh and a later plain close does not quit. A status or a decision with no close under way changes nothing, so the question Restart now asks of its own (`prepareToLeaveApp('update')`) cannot close the window, though false still clears the quitting flag.
- **A page that goes** during a close takes its box and timers with it, and the window closes, or the app quits, as the author asked, since nothing more can be saved; `onRendererGone` returns true then, so `hardening.4` offers no Reload or Quit for that window. A page that loads again, or pushes a state again, is asked again.
- **A reload is a new page.** A reload keeps the page's process, so no `render-process-gone` comes, but the document is new: the dev server's reload, Ctrl+R in development, `hardening.4`'s Reload window. When the main frame commits a new document (`did-navigate`), the guard forgets what the old page pushed, as it does for a new window, so a close before the new page pushes goes through at once. A close under way ends without closing: its box and timers go, the taskbar stops flashing, and the quit it came from is forgotten, as with the page's false. Its request went to the old document, which can no longer answer, and the reload came after the close, so the author is looking at the page they reloaded; the next close is asked of the new page. A late answer from the old page changes nothing, and an allowance stays, since it is the window's. Not `did-start-navigation`, which also fires for a navigation `will-navigate` then refuses, leaving the page where it was (C.54).
- **For Restart now** (`release.8b`, still to come): `allowNextClose('update')`, called before the installer quits the app (11.3 n), takes over a close under way; the function it returns takes the allowance back after an install that failed; and the allowance is used by one close, even one the page's own `beforeunload` then cancels.
- **Shutdown is never blocked** (C.55). `query-session-end` and `session-end` only send `app:flush`, for the page to write its waiting review and drafts writes at once, and neither is prevented. After `session-end` every close goes through at once and any box goes; after `query-session-end` alone a close is still asked about, since the session may yet go on.
- **One quitting flag**, the guard's: `before-quit` sets it (`onBeforeQuit`), the page's false clears it, and `isQuitting` reads it for `hardening.4`.
- The log gets `close.asked`, `close.unresponsive`, `close.box-answer`, `close.decided` and the like, with the stage, the answer and `quitting` only, never the chapter (11.3 m).

**The page's half** (`apply.9`): `src/renderer/state/closeGuard.ts`, which App installs with the IPC calls, the two writers, `saveChapter` and the review's Cancel (History as last read until `apply.26`, which needed it only to date an older review), so it needs no DOM and is tested in node.

- **The push.** `closeGuardStateOf` reads the store: the open chapter, `unsavedDecisionCount`, boxes whose text changed, a review of the chapter running (`locked`) or of any (`reviewRunning`), typed dialog text (`hasDialogText`), and `writesPending`, which counts a review or drafts write waiting, under way, held over a drafts file that cannot be read, or failed and not made since, and a Save to chapter under way. A write that failed used to leave nothing pending, so a close after it went at once; the writers now keep the value until a write for it succeeds (`failures`, `retry` in `src/renderer/state/persist.ts`). The state is pushed once the page has loaded and then whenever it changes, in the same task as the change: a store subscription, and `call` sends before its first await. So a close that comes while the page is still busy with the decision that changed it is already held. In a `dev:clean` window, one evaluation accepted a suggestion and kept the page busy for 1.5 s, WM_CLOSE posted during it; the close was held, asked about once the page was free, and the decision kept. With the push put off to a timer, the same steps closed at once and the review on disk lost the decision.
- **The request.** `app:close-requested` runs `prepareToLeaveApp('close')`, which says `received` at once. A question already open (the leave prompt, or "Apply paragraph edits first?" from Ctrl+S) is answered Cancel, so a save waiting on it ends rather than running out the clock. The save under way and both writers' flushes, failed writes queued again first, then race 5 s (`WRITES_WAIT_MS`). The page stays live meanwhile, and a writer holds a value that arrives during its write for its own 700 ms and lets the flush end without it, so while the store still counts a write or a Save to chapter after a round and none has failed, the page goes round again within the same 5 s, each round in a task of its own so the clock still runs. A dismissal, a comment or a Save pressed while the writes run is therefore on disk before the window goes, even with nothing to ask (a review fix the same day). Past that, or when a write failed, the page asks "Still writing your decisions" (`stillWritingPrompt`): why, in the words of the writers' cards, which sit under the dialog, what else closing would lose, then Wait, in Cancel's place and focused, so Enter and Escape give it, and Close anyway. Wait tries the writes again for another 5 s. Then `closePrompt`'s question, with `prompting` sent first, in the confirm dialog that leaving uses, saying the decisions are kept in this review, the one the chapter opens with (`apply.26`; until then it dated an older review by History, `olderReviewFrom`). Every layout of the page has that dialog, with the rest of the page inert under it: the Setup screen can show over a review with changes not saved, and a close from there used to leave the question in the store with nothing on screen, while main, told `prompting`, showed no box either. With nothing to ask, only writes having been waiting, it answers true at once.
- **The answers.** Cancel stays. Save and close, or Apply, save and close, runs `saveChapter` with the boxes applied; that save is the one under way, so it is timed and asked about as the writes are, and the window goes only on `saved` or `nothing-to-save`, a save that did not get there leaving the window open with its card. Close, keep for later, or Close anyway, stops a running review. Every answer that closes writes what is waiting once more, against the same 5 s, before answering true. Each run ends with `app:close-decision`: false on Cancel, on a save that did not get there, or when something threw, with a card saying so.
- **Restart now** (`release.9`) is to call `prepareToLeaveApp('update')`, which asks the same question and resolves true to go on. It reports no status, since no close is under way, and its decision changes nothing in main but the quitting flag. A call while a run is under way shares its answer, and a close request meanwhile is told `received`, and `prompting` when the question is up.
- **Shutdown.** `app:flush` queues failed writes again and flushes both writers, asking nothing (C.55).

## 9. Risks and things the implementer must verify

1. **Structured output and `maxTurns`.** The Agent SDK may satisfy `outputFormat` through an internal tool call and retries. If `maxTurns: 1` prevents a valid result, raise it to 3 and keep tools removed.
2. **Removing tools.** `Options` has `allowedTools` and `disallowedTools`; check `node_modules/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk/sdk.d.ts` for a top-level `tools` option and for the current built-in tool names before hardcoding the list. The call must never be able to read or write the vault.
3. **SDK inside Electron main.** _Superseded by C.42: there is no SDK; the app spawns the user's `claude` itself._ The SDK spawns the bundled native binary, so Node on PATH is not required. Verify in `npm run dev`. If it spawns through the JS runtime instead, set `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` in `options.env` or pass `pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` pointing at a native `claude.exe`. Never point it at the npm `claude.cmd` shim.
4. **Packaging.** _Superseded by C.42: nothing of Anthropic's is packaged._ The native binary had to be unpacked from asar (`asarUnpack`).
5. **Model availability by plan.** Populate the model list from `q.supportedModels()`; do not assume `opus` exists on every plan.
6. **Refusal fallbacks.** Opus 5 can fall back to Opus 4.8 automatically when a safety classifier flags content, and some categories refuse with no fallback. Fiction with dark themes may trigger this occasionally. Show the explanation and record the actual model from `modelUsage`.
7. **Obsidian concurrent edits.** Detection is solid; prevention is not possible. Document the workflow.
8. **Policy.** The app is given away as open source and runs Claude Code. What keeps that within Anthropic's published terms as far as they can be read (sources and dates in Appendix A; Appendix D 8 and 21, C.42):
   - Each user installs, updates and signs in to their own, unmodified Claude Code, with Anthropic's installer and Anthropic's sign-in flow, on their own Claude plan or API key. Their usage is billed to them, and the app pays for, resells or intermediates none of it.
   - The app ships nothing of Anthropic's, neither binary nor library (`release.12` checks the package). It never reads, stores, displays or proxies Claude credentials. An `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` already in the user's environment reaches Claude Code only through the inherited environment, unread and unchanged (`buildClaudeEnv`). It never starts a sign-in; Setup only shows commands for the user to run (CLAUDE.md rule 2 (a)).
   - It never sets or overrides Claude Code's client or entrypoint identity. The Usage Policy that takes effect on 2026-11-12 bars services that "misrepresent the product or client being used". `buildClaudeEnv` adds one variable, `CLAUDE_CODE_STARTUP_FAILURE_RESULTS`, and `test/main/claudeOptions.test.ts` pins that (CLAUDE.md rule 6).
   - The author has not accepted the Commercial Terms (Appendix D 8). The legal page asks for them when Claude Code runs "in your products or services", and also says it does not prevent an end user signing in to the unmodified binary with their own subscription. No Anthropic page says which side of that line an app like this one falls on.
   - Anthropic may enforce its usage restrictions without prior notice, and acted against third-party tools in 2026 (Appendix A). If that happens to this app, Claude reviews may stop counting against the plan and be billed as extra usage, as third-party tools were in April 2026, or may fail. The OpenAI provider still works, and chapters and saved reviews are not affected.
   - The README carries `DISCLAIMER` (an independent project that runs Claude Code; not made, endorsed or sponsored by Anthropic or OpenAI; no Anthropic software) and `AI_OUTPUT_NOTICE`, both from `src/shared/branding.ts` and checked by `test/branding.test.ts`.
   - No Claude, Anthropic or OpenAI marks in the app's name or logo (`test/branding.test.ts` checks the name; the Seam logo has no lettering). Claude Code is named only in plain text, as the tool the app runs.

## 10. Guidelines file

`resources/GUIDELINES.default.md` is the author-editable "CLAUDE.md for edits". It is copied to `<manuscript>/GUIDELINES.md` on first use and sent verbatim after the static instructions. The author fills in the style sheet section (spelling variant, Oxford comma, character names, invented terms).

`ensureGuidelinesFile` creates it with `COPYFILE_EXCL`, so an existing file is never touched, and only a .md or .txt path is accepted. Review with AI and Settings open it two ways (`setup.10b`). **Open in Obsidian** hands the system `obsidian://open?path=<full path>`, built by the pure `obsidianOpenUrl` in `src/shared/obsidian.ts` with every character but `A-Z a-z 0-9 -._~` percent-encoded as UTF-8; Obsidian finds the most specific vault it knows that holds the path, so the link names no vault and the manuscript folder can be a vault or any folder in one, and for a folder in no vault it knows, Obsidian says it cannot find a vault for the link. Main gives the shell only a link `isObsidianOpenUrl` accepts and refuses a `.txt` file, which Obsidian does not show, before anything is created (`guidelines:open` is `openGuidelines`, with `assertCanOpen` and `openInObsidian`, in `src/main/openGuidelines.ts`; Appendix C "Open in Obsidian names no vault"). **Open** goes to the system's app for the file type, and when there is none shows the file in its folder and says so (`openGuidelinesFile`). The read-only `guidelines:status` reports `{path, exists, styleSheet}`, where `styleSheetStatus` calls the section `blank` while it holds only the template's labels and placeholders, `filled` once any entry has a value, and `missing` when there is no "## Style sheet" heading (`setup.10`).

## 11. Release 1.0: Lapidary

Written 2026-10-14 for implementing agents who were not part of the release discussion. It turns the 21 ideas agreed that day, plus one from the same day's round 6 feedback (small changes, 11.7), into milestones M0 to M12. Two answers the same day changed it: the name is Lapidary, not Igneous (Appendix D 1), and the app ships no Claude Code binary and the author accepts no Anthropic terms, so each user installs Claude Code themselves (Appendix D 8). Sections 0 to 10 still describe the app; a task that changes one of them edits that section in the same commit. Task ids such as `brand.4` and `apply.9` are stable: cite them in commit messages and tick them here. A task that absorbed others names them, and the absorbed ids are never done separately.

Rules for every task in this section:

- **Order.** Milestones run in order unless marked "can overlap". Inside a milestone, follow the listed order.
- **Hot files, one task at a time.** `src/main/index.ts`, `src/main/ipc.ts`, `src/shared/ipc.ts`, `src/main/providers/claude.ts`, `src/renderer/App.tsx`, `src/renderer/state/store.ts`, `src/renderer/components/ReviewDialog.tsx`, `src/renderer/components/SettingsDialog.tsx`, `src/renderer/styles/app.css`, `electron-builder.config.cjs` and `package.json` each receive edits from four to six workstreams. Tasks touching them run one after another, never as parallel agents.
- **Rule 7 and numbering.** New Appendix C entries take the next free number at commit time (C.38 was next on 2026-10-14) and are cited by title, never by a pre-assigned number. Once `release.6a` lands, every user-visible change also adds a line under `## [Unreleased]` in `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Baseline.** On 2026-10-14 the working tree passed 364 tests in 26 files, typecheck, lint and format check. Counts only go up.
- **Safe verification.** Real-window runs use `npm run dev:clean` once `setup.15` exists, with `--quiet` since `dev.1` so the window stays out of the author's way (Appendix C "Quiet test windows"); before that, `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_USER_DATA=.dev-userdata REMOTE_DEBUGGING_PORT=9222 npm run dev` (C.8, C.14). Saves go to a copy at `.dev-userdata/vault/`, never to the tracked `samples/`. Packaged runs use `dist/win-unpacked/Lapidary.exe --isolated-user-data=<scratch dir>` only. CDP key and wheel events skip Electron's input hooks (C.35); native close and file drags need real OS input.
- **Never, as an agent:** read `~/.claude/.credentials.json` or any `secrets.json`; run against the author's real settings; create Windows accounts, type credentials or sign in; click through SmartScreen; change OS or security settings; publish a release, change repo visibility or settings, force-push, or send a message. Those steps are marked **(author)**.
- **Commits.** Commit and push only as the author decided in `release.1`. Otherwise leave each milestone in the working tree with a proposed message.
- **Author sittings.** The author's hands-on steps are grouped: **A** (M0) decisions and the logo; **B** (M4) approvals to publish the repo; **C** (after M7) one UI session: theme contact sheet, scroll A/B, folder picker, the Obsidian save check, prompt wording; **D** (M11) the release session on a clean Windows account, then the 1.0.1 update proof.

### 11.0 Goal and scope

**Done** means a public, MIT-licensed repo `MichaelBradetLegris/lapidary` whose CI built and published `v1.0.0` as a GitHub Release, and on a clean Windows account: the friend downloads one installer, gets past the browser and SmartScreen warnings by following the README, installs Claude Code and signs in by following the Setup screen alone, reviews and saves a chapter, and receives `v1.0.1` through the in-app updater. The app is called Lapidary, has its own logo, dark mode, an unmistakable Save to chapter, a backup before every save, a close prompt, guards against encoding and Obsidian-syntax damage, a log file and Copy diagnostics.

**For** two people: the author (Windows 11, Claude Max) and a friend who writes novels in English in Obsidian, OS unknown. Windows first; macOS and Linux only if `files.17` says he needs them.

**Out of scope for 1.0, and cuts** (with the reason, so nobody rebuilds them):

- Automatic save to chapter: Save stays the one explicit write (CLAUDE.md rule 1), and background writes would race Obsidian's autosave and sync.
- In-app restore or Compare of a backup: restore is by hand; Compare is a follow-up that must never become the chapter's current review.
- Portable exe: re-extracts about 600 MB into `%TEMP%` per launch and cannot update. Local tests use `dist/win-unpacked`.
- Code signing unless Smart App Control blocks the friend (`release.14`, costs money). macOS and Linux builds unless he needs them.
- Electron fuses (from `hardening.9`), build-provenance attestation, a custom title bar, Windows forced colours, the version viewer (section 8 Phase 6).
- An in-app sign-in launcher: the legal page bars offering Claude login. Setup shows a command to copy.
- Bundling Claude Code (author decision, Appendix D 8): the installer contains no `claude.exe` and the author accepts no Anthropic terms. Each user installs Claude Code with Anthropic's own installer and signs in with their own account. The SDK's JavaScript library goes too (11.6 question 21): the app runs the user's `claude` command directly (11.3 q, `cli.1`).
- Cut tasks: `setup.3`'s `claudeExecutable` setting and newest-wins choice (untested SDK/CLI pairs); `setup.16` expiry warning (the CLI may never emit it on the SDK channel); `theme.11` (covered by `hardening.3a`); `hardening.14` (superseded by `files.4`); `release.15` (folded into `setup.3`); `hardening.7`'s domain allowlist, link-scan test, IPC sender check and permission handlers (no remote content; `opensource.12` covers navigation); `hardening.4`'s rescue plan (writers already flush); a second About surface; the `theme.3`/`theme.4` zero-pixel gate (the CSS test and contact sheet stay); `files.7`'s pair and line combination rules and subset property (the touching rule stays; a joint case needs two model edits that combine, visible in the ghost text first); `brand.8` and the old `release.1` repo rename and history rewrite (moot under a fresh repo); portable-specific Setup wording and smoke tests; a custom NSIS uninstall macro for the updater cache (documented instead).
- `apply.18` (a tracked Claude Code spawner) becomes measure-first: `sdk.mjs` spawns without `detached`, so libuv's kill-on-close job object should end `claude.exe` with the app. Build the tracker only if the measurement finds a leftover process. Measured on 2026-10-17 against the user's own `claude.exe`, which replaced `sdk.mjs` in C.42: nothing was left, so there is no tracker (C.56).

### 11.1 Decisions

| Decision | Choice | Why |
| -------- | ------ | --- |
| Name | Lapidary, written only as `APP_NAME` in `src/shared/branding.ts`; a case-insensitive guard test enforces it | Lapidary work cuts and polishes stone, obsidian included, and lapidary prose is polished and concise. Igneous was dropped because two Markdown apps for Obsidian users took it in October 2026 (Appendix D 1). Renames stay one edit (C.21) |
| Frozen identifiers | `APP_ID` `dev.bradetlegris.manuscript-review`; package name `manuscript-review`; `APP_DATA_DIR` `ai-grammar-checker`; `APP_REPOSITORY` `MichaelBradetLegris/lapidary`. Never change after 1.0 | The NSIS uninstall GUID and AUMID derive from appId; the install folder `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\manuscript-review` and updater cache `%LOCALAPPDATA%\manuscript-review-updater` from the package name (`app-builder-lib` `targetUtil.js`, `appInfo.js`); every installed `app-update.yml` holds the slug. A package name unlike `APP_DATA_DIR` keeps `--delete-app-data` away from the data folder |
| Logo | Seam, the original pebble with its rim, ghost line, line of type and dotted underline, in the turquoise palette of 11.3 (o) (Appendix D 2); the ember palette is the alternative | No letterform, not purple, clearly unlike Obsidian's tall faceted crystal, visible on a dark taskbar |
| Repository | A fresh public `lapidary` repo, created when the author asks (Appendix D 3), one root commit by the GitHub noreply address; the private `ai-grammar-checker` is archived; branch `main`, remote `origin` | Old commits carry the gmail and a profile path, and `wip` does not build |
| Licence | MIT, "Copyright (c) 2026 Michael Bradet-Legris" | Short, compatible with everything shipped |
| Anthropic terms | None accepted (author decision, Appendix D 8). The app ships no Claude Code binary; each user installs Claude Code and signs in with their own account or key | The author will not bind himself to Anthropic's Commercial Terms for a free tool a handful of people use. Appendix A has the research. The SDK library is replaced by direct calls to the user's `claude` (11.3 q) |
| Claude wording | Provider label "Claude"; plain text says it runs the Claude Code the user installed, signed in with their own account or `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; `DISCLAIMER` and `AI_OUTPUT_NOTICE` in the README; no control starts a login | The legal page's section on products that run Claude Code allows a plain-text statement that a product runs Claude Code, and no Claude Code or Anthropic name or logo in its own name or logo; it also bars offering Claude login (Appendix A). The SDK branding guidance is for SDK integrations, which the app no longer is |
| Claude binary | The user's own installed Claude Code, run directly as a command (11.3 q); no Anthropic package is a dependency or ships. The installed version must be at least `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE`, the version the flags were verified against, else Setup says to run `claude update` | Matches the author's decisions (Appendix D 8 and 21). Claude Code updates itself, so new models need no app release. The author's own copy had stalled at 2.1.266 on 2026-10-14, so the version check matters |
| Launch check | `claude auth status` plus the `initialize` handshake, which sends no prompt (an SDK handshake until `cli.1`); failures from real runs outrank "ready" | Today it says ready when signed out and spends two model requests per launch |
| Default model | `default` for new settings, passed as no `--model` flag; a Pro account gets Sonnet preselected; existing `opus` kept; Pro accounts see a limits note; `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` removed from the child env | `default` is Opus 5.5 on Pro since CLI 2.1.280 (model-config docs), so the note does the real work |
| Dev switches | Read only in `src/main/devSwitches.ts`; dev builds only; packaged builds accept `--isolated-user-data=<dir>` | A shell-profile variable must never redirect the stored OpenAI key |
| Theme | `nativeTheme.themeSource` from a `theme` setting; CSS tokens swap under `prefers-color-scheme: dark`; no IPC | Electron 44 drives the media query and the Windows title bar (DWM attribute measured) live; the CSP forbids a pre-paint script |
| Scroll bolding | Opaque token backgrounds on every scroller (C.48) | Measured: only an opaque scroller gets its own LCD-text layer |
| Unsaved signal | `unsavedDecisionCount` on the button ("Save 3 changes to chapter"), status bar and a dot before the chapter name; `store.dirty` removed | One exact measure; "Review saved" read as "chapter saved" |
| Leaving and closing | Save / Keep for later / Cancel, Keep for later focused (closing with nothing to keep offers Close anyway / Cancel, Cancel focused); a main-driven close handshake with 3 s and 10 s native fallbacks; shutdown never blocked | Nothing is discarded; a hung renderer cannot trap the window |
| Drafts | Open paragraph boxes persist under `<userData>/drafts` and come back, or are set aside as .txt | Updates, crashes and power loss skip every prompt |
| Backups | Before a Save that changes bytes, copy the old bytes to `<userData>/backups/`; keep newest 20 plus the first of each day for 30 days; no backup, no save | The friend may not use git; userData is never synced or indexed |
| Ctrl+S | Runs Save to chapter, except behind a modal, on repeat, in a paragraph box (card: press Done first), while the suggestion editor is open (card: press its Save first) or while locked | Reflex key; gating stops it cancelling prompts or saving without words still being typed |
| Encoding | Refuse non-UTF-8 files with advice per class; 4 MiB cap | Today bad bytes become U+FFFD, which Save would write |
| Obsidian syntax | App-owned prompt rule plus a pure guard that keeps out untouched model suggestions touching protected syntax (`keptOut`) | Deterministic and testable; compose unchanged |
| Reviews and files | A review belongs to its `sourcePath`; moved or renamed chapters adopt reviews by strict rules; `files:save` writes only chapters in the manuscript folder or opened this session | Today same-named chapters share reviews and Save can write the wrong file |
| Logging | Hand-rolled 1 MiB rotating log, scalar fields only, errors through one policy | No dependency; content cannot leak through structure |
| Installer | One-click per-user NSIS x64, `${productName}-Setup-${version}.exe`; `deleteAppDataOnUninstall` false | No UAC ever; it is exactly what the updater re-runs |
| Signing | Unsigned 1.0; Azure Artifact Signing only if Smart App Control blocks the friend (**about US$9.99/month, ongoing**) | SmartScreen warns at two-user scale even when signed; SignPath Foundation excludes proprietary components |
| Updates | electron-updater 6.8.10 and electron-builder 26.17.0 pinned (v26 line); GitHub provider, no token; check 15 s after launch and daily (toggle); download on consent; install only by Restart now with a visible installer; notify-only off Windows | Silent install-on-quit can half-install; 6.8.10 fixes a stale-blockmap bug |
| Versioning | SemVer from package.json; tags `vX.Y.Z`; 0.1.0 goes to 1.0.0; Keep a Changelog 1.1.0 | One number drives notes, About and the updater |
| CI | Check job on windows-latest and ubuntu-latest; Windows package job; draft release on `v*` tags with SHA-pinned actions and no cache | Free on a public repo |
| OpenAI | Labelled experimental; default never a -pro, -codex, -deep-research or -chat model; real-key check only if the author already has credit (**about US$5 top-up**) | Only ever tested against the mock |
| Telemetry | The app sends no analytics; Claude Code's own telemetry is documented with its opt-outs, not switched off | `DISABLE_TELEMETRY` also disables feature flags |
| Security | Single-instance lock, navigation and file-drop guard, CSP additions, IPC path guards; no fuses | Proportionate for a local app with no remote content |
| Electron | Latest 44.x before the cut (44.7.2 on 2026-10-14) | Current Chromium fixes for the first install |
| macOS, Linux | Conditional. macOS arm64 ad-hoc signed, notify-only updates; Apple Developer Program (**US$99/year**) only if the author wants auto-updates there | Notarization costs money; no Anthropic binary ships either way |

- [x] `files.21` **Comments and labels follow their paragraphs through a save** (done 2026-10-15; C.44) (reported by the author 2026-10-14). Pure and store code; may land any time after M2.
  - **Cause.** `markSaved` in `src/renderer/state/store.ts` folds the decisions into a new base with `commitDecisions`, which moves each hunk's offsets. It leaves every comment's `paragraph` and every hunk's `paragraph` label unchanged.
  - **Effect.** A save that adds, joins or deletes a paragraph shifts every later comment by one; the usual case is a split made with the paragraph pen. The author split 42-43 after a paste from LibreOffice Writer into Obsidian had lost line breaks. His comments marked 63 and 77 then quoted text that sits in 64 and 78. Changes made in Obsidian are not affected: `rebaseReview` already remaps comments, by the paragraph's first 40 characters and then by the quote.
  - **Fix.** A pure `remapAfterCommit(review, committed)` in `src/shared/review.ts` maps each old paragraph's start offset through the committed hunks. A comment follows its paragraph, or, when that paragraph was split, the part that contains its `quote`. Hunk `paragraph` labels are recomputed from their new offsets with `paragraphIndexAt`. `markSaved` uses it.
  - **Repair.** A load-time `repairCommentAnchors`, run by `upgradeLoadedReview`, moves a comment whose `quote` is missing from its paragraph to the nearest paragraph that contains it. Reviews already shifted, such as the author's current chapters, then correct themselves.
  - **Records.** PLAN 3 and 6.3; Appendix C "Comments follow their paragraphs through a save".
  - **Verify.** `test/shared/review.test.ts`: split, join and delete a paragraph through accepted manual hunks and save; every comment and hunk label then points at the paragraph holding its quote. A fast-check property over random paragraph splits and joins. The repair moves a shifted comment and leaves a correct one untouched. Real window: split a paragraph with the pen and Save; the margin notes further down stay beside their quotes.

Verify: `test/renderer/persist.test.ts` with fake timers; real window: accept then switch chapter in one CDP evaluation, and the first chapter's review JSON holds the decision.

What the text left open, or differs: boxes belong to the chapter, not to a review, and go back on whichever review of it comes on screen (a chapter opened, a History entry, a new review), by the page's text alone; `reviewId` is recorded but not compared, since Done applies a box against the page whatever decisions made it (C.53). "Its paragraph" is its own index: a box whose paragraph moved because one was added above is set aside, not guessed onto text that reads the same. Boxes left as they opened are kept too, come back without a card, and are dropped when they cannot go back. A box that could not be set aside stays in the chapter's file, after the boxes on screen, and is tried again at the next open, so the file may hold two boxes with one key. A drafts file that cannot be read is moved aside as `<hash>.unreadable-<time>.json` rather than written over. Show the text opens the file itself: `shell:reveal` `drafts` takes the set-aside file's bare `name`, checked to be a .txt in `drafts/set-aside`, and opens it in the app set for .txt, or shows it in its folder when there is none; a card carries the name as `actionArg`. Boxes are flushed on leave (a chapter or History entry opening); the close and session-end flushes are `apply.8` and `apply.9`'s, through the exported `flushDraftWrites`, and `draftWritesPending` in the store is there for their `writesPending`. `dialogText` is a record per dialog, read by `hasDialogText`: New chapter with a name or text typed, Submit manual review with any text, the suggestion editor while its words differ from what it opened with. `DraftIo` is filled by App, so `state/drafts.ts` needs no preload bridge and is tested in node. Until `apply.7`, leaving still asks to discard open boxes, and Discard deletes the kept file too; the flush on leave writes that deletion. In the window: " And she was tired of knowing." typed into paragraph 6, the file held it 1 s later, Chapter 02 clicked, "Discard paragraph edits?", Discard: the file was gone. So in this build a box is kept through a close, an update or a crash; 7.4, 11.3 (j), the module's comment and the CHANGELOG line now say so, and `apply.7`'s text says its CHANGELOG line covers leaving. (2) A drafts file that could not be read was written over by the next box typed in the chapter. In the window: that box kept, the app stopped, the file held open with no sharing by a PowerShell `FileStream`, the app started and Chapter 01 opened: no box and the card "could not be read back", whose reason was `toAppError`'s words for a refused write ("Windows would not let Lapidary replace ... save again"); the file let go, " He did not stop." typed into paragraph 3: 1 s later the file held paragraph 3's box alone, and paragraph 6's text was lost. Now `state/drafts.ts` keeps `unread` (by chapter, with the reason last shown) and `held`: every write for such a chapter reads the file first (`save`, `readUnread`); while it still cannot be read nothing is written, the write is held, `pending()` and `draftWritesPending` stay true, and the next flush, the one on leave or before a read back included, tries it again unless a newer write is waiting; a restore whose flush leaves a write held does not read the file back, since the held boxes are no longer on screen and placing what the file holds would have dropped them. Once it can be read, its boxes are carried after those on screen, and the card says they open for editing again the next time the author comes back to the chapter; they are not put on the page at once (C.53). The card about the failure says that nothing is written over them and that what is typed is kept only once they can be read; it is shown once per reason, not once per pause in typing, and goes when a later read succeeds. The writer now adds carried boxes when it writes, not when the write is scheduled. Main's `loadDrafts` gives a read it cannot make words of its own ("another program may have the file open, or Lapidary may not be allowed to read it (EBUSY)", or "the file could not be opened (EISDIR)"), with no path, and logs `drafts.read-failed` with the code. The same window after the fix: the card "... could not be read back: another program may have the file open, or Lapidary may not be allowed to read it (EBUSY). Nothing is written over them, so what you type in this chapter is kept only once they can be read."; typed into paragraph 3 while the file was held: nothing written; let go, the file still held paragraph 6 alone; " Not once." typed: 1 s later it held paragraphs 3 and 6, and the card "... could be read back after all. ..."; Chapter 02, Discard: the file held paragraph 6; Chapter 01 again: its box back with "Paragraph 6 is open for editing again, as you left it." The review suggested retrying at the next restore or flush; each debounced write retries too, so what is typed is kept as soon as the file can be read. Its other suggestion, moving the file aside in main, was not taken: a file held open without sharing cannot be renamed on Windows either. (3) Nothing tested the flush before the read back: with that line removed all 17 renderer tests passed, and a box cancelled less than 700 ms before a new review of the chapter came on screen without a leave reopened. A test now cancels a box and shows a new review 600 ms later; it fails without the flush. Mutations, each applied alone and the file put back byte for byte (sha256 checked), each made renderer tests fail: removing the flush before the read back (2), writing over a file not read (4), dropping a write that could not be made instead of holding it (2), flush not trying held writes (2), not carrying what a late read finds (4), not marking a failed restore's file unread (5), the card again at every failed read (1), the store flag ignoring held writes (1), a restore reading back while a write is held (1), a later read leaving the old card (1); main's raw read error back made both new main tests fail.)

Its `claude-agent-sdk` filter cannot match since C.42, when the app began running the user's own `claude.exe`, so each check also looked for every process the run had started, by process id and start time, for their children, and for any new `claude.exe` from `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin`: there were none at the first check, nor later. CHANGELOG Fixed line. (done 2026-10-17; 6.1 describes it. `toAppError(err, {operation, name, platform})` picks `explainReadError` or `explainWriteError`, and `readFailure(err, name)` turns a file system error into an `AppFailure` carrying the read words, so the bridge's write default never reaches it; a refusal already in words (`not-utf8`, too large) and an error that is not the system's (a JSON `SyntaxError`, Node's own `ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE`) pass through as they are. Used in `readChapter` (`files:read`, and the read after a `stale` save), `readForSave` in `writeChapter`, `loadReviewFile`, `readGuidelines` and `guidelinesStatus` (after its ENOENT check), `loadDrafts` and `chapterBackups`. `loadDrafts` drops its own words, since the shared ones serve: the card now reads "... could not be read back: Windows would not let Lapidary read the file they are kept in. Another program may have it open, ... and try again. Lapidary has not changed anything. Nothing is written over them, ..." with no path, and the log line keeps the code. Differences from the text: (1) the chapter list had nothing to reword: `listChapters` skips any folder it cannot read, the manuscript folder included, the page shows a failed list as empty, and its marks (`reviews:index`) skip review files they cannot read, so it is unchanged; a manuscript folder that cannot be read still shows "No .md or .txt files in that folder.", and saying why is a renderer change left to `apply.25` (a). (3) Show backups reads a folder too, and uses it. (4) Besides EBUSY, EPERM and EACCES, a read gets words for ENOENT, ENOTDIR and EISDIR, and general words with the code for any other (EIO, or UNKNOWN for a cloud file Windows could not fetch); a write keeps the raw message for those, as before. (5) The review a chapter opens with is not covered, though 6.1 first said it was: `readEntry` in `reviews.ts` skips a review file it cannot read for any reason, so in a fresh run `reviews:load-latest` opens the chapter on an older review or a blank one; when the entry is cached, `loadReviewFile` does throw the read words, but `openChapter` in `App.tsx` drops any failure of `reviews:load-latest` and opens a blank review. No card says so either way, a decision made there starts a new current review over the one that could not be read, and History leaves that file out the same way. Left to `apply.25` (c).
