### 11.3 Design notes

**(a) Startup order in `src/main/index.ts`.** Module scope: (1) `readDevSwitches({env: process.env, packaged: app.isPackaged, isolatedUserData: app.commandLine.getSwitchValue('isolated-user-data') or null})`; (2) `app.setPath('userData', switches.userDataDir ?? path.join(app.getPath('appData'), APP_DATA_DIR))`, then `chromiumSwitches(switches)` onto `app.commandLine` (only a quiet test window has one, `dev.1`); (3) `if (!app.requestSingleInstanceLock()) app.quit(); else start();` and everything below runs inside `start()`, so a losing instance writes nothing; (4) `initLog(<userData>/logs)`; (5) `installCrashHandlers`; (6) packaged win32 only: `app.setAppUserModelId(APP_ID)`. `whenReady` (async): `Menu.setApplicationMenu(null)`; `registerCoreHandlers()` with every handler, including updates and the close guard (`handle()` only fills a Map and `installIpcBridge()` wires it once, so a later registration is silently dead); `installIpcBridge()`; `const settings = await loadSettings(); applyTheme(settings.theme); installTheme();`; `createWindow()` (themed `backgroundColor`, close guard, navigation guard, the one `render-process-gone` handler, window-open handler); `startUpdates()`. Events: `second-instance` restores and focuses (`bringForward`; a quiet test window is shown without the foreground, and left minimised when it is, `dev.1`); `before-quit` calls `closeGuard.onBeforeQuit()` and awaits nothing; `will-quit` calls `abortAll()` and `stopWatching()`; `window-all-closed` calls `app.quit()` off macOS.

**(b) Developer switches** (all read in `src/main/devSwitches.ts`; packaged builds ignore and log every one except the flag): `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_USER_DATA` (userData), `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_OPENAI_BASE_URL` (was `STET_OPENAI_BASE_URL`; http or https only), `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_DEV_NO_CLAUDE_CODE` (behave as if Claude Code were not installed; was `..._DEV_NO_BUNDLED_CLI` before C.42), `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_DEV_FAKE_UPDATE` (simulate an update to that version), `MREV_VERBOSE` (echo Claude Code stderr), `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_DEV_QUIET_WINDOW` (a quiet test window: shown without taking the foreground and kept rendering while covered; `dev:clean --quiet`; `dev.1`, Appendix C "Quiet test windows"), `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_DEV_SHELL_STUB` (open nothing outside the app: every link, file and folder main would hand the system goes through `systemShell()`, which under the switch prints "[shell stub] ..." to the terminal instead, and main prints "[shell stub] on" as it starts; `dev:clean --shell-stub`; `setup.10b`, Appendix C "A shell that opens nothing"). `--isolated-user-data=<dir>` works in both builds and wins. The neutral prefix never changes with the name.

**(c) Claude child environment and options.** `buildClaudeEnv(base)` copies `base`, then removes `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` (the switch behind `--bare`) and the switches in (b), and adds `CLAUDE_CODE_STARTUP_FAILURE_RESULTS = '1'`. It sets no SDK client variable, because the app is not the SDK (C.42). It never sets `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER`: Claude Code is the user's own install and keeps itself current. Everything else passes unchanged, including `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_MODEL`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_*`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `DISABLE_TELEMETRY`, `DISABLE_ERROR_REPORTING` and proxy variables. No sign-in method is removed. `claudeArgs` (in `src/main/providers/claudeOptions.ts`, built on `BASE_CLAUDE_ARGS`) keeps the same guarantees as flags: no built-in tools (`--tools ""`), no MCP servers (`--strict-mcp-config`), no setting sources (`--setting-sources ""`), no session persistence, `--permission-mode dontAsk`, and `cwd` = userData (rule 6). Never `--bare`: it disables subscription sign-in. Because `settingSources` is empty, `apiKeyHelper` and env blocks in `~/.claude/settings.json` are not read; the README says to set the variable instead.

**(d) Settings.** Keys added in 1.0, all normalised in `src/shared/settings.ts` with defaults: `theme` (`system`; anything else not in system/light/dark becomes `system`), `onboardingDone` (missing: true when `manuscriptFolder` is set, else false; while false the welcome guide shows, unless Start or Skip closed it earlier in the run, and Start, Skip and Settings' Show the welcome guide again write it, section 7, `setup.11`), `dismissedHints` (`[]`, unique strings; the ids in use are `pro-model-preselected`, `setup.1`, also written by Show the welcome guide again so the guide brought back never preselects (C.67), and `save-to-chapter`, the first-use Save hint of `apply.12`, section 7), `checkForUpdates` (true), `updateDownloadConsent` (null or a version string), `sideSplit` (null, the usual split; else the side panel list's share of the height under the focused suggestion's box, a number strictly between 0 and 1, anything else becoming null; `ui.6`, section 7 The split); `model` default becomes `default`, existing values kept. Read failures in `settingsStore.ts`: ENOENT gives cached defaults; a JSON parse failure first copies the file to `settings.json.corrupt-<yyyymmddhhmmss>` with `COPYFILE_EXCL`, logs `settings.unreadable`, then caches defaults; any other read error retries once after 200 ms, then returns defaults without caching, and `save` re-tries the read and refuses to overwrite.

**(e) Theme.** Main sets `nativeTheme.themeSource` before the window exists; CSS overrides every colour token inside `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { :root { color-scheme: dark; ... } }`. The window is hidden until first paint, load or 3 s (C.36), and its `backgroundColor` comes from `WINDOW_BACKGROUND`, which the test pins to `--bg`, so no light frame shows. Light tokens added (values equal today's literals): `--accent-fill #3b5bdb`, `--on-accent #ffffff`, `--focus-bg #fff3bf`, `--para-active-bg #fff8dd`, `--para-flash-bg #fff6c9`, `--selected-bg #e7ecff`, `--badge-pending-bg #fff3bf`, `--badge-pending-border #f0dfa0`, `--badge-pending-ink #6b5600`, `--active-ring #f2dd93`, `--find-bg #ffe9a8`, `--find-active-bg #ffb64d`, `--find-active-ink var(--ink)`, `--warn-bg #fdf8ec`, `--warn-bg-strong #fdf7e2`, `--warn-border #e5d49a`, `--danger-bg #fdeceb`, `--editor-bg #fbfaff`, `--shadow-rgb 35 32 28` (shadows become `rgb(var(--shadow-rgb) / N%)`), `--backdrop rgb(35 32 28 / 42%)`, `--modal-shadow rgb(0 0 0 / 22%)`, `--hover-overlay rgb(0 0 0 / 8%)`, `--tint-pending 9%`, `--tint-hover 18%`, `--tint-decided-hover 16%`, and since `small.6` `--tint-small 16%` and `--tint-small-hover 26%` for a small change's capsule (11.7); editor glows become `color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent-fill) 12% or 24%, transparent)`. `--accent` is text, links and small marks (a commented paragraph's number, the information card's edge, the spinner, the open editor's border, the "you" chip; the unsaved dot was one until `apply.4` gave it `--unsaved`); `--accent-fill` is filled buttons (`button.primary`, the active Setup tab) and the editor glow, with `--on-accent` on them. Delete `--cat-typo`, `--cat-agreement`, `--cat-tense`, `--cat-redundancy`, `--cat-awkward`, `--cat-formatting`. Dark starting values: `--bg #1c1a17`, `--panel #24221e`, `--panel-alt #2d2a25`, `--border #3b3731`, `--border-strong #575148`, `--ink #ebe6dc`, `--ink-soft #bdb5a8`, `--ink-faint #968e82`, `--accent #9db1ff`, `--accent-fill #4263eb`, `--accent-hover #3653d6`, `--accent-active #2c45b8`, `--on-accent #ffffff`, `--accept #6cc28b`, `--dismiss #968e82`, `--danger #ff8a80`, `--warn #e0b955`, `--unsaved #ff8a3d` (added by `apply.4`), categories spelling `#ff7b72`, homophone `#f2875e`, grammar `#5cc0e0`, punctuation `#b994f5`, word-choice `#d9b44a`, consistency `#6cc28b`, other `#a8a092`, `--focus-bg #4a3f14`, `--para-active-bg #2f2a1a`, `--para-flash-bg #3d3415`, `--selected-bg #26304f`, `--badge-pending-bg #3d3415`, `--badge-pending-border #5c4f1f`, `--badge-pending-ink #e8c95a`, `--active-ring #8a7426`, `--find-bg #52420e` (#5e4b10 until `theme.5`, where `--ink-faint` on it, a find match in the frontmatter, measured 2.61:1; now 3.03), `--find-active-bg #e09a3a`, `--find-active-ink #16130f`, `--warn-bg #33291a`, `--warn-bg-strong #33291a`, `--warn-border #5c4a22`, `--danger-bg #3a1f1c`, `--editor-bg #20222b`, `--shadow-rgb 0 0 0`, `--backdrop rgb(0 0 0 / 60%)`, `--modal-shadow rgb(0 0 0 / 45%)`, `--hover-overlay rgb(255 255 255 / 10%)`, and the tint shares `--tint-pending 9%`, `--tint-hover 18%` and `--tint-decided-hover 16%`, the same as light, measured and kept by `theme.8` (below), and `--tint-small 16%` and `--tint-small-hover 26%`, the same as light by the same measure (`small.6`, below). `--font-ui` and `--font-prose` are the same in both themes and stay in the light block only; every other token is in both. Also: `a, a:visited { color: var(--accent) }`; zero-specificity `:where(select, input, textarea) { color: var(--ink) }` and `:where(input[type='password']) { background-color: var(--panel); border: 1px solid var(--border); border-radius: 4px; padding: 3px 6px }` (a background alone turns off the browser's drawing of the field, which falls back to a 2 px inset border, so it takes the other text fields' frame; no font, so `.modal__key-input` keeps its own); `accent-color: var(--accent-fill)`. Contrast tiers (WCAG 2.x, both themes): 4.5:1 for every text token and category on the three surfaces, categories on the active and flash paragraph backgrounds, `--ink` on every highlight, warn, danger and hover-tint background, `--ink-soft` and `--accent` on focus and selected rows, `--ink-soft`, `--accept` and `--danger` on `--warn-bg` (Test the connection's note and result in a Setup status card that warns, `setup.8`), `--on-accent` on the fills; 3:1 for `--ink-faint` and `--dismiss` on surfaces and highlights and for categories as marks. The author approved the light tweaks (Appendix D question 9), so since `theme.5` `--ink-faint` and `--dismiss` are `#8a8378` and `--warn` and `--cat-word-choice` `#84681c`, and every light pair passes with no per-token floor (the floors that would have applied otherwise were warn and word-choice 4.2, ink-faint 2.5, dismiss 2.4). Every new colour goes into both blocks with its pairs. `--unsaved` (`apply.4`) is the ember of changes not saved to the chapter: light `#ad4b0d`, dark `#ff8a3d` (the ember of the logo's original palette, (o)), on the dot before the chapter name (a mark, 3:1) and the status bar's count (text, 4.5:1), both on `--panel`. Light measures 5.46:1 on `--panel`, 5.09 on `--bg`, 4.79 on `--panel-alt` and 4.71 on `--selected-bg`, dark 6.77, 7.40, 6.09 and 5.53, so `apply.5`'s dot in the chapter list passes on a hovered or selected row too (`themeContrast.test.ts` checks both since `apply.5`). It replaces the `--accent` blue the dot had, so the dot no longer reads as a link or a comment. `test/renderer/themeCss.test.ts` pins every value in both blocks, so a changed value is edited there and here (dark) or on `theme.4`'s list of light differences (light) in the same commit. Surfaces for screenshots: Setup, nothing open, main view with a review covering every state, notification tones, find, paragraph editor, edit popover, lock card (by eye), every dialog, Bulk menu, legend and zoom.

Deliberate choices from `theme.8` (2026-10-16), after every surface was shot in both themes and the states screenshots miss were checked by hand:

- **Tints kept at 9%, 18% and 16% in dark.** Measured as the tinted box's WCAG contrast with what it sits on (the ratio counts screen flare, so dark differences are not overstated): a pending suggestion on the page is 1.14 to 1.18:1 in dark against 1.12 to 1.15 in light, hovered 1.35 to 1.45 against 1.26 to 1.34, and a decided one hovered 1.26 against 1.18. On the active paragraph dark is again the stronger, and on the focus yellow the two themes are within 0.04 of each other. The measure compares the range across the seven categories, lowest with lowest and highest with highest, and dark is at least light on every background. Category by category, spelling and homophone come out up to 0.03 lower in dark: spelling's pending tint is 1.145:1 on the dark page against 1.154 in light, and on `--focus-bg` 1.139 against 1.151 at rest and 1.302 against 1.334 hovered; homophone hovered on `--focus-bg` is 1.315 against 1.321. A gap that size is no reason to raise a share. At 100% and enlarged three times, every pending tint and hover in the main view is visible in dark. The tint is meant to be faint in both themes, as the 2 px category underline carries the state; raising it would make dark louder than light.
- **The small tints are the light shares in dark too** (`small.6`, 2026-10-19). By `theme.8`'s measure a capsule at rest is 1.300 to 1.380:1 on the dark page against 1.227 to 1.296 in light, hovered 1.590 to 1.755 against 1.406 to 1.540; on `--para-active-bg` 1.309 to 1.398 against 1.229 to 1.297, hovered 1.575 to 1.746 against 1.409 to 1.542; on `--focus-bg` 1.264 to 1.353 against 1.222 to 1.290, hovered 1.470 to 1.631 against 1.396 to 1.529. Dark is at least light, lowest with lowest and highest with highest, on all three. Category by category only spelling and homophone on `--focus-bg` come out lower in dark, spelling by 0.026 at rest and 0.059 hovered; that pair never shows, as a focused capsule is filled with `--focus-bg` itself rather than tinted over it. `--ink` on each category at `--tint-small-hover` over `--bg` is 9.67 to 10.59:1 in light and 7.95 to 8.78 in dark; `themeContrast.test.ts` checks that pair, and `themeCss.test.ts` pins both values in both blocks. Nothing is filled behind ghost text, which therefore meets only the page and the paragraph backgrounds its pairs check: until the `small` review a focused small change's ghost was filled with `--focus-bg`, where spelling, homophone, punctuation and other came to 4.14, 4.18, 4.28 and 4.03:1 in dark (light 4.70 and up), and `themeContrast.test.ts` now fails any rule that fills the ghost row.
- **The focus ring is the browser's own; there is no `--focus-ring`.** Chromium draws `outline: auto` as a double ring, rgb(229, 151, 0) with a white edge in light and rgb(238, 238, 238) with an rgb(16, 16, 16) edge in dark, so it shows on any background. The white ring that `theme.5` and `theme.7` noted on the find field, the edit popover's box and a focused select is that dark ring. Every Tab stop checked showed a ring in both themes, the filter chips and zoom buttons included, whose `overflow: hidden` would clip a ring drawn outside them (Chromium draws it inside there). A colour of the app's own would be held to 3:1, which the light orange (2.36:1 on `--panel`) does not reach, so light would change too.
- **Text selection is the browser's own; there is no `--selection-bg` for now, and the author decides in sitting C (`theme.10` a).** White text on rgb(49, 101, 206) in light (5.4:1) and rgb(6, 57, 163) in dark (9.9:1), in the prose, the find field and the paragraph editor. Ghost text is never selected (`user-select: none`), but it sits in the gap between lines that the selection band covers, so while text is selected a ghost above a selected line is drawn on the band in its own colour. On the light band the categories read at 1.02 to 1.27:1 (word-choice 1.02, consistency 1.03, other 1.04, grammar 1.05, punctuation 1.07, spelling 1.21, homophone 1.27) and the struck-through "was" ghost (`--ink-faint`) at 1.44; on the dark band at 3.8 to 5.0 and 3.06. The "delete" tag is drawn at 80% and a dismissed ghost at 32%, so in the real window the red DELETE measured about 1.35:1 in light (2.9 in dark), and the dismissed "most of" in the selection shot about 1.1 (1.6 in dark), against 1.5 and 2.0 for the same ghost on the page. Kept for now because a selection is brief and the author's own act, the ghost is decoration that never joins a selection, and it reads as before once the selection is gone. The other way is a lighter band of the app's own, `::selection` on the prose only with `--ink` text (`--ink` on the browser's light blue is 3.0:1, so the band itself would change), in both blocks with contrast pairs for `--ink` and the `--cat-*` ghosts on it. That changes light, which needs the author's yes, so the contact sheet's selection shot puts the question to him.
- **Scrollbars are the browser's,** drawn from each block's `color-scheme`: thumb rgb(139, 139, 139) on rgb(252, 252, 252) in light, rgb(159, 159, 159) on rgb(44, 44, 44) in dark. The dark track is a neutral grey a shade cooler than the basalt panels. `scrollbar-color` would change the scrollbars' shape in light as well, so this waits for the author's report in sitting C (`theme.10` c).
- **Disabled buttons** keep `opacity: 0.45` in both themes, and read as disabled, the filled blue ones too (Save to chapter, Create, Start review).
- **Margin notes** have no connector lines; a note is tied to its paragraph by its ¶ number, the paragraph's number in `--accent` and, while active, `--active-ring` around the note and `--para-active-bg` behind the paragraph. All read in both themes. The notification cards and the lock card over the scroller read in both themes, with greyscale text as C.48 accepts. The lock card was looked at during a real review against the OpenAI mock's `slow` scenario.
- **A focused suggestion in the active comment's paragraph gets a hairline.** `--focus-bg` on `--para-active-bg` is 1.05:1 in light (1.37 in dark), and such a suggestion was hard to find, so `.para--active .hunk--focused` draws `0 0 0 1px var(--focus-bg), 0 0 0 2px var(--active-ring)` in place of the 2 px `--focus-bg` spread: the same 2 px, so it covers no more of the neighbouring letters, with its outer pixel in the active comment's ring colour (1.27:1 on the paragraph in light, 3.14 in dark). Both are told apart by hue more than by lightness (the focus yellow is 1.03:1 on the light page), so there is no 3:1 tier; `themeContrast.test.ts` checks that the ring stands out from the paragraph more than the yellow does, in both themes.

**(f) Claude status and the Setup screen.** Probes run in parallel and send no model request: `runClaudeCli(path, ['auth','status','--json'])` through `parseAuthStatus` (keeps only `loggedIn`, `authMethod`, `apiProvider`, `apiKeySource`, `subscriptionType`, `email`); the handshake, Claude Code's own `initialize` exchange over stream-json, which answers with the model list and the account and sends no prompt (`claudeHandshake.ts`, the process always ended; 60 s budget on the first check, 20 s later; C.46; until `cli.1` the SDK's `query()` with a prompt that never yields); `--version` of the one Claude Code found (until `cli.1`, of both the bundled and the installed binary). `auth status` is trusted only when it says signed out. `classifyClaude`, first match: `auth.loggedIn === false` gives not-signed-in; `account.tokenSource` `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` with no cloud provider gives ready, auth-token (`setup.17`, C.71: the token outranks an API key and the sign-in, a cloud provider outranks it, and `auth status` calls it `oauth_token`); `account.apiKeySource` set and not `none` gives ready, api-key; `account.apiProvider` not `firstParty` gives ready, cloud; `account.email` gives ready, subscription; `account.tokenSource === 'none'` gives not-signed-in (when `auth.loggedIn` was true: "Claude Code is signed in through its own settings (<authMethod>), which <APP_NAME> does not load. Sign in with the command below instead."); no account: `auth.loggedIn` true gives ready with credential from `authMethod` (`claude.ai`, `oauth_token` subscription; `api_key`, `api_key_helper` api-key; `third_party` cloud), otherwise error. A free plan adds a plan warning. `classifyClaudeFailure`, first match (the caller handles refusal before): startup reason (`cli_version_too_old` outdated; `org_verify_failed`, `gateway_signin_required` login-expired; `org_pin_api_key_conflict` billing with API-key wording; `shell_tool_missing` "could not find PowerShell or Git Bash"); usage limit by the SDK's prefixes, copied into `claudeStatus.ts` at `cli.1`; text `/login expired|session expired|token (has )?expired|token revoked/i` login-expired; the assistant-error table (every row of today's `mapAssistantError`, plus `verification_required` billing, `cloud_credential_error` not-signed-in, `server_error` retry later); text rules (not logged in or please run /login; does not support this model or newer is required: outdated; invalid API key or disabled organization: billing with "An ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is in use and was refused"; credit balance or spend limit; not available with the plan; high load; overloaded, 529, 429); today's subtype table; else failed. A rewritten message keeps the original in `detail`. Views (`setupView`, first match): missing-binary gives not-installed; not-signed-in gives expired if the last failure is login-expired, else signed-out; a last failure of login-expired or not-signed-in outranks a ready runtime; error gives "could not check"; last failure outdated; usage limit in force; credential api-key; ready. The last failure clears on a successful review or connection test, on a signed-out check followed by a ready one, and (outdated only) when the binary path or version changes; a plain re-check never clears a sign-in failure. Test the connection (`setup.8`, 5.6 item 5) is the one model request Setup sends, only when pressed: `ai:test`, Haiku, `--max-turns 1`, the system prompt "Reply with the single word OK.", the review's flags, environment and folder (rule 6), stopped after 60 s; offered in the status card where `canTestConnection` says, with the note "Sends one very small request to Claude, which counts against your plan like any other." on a Claude plan, and in the card's own terms for an API key, a cloud provider, the `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` (C.71) or a check that failed (`connectionTestNote`, 5.6 item 5, `setup.8` review); held rather than disabled while it runs, as Check again is, so it keeps the keyboard focus (`heldButton`); its success sends `nextLastFailure` the `success` event that a review's sends (the task's `test-ok`). Review with AI opens Setup only for missing-binary or a definite not-signed-in; an error or timeout opens the dialog with a warning, and Setup offers "Try the review anyway" (`reviewGate`, `reviewDialogWarning`, `canTryReviewAnyway`, 5.6). Commands: `claude auth login`, with `& '<path>' auth login` on Windows and `'<path>' auth login` on POSIX under it whenever the path is known, for a terminal that does not find `claude` (single quotes with `'` doubled; `setup.7` shows both rather than choosing, since the terminal's PATH need not be the app's), on the tab of the system the app runs on only, since the path is that machine's (`pathForTab`, `setup.7` review); install-and-sign-in `irm https://claude.ai/install.ps1 | iex; & "$env:USERPROFILE\.local\bin\claude.exe" auth login` or `curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.sh | bash && ~/.local/bin/claude auth login`; outdated gives `claude update`. Signed-out intro: "Claude Code is not signed in. If reviews worked before, your sign-in has ended; this happens from time to time and nothing in <APP_NAME> is lost." Footer: "<APP_NAME> never sees your Claude password or sign-in token. You sign in with Anthropic, in Claude Code and your browser." Missing-binary is the first-run state for most people: it leads with the install-and-sign-in command, says Claude Code is Anthropic's free command-line tool that needs a Claude plan or an API key, and that <APP_NAME> does not include it.

**(g) Reviews bound to chapter files.** Invariant: a review file lives in `folderFor(reviewsFolder, review.sourcePath)`. Belonging: `samePath(review.sourcePath, chapterPath, ci)`. Current review, the one the chapter opens with: the belonging review last on screen, recorded per chapter path in the folder's `.last-open.json` by `recordShown` (`reviews:shown`, when the page shows one from History or the selector) and by `saveReview` for every new entry, written only when it changes and never touching the review file; while nothing is recorded, or the file recorded is gone, the belonging review with the greatest `decidedAt ?? createdAt` (`apply.26`, 6.4, C.57); History stays in `createdAt` order. A review file in the chapter's folder that is there but cannot be read stops `reviews:load-latest` with the read words unless the record names a review that could be read, and the page stays on what was open, with a card that names the chapter (`apply.25` and its review, 6.4). A review whose latest decisions could not be written, even when tried again just before, does not open from the list, History or the selector (6.4). `saveReview` honours an explicit path only if it ends `.review.json` and lies in the right folder; otherwise it writes a new entry there and returns its path (never throws for a path reason, so a reviews-folder change cannot break saves). Adoption, only when nothing belongs and the candidate's source file no longer exists: (a) at least 2 matching trailing path segments in the same slug folder (moved vault), or (b) equal `base.hash` with at least 500 characters (renamed or moved in Obsidian); rank hash matches, then more segments; two old paths equal on both adopt nothing, since it cannot be told which one the chapter was. Adoption rewrites every history file of the old path into the new folder with the new `sourcePath` and removes each old file only after its copy is written, following a journal (`.adoption.json` in the reviews folder) that the next store call completes if a crash interrupts it; an info card names the old path.

**(h) Save path and backups.** `writeChapter` order: (1) read the raw bytes once, refuse non-UTF-8 (`files.3`), hash; (2) hash differs from `expectedHash`: `stale`; (3) new bytes equal disk bytes: `written`, `unchanged`, no backup; (4) `beforeReplace`: refuse a backups root inside the manuscript folder or the chapter's own folder (`assertOutside`), then write the backup unless identical to the newest; a failure aborts with "Could not back up the chapter before saving, so nothing was written: <reason>"; (5) re-read, and a changed hash returns `stale`; (6) `atomicWriteFile`: temp file in the same folder, rename, on EPERM, EACCES or EBUSY back off 50, 100, 200, 400, 800 ms, re-hashing the target before each retry (`changed` returns `stale`, `unreadable` skips a round), then `explainWriteError`; (7) remember the hash for the watcher; (8) `afterWrite` prunes backups, best effort. Layout: `<userData>/backups/<chapterSlug>-<first 8 hex of sha256(pathKey)>/` with a one-time `chapter.json` `{sourcePath}`, files named by local time, for example `2026-10-14 16.04.33 Chapter 12.md`, `-2` after the time on a collision. Retention keeps the newest 20 plus the oldest of each local day within 30 days and never deletes a name it cannot parse.

**(i) Leaving and closing.** `CloseGuardState = {chapter, unsavedDecisions, changedParagraphs, locked, reviewRunning, writesPending, dialogText}`, pushed once the page has loaded and then on change. Main `createCloseGuard(deps)`: on window `close`, an allowed close, a gone renderer or nothing at risk closes at once with no IPC; otherwise preventDefault, reveal (restore, show, focus, flash), send `app:close-requested` once and arm a 3 s ack timer; no `received` opens a native "not responding" box (Wait / Close anyway); `received` arms a 10 s timer to a "still finishing" box; `prompting` clears both; a repeated close before `prompting` opens the box at once; every box has an AbortSignal so a late answer dismisses it; Close anyway destroys the window. `app:close-decision` true allows and closes on the next tick (and quits when quitting); false resets `quitting`. `query-session-end` and `session-end` only broadcast `app:flush`; shutdown is never blocked. `allowNextClose('update')` lets the update quit skip everything. What `apply.8` settled beyond this is in 7.7: after `session-end` every close goes through at once, Wait sets no new timer, the taskbar flashes only for a window that was not in front, a page that goes during a close ends it as asked, a reload is a new page whose arrival ends a close under way without closing (`did-navigate`), and a status or decision with no close under way changes nothing. Renderer `prepareToLeaveApp(reason)`: send `received`; race the in-flight save and both writer flushes against 5 s (on timeout or failure: "Still writing your decisions" with Close anyway and Wait); `closePrompt` (title "Close <APP_NAME>?"; buttons Cancel, "Close, keep for later" (focused) and "Save N change(s) and close", or "Apply, save and close" when a box changed, with no Save while a review of the open chapter runs; when nothing would be kept and only a running review or typed dialog text would be lost, Cancel (focused) and "Close anyway", with no Save; lines say how many changes are not saved to the chapter, that the decisions are kept in this review (always the one the chapter opens with, since `apply.26`: the review last on screen; the line for an older review, "The chapter opens with another review, so reopen this one from History.", went with it), and that the chapter file changes only on Save to chapter; that open paragraph boxes are kept and open for editing again when the author comes back to the chapter (the words `apply.7` gave them, C.53); that a running review stops and its result is lost; that text typed in an open dialog is lost); Save proceeds only on `saved` or `nothing-to-save`; Keep for later cancels a running review and flushes; a `finally` always answers. What `apply.9` settled beyond this is in 7.7: the push goes out in the same task as the change, a write that failed counts as pending until it is made and is tried again, Wait tries the writes again for another 5 s, the save that Save and close starts is timed like them, a write or a save that arrives while they run gets another round within the same 5 s, the Setup screen shows the question as the main layout does, and a question already open gives way. Leaving a chapter uses `leavePrompt` with the same three choices ("Save N change(s) first", or "Apply and save first" when a box changed, Keep for later, Cancel), titled "Leave <chapter>?", never prompts while locked, and re-clicking the open chapter, whichever of its reviews is on screen, or the review on screen in History or the selector, only flushes and reloads nothing (`isOpenTarget`, `apply.7`, 7.7). Another review of the open chapter, from History or the review selector, opens without a question (`apply.26`); the "Open the review from <date>?" title and `openedBy` are gone.

**(j) Drafts.** `<userData>/drafts/<first 16 hex of sha256(pathKey)>.json` holds `{version: 1, sourcePath, reviewId, savedAt, drafts: [{key, paragraphs, initial, text}]}` (at most 200 drafts, 1 MB text each), written by a keyed 700 ms writer, flushed on leave, close and session end; an empty set deletes the file (leaving keeps them: the leave prompt's Keep for later flushes them, `apply.7`). On open, a draft reopens only when its paragraph still reads exactly `initial`; a changed draft that cannot be placed is written to `drafts/set-aside/<chapter> <stamp>.txt` and a card offers Show the text. (`apply.16`, C.53.) Drafts belong to the chapter: they are placed whenever a review of it comes on screen, by the page's text at the draft's own index (`placeDrafts`), whichever review that is, and `reviewId` is a record. Show the text opens the .txt (`shell:reveal` `drafts` with the file's `name`). A draft that could not be set aside stays in the chapter's file until the next open; an unreadable file is moved aside as `<hash>.unreadable-<time>.json`; a file that cannot be read at all stays where it is and is never written over unread: each write for its chapter reads it first, is held while it still cannot be read and tried again at the next flush, and once it can be read its drafts are carried after those on screen until the next open. Escape or Cancel on a changed box asks (`discardDraftPrompt`, Keep editing focused).

**(k) Protected Obsidian syntax.** Hard spans: frontmatter; fenced code (3 or more backticks or tildes) and inline code; `%%...%%`, `<!-- -->`, `$$...$$` across lines (an unclosed one protects only its opening marker); embeds `![[...]]` whole; `[[target]]` whole, and `[[target|alias]]` as `[[target|` plus `]]` so the alias stays editable; links as `[` plus `](url)`; images whole; footnote refs `[^x]`, line-start `[^x]:`, inline footnotes `^[` and their `]`; block ids `^id` at line end; tags `#` at line start or after whitespace or `(` with at least one non-digit; callout `[!type]` with optional `+` or `-`; line-start `>` and heading `#` prefixes; thematic-break lines; bare http(s) URLs; HTML tags only for real element names (a, abbr, b, br, blockquote, center, code, del, details, div, em, font, hr, i, iframe, img, ins, kbd, li, mark, ol, p, pre, s, small, source, span, strong, sub, summary, sup, table, td, th, tr, u, ul, video, audio) or with attribute syntax. Not protected: emphasis, bold, strikethrough, highlight markers, indented code, inline `$math$`, tables, lists. Guarded hunks: `source 'claude'`, not authored, pending, `baseSide 'original'`, not stale, no `keptOut`. First failing rule wins: before `bodyStart` gives `frontmatter`; not inside one body paragraph gives `paragraph-break`; overlapping a hard span (a zero-length hunk strictly inside one) gives that kind; `original` or `replacement` containing `[ ] % $ ^ # < > | ~`, a backtick or a newline gives `markup`; applied alone, changing the paragraph's `syntaxSignature` or paragraph count gives the kind or `markup`. After `resolveOverlaps`, a survivor starting exactly where the previous survivor on the line ends gives `combined`. A failing hunk becomes `anchor 'stale'`, `start = end = 0`, `keptOut: reason`; the note reads "Kept out of the text: it would change Obsidian syntax (<label>). Make this change in Obsidian or with the paragraph pen if you want it."

**(l) Encoding.** `classifyBytes` order: FF FE or FE FF prefix gives utf16; any NUL: utf16 when NULs fill at least 25% of the even or odd positions in the first 4096 bytes, else binary; valid UTF-8 (BOM allowed) passes; more valid multi-byte sequences than invalid bytes gives mixed (with the first bad line); else legacy. Messages: UTF-16 and legacy say how to re-save as UTF-8 (Notepad on Windows, VS Code elsewhere); mixed says to retype the N damaged characters in Obsidian and never re-save in Notepad; all end "The app has not changed the file." Decoding stays `toString('utf8')` so the BOM survives.

**(m) Logging policy.** Lines are `<ISO> <LEVEL> <event> k=v`, scalar fields only, strings scrubbed (OpenAI and Anthropic key shapes, Bearer tokens, emails, the home directory in plain, forward-slash, percent-encoded and `file:///` forms) and cut at 300 characters. `loggableErrorFields`: `*-invalid-output` and `claude-refusal` log the code only; other app errors log code and message; `SyntaxError` logs its name and stack frames only (V8 quotes the input); errno errors log name, errno, syscall; others name, message, frames. Never logged: chapter text, prompts, guidelines, replies, `detail`, the account email, the key's last4, drafts, backups.

**(n) Release and update workflow.** Builder: `publish: [{provider: 'github', owner, repo, releaseType: 'draft'}]`; `nsis: {oneClick: true, perMachine: false, runAfterFinish: true, createDesktopShortcut: true, shortcutName, artifactName: '${productName}-Setup-${version}.${ext}', differentialPackage: true, deleteAppDataOnUninstall: false}`; `win.target` nsis x64 only. `verify-package.ts`: asar listing has `/out/main/index.js` and `/package.json` and no `/samples`, `/test`, `/src`, `/dist`, `/.dev-userdata` (normalise backslashes); no `@anthropic-ai` package and no `claude.exe` or `claude` binary anywhere in the package; `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`, `LICENSE.txt`, `GUIDELINES.default.md` exist; `--installer` adds `app-update.yml` (repo, no `publisherName` unless `EXPECT_SIGNED`) and `latest.yml` (version, file name, size). `release.yml`: on tags `v[0-9]+.[0-9]+.[0-9]+*`, windows-latest, `contents: write`, actions pinned by commit SHA, setup-node with `package-manager-cache: false` and no restored caches, the tag passed as `env: TAG` and never interpolated into scripts; steps `node scripts/check-release.cjs "$TAG"` before `npm ci`, lint, test, `npm run package`, verifier with `--installer`, `sha256sum` of the three files into `SHA256SUMS.txt`, notes from the CHANGELOG section plus footer, `gh release create "$TAG" --draft --verify-tag` with exactly 4 assets (`--prerelease` when the version has `-`). The author publishes with `gh release edit vX.Y.Z --draft=false --latest`; drafts are invisible to the updater. Updater flags: `autoDownload false`, `autoInstallOnAppQuit false`, `autoRunAppAfterInstall true`, `allowPrerelease false`, `allowDowngrade false`, logger = the app log. A consented version that reappears downloads from the cache without asking. Restart now: the renderer refuses while a review runs ("Restart after the review finishes"), runs `prepareToLeaveApp('update')`, shows the overlay, invokes `update:install`; main `runUpdateInstall` calls `closeGuard.allowNextClose('update')`, `abortAll()`, `await writesSettled(2000)`, `stopWatching()`, `autoUpdater.quitAndInstall(false, true)` and awaits nothing after it (the NSIS installer stops every process under `$INSTDIR` about 1.3 s after it starts). An install error takes the allowance back (the function `allowNextClose` returns, `apply.8`) and shows the error card.

**(o) Logo concepts.** Shared geometry: `pebble_points(cx, cy, a, b_top, b_bottom, n, rot)` is a superellipse, x = a·sgn(cos t)·abs(cos t)^(2/n), y = b·sgn(sin t)·abs(sin t)^(2/n) with b = b_bottom where sin t > 0 and b_top otherwise, rotated by rot about the centre; vertical gradients via `Image.linear_gradient('L')` and `ImageOps.colorize` through a polygon mask; values are fractions of the side, drawn at 8x and reduced with LANCZOS; the small spec applies at 40 px and below. **A "Seam"** (chosen 2026-10-14, in turquoise: rim #5ad3db to #178e9c, body #29393c to #121b1d, ghost #7d999b, line #f1efe6, dots #4fd8e0; the colours below are the ember original, kept as the alternative): pebble cx .5, cy .52, a .46, b_top .42, b_bottom .36, n 2.6, rotation -5 degrees; rim = whole pebble #f08a3c to #c2401c, inner pebble (each semi-axis minus max(.035, 1.25/size)) #4a3d36 to #211a17; large: ghost #9a8577 x .33 to .67, top .32, height .05, fully rounded; line #f4ecdc x .25 to .75, top .43, height .11, radius height/4; 5 dots #ff8a3d diameter .07, gap .045, centred, top .62; small: no ghost, line top .40 height .13, 3 dots diameter .10 gap .07 top .60. **B "Ember tile"**: rounded square pad .03 radius .22, #ec7a3a to #a8361a; ghost #ffd2ad x .315 to .70 top .33 height .055; line #2a211d x .215 to .785 top .52 height .115 with serif uprights height .168 width .03; 6 dots #fff1e0 diameter .05 gap .04 top .688; small: line top .42 height .14, no serifs or ghost, 3 dots diameter .10 gap .07 top .64. **C "Cursor"**: pebble and rim as A with cy .53, b_top .41; line #f4ecdc x .20 to .54 centred at y .465 height .11 (small .14); ghost (large) #9a8577 x .26 to .50 top .30 height .05; ember #ff8a3d I-beam at x .66, stem width .075 (small .10) from y .27 to .69 (small .26 to .70), caps width .19 (small .22) height .065 (small .09), all rounded. None uses purple, facets or a radial spark. B sits closest to Claude's orange; C's I-beam reads as a letter. On macOS 26 any non-squircle icon sits on a grey tile, which `brand.10` handles.

**(p) Where data lives** (README and `opensource.11`). `%APPDATA%\ai-grammar-checker` (name predates the rename): `settings.json`, `settings.json.corrupt-*`, `secrets.json` (the encrypted OpenAI key), `reviews\` (default reviews folder, holds chapter text), `backups\` (full chapter copies), `drafts\` and `drafts\set-aside\` (typed prose), `logs\main.log` and `main.1.log`. `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\manuscript-review`: the app. `%LOCALAPPDATA%\manuscript-review-updater`: a cached copy of the installer, used for small updates. Uninstalling removes only the app folder; the README says how to delete the rest. The manuscript folder holds chapters and `GUIDELINES.md` (created once, never overwritten). The Claude sign-in belongs to Claude Code, never to the app. On macOS and Linux the data folder is `~/Library/Application Support/ai-grammar-checker` or `~/.config/ai-grammar-checker`.

**(q) Claude through the user's `claude` command** (11.6 question 21, `cli.1`). No Anthropic package is a dependency, so nothing of Anthropic's ships. `claudeCli.ts` runs the path from `setup.3` with no shell, `windowsHide`, `cwd` = userData, the env from `buildClaudeEnv`, and `-p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --tools "" --setting-sources= --strict-mcp-config --no-session-persistence --permission-mode dontAsk --effort <e> --max-turns 3 --include-partial-messages`, plus `--model <m>` unless the model is `default`. The system prompt and the JSON schema travel in the `initialize` control request (`systemPrompt: [text]`, `jsonSchema`), as the SDK sent them, so a long guidelines file never meets the Windows command-line limit; the chapter goes in as one user message on stdin. It reads NDJSON lines: `system` init, `assistant`, `rate_limit_event` (its `status`, `resetsAt`, `rateLimitType`, `utilization` and `isUsingOverage`: `readRateLimit`, `setup.9`, C.64), and `result` with `structured_output`, `usage`, `total_cost_usd`, `modelUsage`, `is_error` and `subtype`. The same classifiers as before map failures. Cancel ends the process tree (`taskkill /PID <pid> /T /F` on Windows). The model list and account details come from the CLI's `initialize` control request over stream-json, which is what the SDK sent. Check it against the installed version first; if that exchange is unavailable, fall back to a static alias list (Default, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku) without version labels. Every flag was present in Claude Code 2.1.266's `--help` on 2026-10-14: `--tools ""` disables all tools, and `--bare` must never be used because it turns off subscription sign-in. `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE` in `src/shared/ai/claudeStatus.ts` is the oldest version the argument test was verified against.

### 11.4 Release checklist

Before the cut (agent unless marked):

1. M0 to M10 ticked or recorded as cut in this section; every M12 task marked build or cut in Appendix D.
2. `release.21` done; `npm view electron-builder dist-tags` and `npm view electron-updater dist-tags` checked for newer v26 patches.
3. The package verifier confirms that no Anthropic binary ships (Appendix D 8), and the pages in Appendix A were re-read for changes.
4. CHANGELOG `[1.0.0]` dated; README final; `npm run check:public` clean.
5. On a fresh clone of `main`: `npm ci`, `branding:check`, `check:public`, `lint`, `format:check`, `test`, `build`, `package` (verifier with `--installer`).

Cut:

6. `npm version 1.0.0 -m "Release %s"`; **(author)** approves `git push origin main` then `git push origin v1.0.0` (never `--tags` or `--all`).
7. The workflow creates a draft with exactly 4 assets; check `SHA256SUMS.txt` against the files.

Smoke test, sitting D **(author)**, on a new local standard account (Settings > Accounts > Other users > "Add a user without a Microsoft account") with no Claude Code, no `.claude` folder and no Node; record this PC's Smart App Control state:

8. Download the setup exe from the draft in Edge; screenshot every browser prompt; `Get-FileHash` matches `SHA256SUMS.txt`.
9. Run it; screenshot SmartScreen; expect only a progress bar, no UAC, install under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\manuscript-review`, shortcuts named Lapidary, a legible taskbar icon in light and dark.
10. About shows 1.0.0; Setup says Claude Code is not installed; the theme follows Windows; a second shortcut launch focuses the first window.
11. Install Claude Code and sign in using only the Setup screen (`setup.14`); the app turns Connected on refocus; record the CLI's printed lines; Test the connection works.
12. Copy `samples/manuscript` to Documents, complete the Welcome steps, run a review with the Default model, accept, dismiss, edit a paragraph, close with an open box (prompt), Save to chapter (backup created; Notepad shows the file with its line endings), Copy diagnostics (the agent checks the pasted text for versions and no secrets).
13. With the dev switch variables set in the environment, the app ignores them.
14. Uninstall: the app folder goes, the data folders stay; reinstall 1.0.0. Launch once with Appearance set to Dark: no light flash.
15. **(author)** Publish: `gh release edit v1.0.0 --draft=false --latest`. Check for updates now says "Up to date".

Updater proof: ship a small real fix as 1.0.1 through steps 4 to 7 and publish it. In the test account, 1.0.0 offers 1.0.1 within 15 s with a size; the download shows progress; Restart now with an open box raises the close prompt; after closing the box the overlay and the NSIS progress window appear and the app reopens as 1.0.1 with no UAC or SmartScreen; settings and reviews intact; Restart now during a review is refused; a downloaded but uninstalled update survives a restart without a second download. The agent reads "Full: X, To download: Y" from the pasted log and records sizes in PLAN; above about 20 MB for an app-only change, note `differentialPackage: 'store-asar'` (26.17.0) as a follow-up. Then the author runs `brand.9`'s carry-over checks on his own account, sends the friend the release link, and `files.13` begins.

Conditional on the friend's OS: **macOS** (`release.16`, `brand.10`): a `macos` job uploads dmg, zip, blockmaps and `latest-mac.yml`, a final job rewrites `SHA256SUMS.txt`; on his Mac: Safari download, Open Anyway, Setup ready, one review and Save, binary hash equals the manifest's, `codesign -dv` still shows Anthropic's team, the update card links to the release page. **Linux** (`release.17`): a `linux` job uploads .deb, .AppImage and `latest-linux.yml`; on Ubuntu 24.04 the deb installs, a review and Save work, and an AppImage updates itself.

### 11.5 Risks

- **Anthropic's terms move.** The author accepts no Anthropic terms and ships no Anthropic binary; each user runs their own Claude Code under their own agreement with Anthropic. The SDK documentation still discourages third-party products built on subscription sign-in, and Anthropic has pressed at least one free tool to drop it (Appendix A). If that happens here, the API-key route and OpenAI remain. Mitigations: no login control, no credential handling, plain-text attribution, the pages re-read before each release.
- **Claude Code version skew.** Each user's Claude Code updates on its own schedule; the author's had stalled at 2.1.266 on 2026-10-14. Setup flags anything older than `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE`. A newer Claude Code could rename or drop a flag the app passes; the pinned argument test, the per-release check in `release.10` and a clear error (never a silent wrong answer) cover that.
- **Smart App Control or S mode** blocks an unsigned app outright. Ask early (`files.17`); signing costs money and does not remove SmartScreen at this scale.
- **The NSIS updater kills the app** about 1.3 s after it starts. Guard, flushes, overlay and `allowNextClose` happen before the installer is spawned; drafts and decisions are on disk within 700 ms.
- **A close-guard bug traps the window.** Clean states close with no IPC, the renderer always answers in `finally`, two native fallbacks end in `destroy()`, and a gone renderer is bypassed.
- **Content leaks** through new log calls, diagnostics, issues or screenshots. The scalar-only API, one error policy, content-scan tests, `check:public` and the issue-form checkbox guard against it; screenshots come from the mock.
- **Supply chain.** Whoever controls the GitHub account or the release job can update every install; unsigned updates are checked only by sha512. 2FA, SHA-pinned actions, no cache, draft-then-publish.
- **SDK and toolchain churn.** The Agent SDK ships almost daily and CI cannot run a live Claude call; v26 fixes hide under the `v26` dist-tag; electron-builder 27 and electron-updater 7 are alpha. Quarterly Dependabot, a manual bump routine, exact pins.
- **Update size.** Without Claude Code the installer is mostly Electron (measure it in `release.4`); an app fix downloads most of the asar; deleting a published `.blockmap` turns every update into a full download.
- **Guard false positives** keep a few legitimate prose fixes out (a stray `#word`, "per cent" to `%`); they stay visible under Unanchored. Joint syntax creation by two model edits is possible after the trim and shows in the ghost text before acceptance.
- **Adoption** can attach a review from a disconnected drive to a same-named chapter; strict rules, a 500-character floor, no adoption on ties and an info card limit it.
- **Hot-file collisions** across workstreams: the one-task-at-a-time rule and the M2 seams.
- **The Linux CI leg** has never run; it stays non-required until it passes once.
- **The scroll fix may not be the whole story** (GPU, display); an "unchanged" A/B result is recorded as a known cosmetic issue.
- **Same-name products.** Igneous was dropped for this (Appendix D 1). On 2026-10-14 Lapidary had only small, unrelated software uses and no software trademark; the domain lapidary.app is someone else's.
- **Disk use**: the installed app plus the updater cache (measure both in `release.4`), which uninstall leaves behind (documented).

Replace it with direct calls to the user's installed `claude` command (`claude -p` with `--output-format`, `--json-schema`, `--tools`, `--setting-sources`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--no-session-persistence`, `--system-prompt`, `--model` and `--effort`, all present in Claude Code 2.1.266), so the installer contains no Anthropic code at all (recommended, a contained rewrite of `providers/claude.ts` plus CLAUDE.md's "only through the Agent SDK" line), or keep the SDK?
22. Colour. **Answered 2026-10-14: turquoise for now** (Appendix D).

### 11.7 Small changes you can see (`small`)

A change that touches only punctuation, a space or a one-letter word gets trimmed to 0 or 1 characters (4.3, step 5). Its mark in the line is then only as wide as that one character: 4 to 7 px of underline, or a 2 px caret for an insertion. In the screenshot the author sent with the round 6 feedback (2026-10-14), paragraph 74's accepted hyphen-to-space in "double standard" is a 4 px grey line under a space, and the faint "WAS -" above it has a strike-through that merges into the hyphen. Paragraph 76's pending comma after "Mira" is a 2 px purple bar with a 3 px comma above it and an invisible x 23 px to its right. Both are hard to spot while scrolling and hard to hit: about 5 x 22 px on the line, against 68 x 21 px for the word "standard".

**Decisions.**

- **Drawn bigger, never stored bigger.** Nothing here changes a hunk field, an offset, a text node in the prose, or anything `compose.ts` reads, so Save to chapter and Copy result cannot change (CLAUDE.md rules 4 and 5). Widening the stored hunk instead would move offsets, demote neighbours in `resolveOverlaps`, and let `touches()` supersede it more often. It would also make the ghost repeat the whole word, against 4.3 step 5.
- **One definition of small.** `isSmallText(text)` lives in a new `src/shared/ui/small.ts`. After combining marks (`\p{M}`) are removed, the text is small when it has at most 1 code point, or at most 3 code points with no letter or digit (`\p{L}`, `\p{N}`). So "", ",", " ", "i", "--" and "..." are small; "an", "it" and "----" are not. `trim.ts` already widens every letter change to its whole word, so this catches exactly punctuation, whitespace and one-letter words, which are all under one em at 17 px. Each surface asks the question of the text it shows:
  - the line asks `isSmallLive`, which tests the live face and excludes text holding a line break, because that box is split across lines;
  - the keycap tests the ghost face;
  - margin marks and list rows ask `isSmallChange`, which tests both sides, so a row looks the same whatever its status.
- **The caret stays with its word and follows status.** `.hunk__caret` becomes `display: inline`. The inline-block caret broke away from "Mira" onto the next line at 3 of 651 window widths, the inline one at none, and no word joiner is needed. The caret turns `--ink-faint` when accepted and `--border-strong` when dismissed. Today it keeps the category colour, against "colour means undecided" (7).
- **A capsule under small live text.** An absolutely positioned `::after` on `.hunk__live`, 0.75em wide or the glyph's width if wider. It is painted beneath the glyphs (`.para { isolation: isolate }`, `z-index: -1`), so neighbouring letters stay pure ink.
  - Pending: the category colour at `--tint-small`, mixed over transparent so it also sits correctly on the active paragraph's yellow, with a 2 px category `border-bottom` across it.
  - Accepted: a 2 px `--ink-faint` border-bottom and no fill.
  - Dismissed: a 1 px dotted `--border-strong` line.
  - Hit zone: a transparent `::before`, 0.9em wide.
  - Touching hunks: both pseudo-elements pull back to the hunk's own edge on any side where the neighbouring plain text is shorter than 2 characters, so neither covers a touching hunk.
  - Why it moves nothing: absolute boxes add no advance, so line breaks, the span rect (scroll target, tooltip, popover), the inputs to `clipGhosts` and the heights of margin notes are unchanged.
  - Borders rather than box-shadow, because forced colours drop shadows.
  - The click target on the line grows from about 5 x 22 to 16 x 23 px.
  - When focused, the capsule (not the whole span) takes `--focus-bg`. The ghost above stays on the page, as a word-sized change's does, and the arrowhead marks the line. Until the `small` review `.hunk__ghost` took `--focus-bg` too, where spelling, homophone, punctuation and other came to 4.14, 4.18, 4.28 and 4.03:1 in dark, under the 4.5 that 11.3 (e) asks of a category as text; a ring with no fill read as a keycap round the whole ghost (C.68).
- **A keycap ghost.** A small ghost sits in a box: a 1 px rounded border in the ghost's colour at 60%, no fill, and the glyph at 1.25 times the ghost size. Since `small.7` only named whitespace keeps the box: with a box on every key the rows piled up on a dialogue line with six small changes, so punctuation and one-letter words are the bare glyph at 1.25 times, still unstruck.
  - Whitespace is named in small caps: SPACE, 2 SPACES, NO-BREAK SPACE, TAB, LINE BREAK, PARAGRAPH BREAK.
  - An accepted insertion reads "ADDED [,]" instead of a bare "ADDED".
  - Text decoration does not reach inside an inline-block, so a small original is not struck through. Its "was" label carries that meaning, and the rule in 7 that a replaced original is always struck through gets this exception.
  - Case 1 then reads "WAS [-]". An inserted space reads "-> [SPACE]" instead of an arrow pointing at nothing. On case 2 the hover-only x moves from +23 to +38 px, so a click aimed at the comma no longer dismisses it.
  - No fill, because light `--cat-word-choice` is exactly 4.5:1 on `--bg` and any tint behind it would fail the theme contrast test.
  - A pure `ghostContent(hunk)` in `src/shared/render.ts` chooses the role label, tag and key, replacing the inline choice in `HunkSpan.tsx`.
- **Words for invisible characters.** One table in `small.ts` (`whitespaceName`, `characterName`, `sideText`) feeds the list row, the tooltip, the details and stale pairs, and the aria-label.
  - Case 1's row reads "- (hyphen) -> (space)" instead of "- -> (nothing)", which described it as a deletion. "(nothing)" stays for the empty string only.
  - Words, not U+2423 or U+00B6: a new reader will not know those symbols, and Literata may lack them.
  - List rows for small changes also show a few words on either side, with the change in a chip styled like the keycap ("...up with Mira [-> ,] who had..."), built by a pure `changeInContext(base, hunk)`. Stale rows keep their pair.
- **Margin marks for pending small changes, and an arrowhead for focus.**
  - A 3 px x 0.9em bar in the category colour sits in the rail, level with each line that holds a pending small change. An ink arrowhead marks the line where the focused hunk starts, whatever its size.
  - The left edge is where the eye returns on every line, and it is where proofreaders and change bars put their marks.
  - Marks are buttons with no text, placed by CSS anchor positioning (`anchor-name` on `.hunk__live`, `top: calc(anchor(top) - 0.53em)`). That follows reflow, zoom and font loading with no measuring pass. Electron 44.3.0 ships Chromium 152, where a mock placed marks within 0.5 px, including next to a caret that had wrapped to the next line.
  - The 11 x 39 px target lies outside the prose. Hovering shows the tooltip under the change, for reading only; to edit, click the bar and press `e`. Clicking selects without scrolling, and then `a`, `d` and `u` act.
  - The bar and the arrowhead are one button under one key, so a bar that becomes the arrowhead under the pointer keeps its element and its leave still runs. A mark that unmounts while the pointer is on it closes its card itself (`markHover`), since React fires no leave for an element that has gone (the `small` review).
  - Decided small changes get no mark, so a worked chapter goes quiet.
  - Bars and arrowhead are drawn with borders. The rail narrows from 42 to 39 px.
- **Keys that start where the author is reading.**
  - Shift+J and Shift+K go to the next and previous pending change, skip decided ones, and wrap.
  - When nothing is focused, or the focused change is off screen, `j`/`k` (and their Shift forms) start from the paragraphs on screen; otherwise they step as today. Today the first `j` after scrolling jumps back to the old focus or to paragraph 1.
  - Today Caps Lock also disables `j`/`k`, because only lowercase is handled. Shift is now read from `event.shiftKey`.
  - Letters rather than `[` and `]`, which need AltGr on a Canadian French layout and never reach `keyboard.ts`.
  - The keys and the list sort with one comparator (start, end, id), which is the order `activeHunks` renders in. Today they break ties three different ways.

**What each shape looks like afterwards.** Capsule, cup (a grey line with no fill) and dotted line are all 0.75em wide; [x] is a keycap and "bar" is a margin mark. A focused hunk of any of these shapes has its capsule on `--focus-bg`, with the arrowhead in the rail; its ghost stays on the page. Word-sized hunks are unchanged apart from that arrowhead.

| Shape | Pending | Accepted | Dismissed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Comma inserted after "Mira" (case 2) | caret in a purple capsule; `-> [,]`; bar | comma over a cup; `ADDED [,]` | grey caret over a dotted line; faded `-> [,]` |
| Comma or doubled space deleted | the comma or gap in a capsule; `-> DELETE` (plus `[SPACE]` for whitespace); bar | grey caret over a cup; `WAS [,]` or `WAS [SPACE]` | glyph over a dotted line; faded `-> DELETE` |
| One-character swap, "." to "," | period in a capsule; `-> [,]`; bar | comma over a cup; `WAS [.]` | period over a dotted line; faded `-> [,]` |
| Hyphen to space, "double standard" (case 1) | hyphen in a capsule; `-> [SPACE]`; bar | a cup under the gap, reaching the edges of "e" and "s"; `WAS [-]`, not struck through; row "- (hyphen) -> (space)" | hyphen over a dotted line; faded `-> [SPACE]` |
| One-letter word, "i" to "I" | "i" in a capsule; `-> [I]`; bar | "I" over a cup; `WAS [i]` | "i" over a dotted line; faded `-> [I]` |

A change whose live text holds a line break, such as a paragraph join, gets no capsule. Its keycap names what replaces the break, and while it is pending its bar marks the line.

**Tasks**, in order. `small.2` to `small.4` are pure and can land at any time after M2. The other tasks change what the window shows, so they land after `theme.4`.

**Acceptance.**

- Paragraph 76, pending comma after "Mira":
  - while scrolling at reading speed, the spot after "Mira" shows as a tinted capsule, and a bar sits in the margin beside its line;
  - a click anywhere on the capsule accepts it;
  - the ghost reads "-> [,]" with the comma near reading size, and a click on the comma never dismisses it.
- Accepting the comma and then dismissing it leaves no purple in the line; dismissed, the caret is light grey.
- Paragraph 74, accepted "double standard": the ghost reads "WAS [-]" with the hyphen plainly a hyphen, the gap has a grey line about a letter wide, and the list row reads "- (hyphen) -> (space)".
- After scrolling to the middle of a chapter, Shift+J lands on the next pending change below the top of the screen, never on paragraph 1. With Caps Lock on, `j` still moves.
- Save to chapter on a chapter with a dozen small accepted changes writes exactly those changes, and the chapter's git diff shows nothing else. Copy result matches, and Ctrl+F and a copied paragraph show no extra text.
- At 200% zoom and in dark mode, capsules, keycaps and bars line up with their text. Word-sized hunks look as before, apart from the focus arrowhead.
- `npm test`, `npm run lint` and `npm run build` pass, including the new guard, predicate, ghost, marks, navigation and contrast tests.

**Not doing.**

- **Widening stored hunks or setting a minimum stored width.** That is a data change, not a visual one (first decision).
- **Showing a pending comma in the line.** The legend promises that the line is what Save would write, and the save workstream is built on "The chapter already matches the page".
- **Padding or a minimum inline width on tiny text.** It reflows the line under the pointer on every click and moves span rects and margin notes.
- **Underlining the neighbouring word and making it clickable.**
  - It misstates the change: "ADDED" over an underlined "Mira," reads as "Mira was added".
  - It needs a measuring pass on every accept and a contrast exception below 3:1.
  - It stays a fallback only if the author finds the capsule too small, and then for paint only.
- **A fixed-width dab centred on the change.** The capsule does the same job, painted beneath the text.
- **A blue focus ring and an arrival pulse.** The ring uses the comment blue, the pulse is motion, and the arrowhead already marks where focus lands. The yellow focus on the active paragraph's yellow (1.05:1) goes to theme.8, which gave it an `--active-ring` hairline inside the same 2 px (11.3 e).
- **Symbol glyphs for whitespace, in the line or in the ghost.** An interpunct reads as punctuation, and a real glyph in the line would be found by Ctrl+F and copied.
- **Grey margin marks for decided changes on paragraph hover.** They flicker as paragraphs pass under a resting pointer, and they promote layers in the scroller that theme.1 made LCD-clean. Category colour on decided tiny hunks is left out too, so a worked chapter stays quiet.
- **Moving or disabling the x (C.34), a "small only" filter chip or key, and minimap marks sized by width (C.17).**

**Dependencies.**

- **theme.4.** The light baseline must stay pixel-identical through theme.4, so every visible step lands after it and, when it lands, adds itself to the list of deliberate light differences in `theme.4`'s tick (M5): caret colour, capsule, keycap, marks, list chip, rail width. These steps use theme.4's `--focus-bg` and `--tint-*` tokens.
- **theme.5.** `--tint-small` and `--tint-small-hover` go into both token blocks and the contrast test. There is no new colour token: marks reuse `--cat-*`, `--ink`, `--ink-faint` and `--border-strong`, and nothing here uses the save workstream's ember `--unsaved` colour.
- **theme.1, theme.3 and theme.8.**
  - theme.1: its LCD probe is rerun with the isolation in place.
  - theme.3: its fixture and driver are reused for this work.
  - theme.8: it owns `--focus-bg` against `--para-active-bg`, settled with a hairline (11.3 e). It landed before `--tint-small` existed, so `small.6` sets the dark `--tint-small` and `--tint-small-hover` itself, by theme.8's measure as `small.6`'s own bullet states it (the range across the categories, dark at least light), checked by eye in the theme.3 driver's dark shots. The guess here was 22 to 30%, but `--tint-pending` and `--tint-hover` came out as strong in dark as in light at the same share, so the light 16% may do.
- **11.6 question 9** (`--ink-faint` to #8a8378, 2.8 to 3.4:1) decides the grey of case 1's line and its "was" ghost. This workstream does not decide it a second time.
- **apply.4 (save workstream).** The legend is rewritten once, with this workstream's lines next to the save workstream's "Not saved to chapter" item (`apply.4`'s; `apply.12` added no legend line). `apply.4` did the rewrite before any of this workstream had landed, so `small.10` adds its items to that list and does not rewrite it again.
- **Appendix C.** The new entry takes the next free number at commit time, as the rules at the top of section 11 say.
- **Line numbers.** `HunkSpan.tsx` and `app.css` changed while this was designed (the hidden "insertion" word was removed in C.37), so implementation cites selectors and functions, not line numbers.

## Appendix B: development machine (2026-09-08, updated 2026-10-16)

Windows 11, Node 24.21.0, npm 11.19.0, git 2.45.2. Claude Code 2.1.266 is installed natively at `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\claude.exe`. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is not set. PowerShell 5.1 is the primary shell and Git Bash is available. Shell heredocs are unreliable on this machine: long ones fail, and escape handling has turned a backslash sequence into a control character. Files are therefore written with the editor tools, not shell redirection. The folder is a git repository. Since 2026-10-16 it is public at `github.com/MichaelBradetLegris/lapidary` (remote `origin`), default branch `main`, which began as one fresh commit of the tree (`opensource.9`); the earlier private repository keeps the old history. The folder has a repo-local identity, `Michael Bradet-Legris <45394569+MichaelBradetLegris@users.noreply.github.com>`, and `commit.gpgsign false`, so no public commit carries the personal address or a key tied to it. Under Appendix D 7 as changed on 2026-10-14, the agent may commit and push: it works on a branch per milestone, pushes it, and fast-forwards `main` once the milestone verifies. It never force-pushes, and the `main` ruleset refuses force-pushes and deletion anyway.

## Appendix C: deviations from this plan, recorded as they happened

### C.1 Vite 7 instead of Vite 8 (Phase 0)

electron-vite 5.0.0, the newest stable release, declares `vite: ^5 || ^6 || ^7` as a peer dependency. Vite 8 is only reachable through electron-vite 6.0.0-beta.1, and `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6 requires Vite 8 while version 5 covers Vite 4 to 7. The project therefore uses electron-vite 5.0.0 with Vite 7.3.6 and `@vitejs/plugin-react` 5.2.0, all stable releases. Nothing in the design depends on a Vite 8 feature. Revisit when electron-vite 6 ships stable.

### C.2 ESM main process, CJS preload (Phase 0)

`@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` is ESM only (`"type": "module"`, `main: sdk.mjs`). Requiring it from a CommonJS Electron main bundle would depend on Electron's `require(esm)` support, which the plan never verified. The package is therefore `"type": "module"`, electron-vite emits `out/main/index.js` as ESM, and the preload is pinned to CommonJS (`out/preload/index.cjs`) so the window can keep `sandbox: true`. Paths in main use `import.meta.url` rather than `__dirname`.

### C.3 Typecheck runs through project references (Phase 0)

`tsc --noEmit -p ...` cannot be combined with `composite`, so `npm run typecheck` is `tsc --build --force` over `tsconfig.node.json` and `tsconfig.web.json`, both `emitDeclarationOnly` into `node_modules/.tmp`. Behaviour is the same: type errors fail the command, nothing lands in `out/`.

### C.4 Matches must respect word boundaries (Phase 1)

Sections 4.3 and 4.5 anchor on plain substring search. The property tests found this is not safe: "began" matches inside "beganning" and, in the generated cases, "w1" matches inside "w10", so a hunk could anchor to the wrong span and rewrite untouched prose. `cleanOccurrences` in `src/shared/diff/fuzzy.ts` now prefers matches whose neighbouring characters are not word characters, falling back to raw matches only when a word occurs solely mid-word. It also decides `exact` versus `ambiguous`.

Rebase gained a related change: instead of "look for the expected side, then the other side", it collects candidates for both sides and ranks them by word-boundary cleanliness first, then by distance from the old offset, with a one-character tie-break in favour of the side the hunk expected. Without this, an accepted hunk whose `original` still appears mid-word elsewhere re-anchored onto the wrong text.

### C.5 Rebase adopts what is on disk (Phase 1)

Section 4.5 says to update `baseSide` when the author applied a change by hand, but not what happens to the decision. Leaving a hunk `pending` while `baseSide` is `replacement` would make the next save silently revert the author's own edit. The rule implemented is: when the found side differs from the recorded one, the disk wins and the decision follows it. Text now showing the replacement becomes `accepted`; an `accepted` hunk whose text is back to the original returns to `pending`. When the found side matches the recorded side, which is the normal case, the decision is untouched, so the round-trip property in 4.5 still holds. Since `apply.23` the disk wins only where it plainly says so, and a hunk it seems to contradict, but not plainly, leaves the page instead (C.52).

### C.6 Rebase ranks candidates by surrounding context (Phase 3)

Ranking re-anchor candidates by distance from the old offset breaks on short common words. Driving the real app showed it: after a paragraph was inserted above them, an accepted `of` -> `have` hunk re-anchored onto an unrelated `of` and its decision was flipped back to pending. `rebase.ts` now scores every candidate by how many characters either side of it still agree with the old file (40 each way) and ranks word-boundary cleanliness first, then context agreement, then distance, then the expected side. There is a regression test for it in `test/shared/rebase.test.ts`. `apply.23` (C.52) adds zero-width places for a side that holds no text, counts the start and end of the file as agreeing where both texts begin or end there, and puts the expected side before distance.

### C.7 The guidelines file is not a chapter (Phase 3)

`GUIDELINES.md` lives in the manuscript folder and is Markdown, so it appeared in the chapter list and could be opened and reviewed. `files:list` now excludes the resolved guidelines path.

### C.8 How the running app is tested

electron-vite's dev command honours a `REMOTE_DEBUGGING_PORT` environment variable, so `REMOTE_DEBUGGING_PORT=9222 npm run dev` exposes the renderer over the DevTools protocol without any code in the app. A short script can then evaluate expressions in the live window, including `await import('/state/store.ts')`, which returns the very store module the app is running. That is how the file list, save, watcher and rebase behaviour above were exercised against real files. No debugging hooks are compiled into the app.

### C.9 Tools are removed with `tools: []`, not a hardcoded deny list (Phase 4, risk 2)

`sdk.d.ts` has a top-level `tools` option documented as "`[]` (empty array) - Disable all built-in tools". That is used instead of enumerating `disallowedTools`, so the list can never drift as tool names change. The call also passes `mcpServers: {}`, `strictMcpConfig: true`, `settingSources: []`, `persistSession: false`, `permissionMode: 'dontAsk'` and `cwd: app.getPath('userData')`. `Options.env` REPLACES the subprocess environment rather than merging, so `process.env` is spread explicitly.

### C.10 `maxTurns` is 3, not 1 (Phase 4, risk 1)

The SDK delivers structured output through an internal end-turn tool call and retries on a schema mismatch, so one turn is not a safe budget. The value is 3 as the plan's risk note anticipated. The verified run used one turn of it.

### C.11 The zod JSON Schema target is `draft-07` (Phase 4)

Section 5.3 writes `z.toJSONSchema(..., { target: 'draft-7' })`. zod 4.5.4 accepts `'draft-04' | 'draft-07' | 'draft-2020-12' | 'openapi-3.0'`. The code uses `'draft-07'` and a test asserts the emitted `$schema`.

### C.12 The model dropdown keeps aliases the CLI does not list (Phase 4, risk 5)

`supportedModels()` on the author's Max plan returns `default`, `opus[1m]`, `claude-fable-5-1[1m]`, `sonnet`, `haiku`. Plain `opus`, which is the default setting and which the CLI resolves correctly to `claude-opus-5`, is not among them, so a plain `<select>` displayed the wrong row. `modelOptions` in `src/renderer/state/models.ts` prepends the stored value when the list does not contain it.

### C.13 Risk 3 is closed: the SDK runs its bundled binary from Electron main

_Superseded by C.42 (2026-10-14): the app no longer uses the SDK or bundles Claude Code._

No `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` and no `pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` were needed. `checkRuntime()` found `node_modules/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-win32-x64/claude.exe` and reported `state: 'ready'` with the account email and `Claude Max`. `pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` is passed only when the bundled binary is absent and a native install is found instead.

### C.14 A dev-only userData override (Phase 5a)

The author's saved settings point at their real vault, so a test run must not read them. `src/main/index.ts` honours `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_USER_DATA` and calls `app.setPath('userData', ...)` at module scope, before anything can read the path. Since `hardening.1` only development builds honour the variable, read in `src/main/devSwitches.ts`; a packaged build takes `--isolated-user-data=<dir>` instead (C.39). Verification runs used `.dev-userdata` in the repo (gitignored) with settings pointing at `samples/`. Since `setup.15` they start through `npm run dev:clean` (C.43), on `.dev-userdata` or a scratch folder given with `--user-data`, whose settings point at a scratch copy of the manuscript, never at `samples/` itself, so a save cannot change a tracked file (section 11, Safe verification). Nothing in the app depends on the variable being set.

### C.15 Find uses the CSS Custom Highlight API, not `findInPage` or `<mark>` (Phase 5a, item 11)

Wrapping matches in elements would split the text nodes inside hunk spans, which is exactly the structure the offsets and the absolutely positioned ghosts depend on. `webContents.findInPage` avoids that but cannot be scoped, so it would count and highlight ghost text as if it were the chapter. `CSS.highlights` paints ranges over the existing nodes: no DOM change at all, and the tree walker skips `.hunk__ghost`, so the count only refers to real prose. `src/renderer/state/find.ts` degrades to counting and scrolling without painting if the API is ever missing.

### C.16 Bulk actions only move pending suggestions (Phase 5a, item 8)

The old `setAllStatuses` overwrote every hunk, so "Dismiss all" silently threw away work the author had already accepted. The confirmation wording the author asked for ("Suggestions you already accepted are not changed") is now the actual rule: accept and dismiss target only `pending` hunks, and reset targets only decided ones. `bulkPlan` returns the affected ids with the sentence, and the store applies exactly those ids, so the count in the modal cannot drift from what happens.

### C.17 The position strip approximates by character offset (Phase 5a, item 14)

The marks beside the scrollbar are placed at `hunk.start / base.text.length`. Prose sets at a constant line height, so character position tracks vertical position closely enough for a locator, and the alternative, measuring every hunk element on every reflow, is not worth the cost for a hint. Clicking a mark focuses and scrolls to the real element, so the approximation never decides anything.

### C.18 The "Ã—" glyph was in the source, not in the pipeline (Phase 5a, item 1)

`App.tsx` contained the characters `Ã` (U+00C3) and `—` (U+2014) where a multiplication sign was meant: someone's editor had read UTF-8 `C3 97` as Windows-1252 and saved the result. The file itself is valid UTF-8, which is why the chips' `×` rendered correctly and only this one did not. Rather than fix the character, every control glyph in the renderer is now an inline SVG path in `src/renderer/components/icons.tsx`, which no encoding round trip can corrupt.

### C.19 Dev shortcuts open DevTools detached (Phase 5a, item 10)

With the application menu removed, F12, Ctrl+Shift+I and Ctrl+R are handled in `before-input-event` and only when the app is not packaged. `toggleDevTools()` did not open a window on this machine; explicit `isDevToolsOpened()` plus `openDevTools({ mode: 'detach' })` does, and a detached window leaves the manuscript at full width. Note for future test rounds: CDP `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` does not reach `before-input-event`, and `SendKeys` does not deliver `{F12}`; real key presses through `keybd_event` do.

### C.20 The packaged app must be handed the unpacked binary (Phase 5b, item 1; risk 4)

_Superseded by C.42 (2026-10-14): nothing of Anthropic's is packaged any more._

`asarUnpack` alone was not enough. `bundledBinary()` looked in `app.getAppPath()` first, which once packaged is `resources/app.asar`, and Electron's fs shim answers `existsSync` for an unpacked file using its **archive** path. So the check passed, the asar path was handed to the SDK, and the spawn failed with a misleading "binary exists but failed to launch ... this usually means the binary does not match this system's libc". The packaged app reported `state: 'error'` with a message about musl on a Windows machine.

Two changes fixed it. `bundledBinary()` now rejects any candidate inside an asar outright and looks in `resources/app.asar.unpacked` first, and `resolvedExecutable()` passes the located path to the SDK explicitly whenever the app is packaged, rather than leaving the SDK to resolve its own sibling package with `require.resolve` (which would land inside the archive for the same reason). Development is unchanged: the SDK still finds its own binary, as C.13 recorded. The config unpacks both `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-win32-x64/**` and `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk/**`.

### C.21 The app name is read out of the TypeScript source by two build scripts (Phase 5b, item 1)

`APP_NAME` lives in `src/shared/branding.ts`, which main and renderer import directly and `electron.vite.config.ts` substitutes into `index.html` through a `transformIndexHtml` plugin. The two consumers that cannot import TypeScript, `package.json` and the electron-builder config, read the constant out of that file with a regex in `scripts/branding.cjs`; `scripts/sync-branding.cjs` writes it into `package.json`'s `productName` and runs automatically as `prepackage`. It is not elegant, but it means renaming the app is a single edit, which is what the author asked for. Since `opensource.4` (2026-10-16) the same script also writes `repository`, `homepage` and `bugs` from `APP_REPOSITORY`. `npm run branding:check` runs it with `--check`, which writes nothing and exits 0 when package.json is in sync, or lists each field that differs and exits 1.

`APP_DATA_DIR` in the same file is deliberately **not** the display name.
