Anthropic's announcement of the new version, "2026 Usage Policy update" (2026-10-08), lists its changes without mentioning this clause. The same version names Claude Code users and the end users of products that integrate Claude among the people it binds, and its bypass bullet now includes circumventing "the account sign-up or log-in process"; the app never starts or handles a sign-in. https://www.anthropic.com/legal/aup and https://www.anthropic.com/news/2026-usage-policy-update
- **Help Center, Agent SDK with your plan.** Update of 2026-10-07: Max and Team plans now include monthly API credits that cover the Agent SDK, `claude -p`, the Claude API and Claude Managed Agents, and the SDK, `claude -p` and third-party apps can still be used "with your subscription limits". The June 15 notice that the planned change is paused is still there. The page's earlier text, including the promise of notice before changes, is gone. https://support.claude.com/en/articles/15036540-use-the-claude-agent-sdk-with-your-claude-plan
- **Help Center, Monthly API credits for Max and Team plans** (new on 2026-10-07). The credits cover `claude -p` and the Agent SDK when they run with an API key from the linked Console organisation. Signed in with a Claude plan instead, `claude -p` and Agent SDK usage "still draw from your plan's usage limits". That is how the app's reviews run: a review counts against the user's plan limits, or is billed to their key when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set. https://support.claude.com/en/articles/17154008-monthly-api-credits-for-max-and-team-plans
- **API credits for subscribers** (platform docs, first archived 2026-10-07). Max 5x $100 and Max 20x $200 a month; Team $20 per Standard seat and $100 per Premium seat, pooled and capped at $500; Free, Pro and Enterprise are not eligible. Its coverage table lists the Claude API, Claude Managed Agents, the Agent SDK and the Playground as covered, and Claude Code, extra usage in the Claude apps and other platforms as not covered. This page does not mention `claude -p`, while the Help Center says the credits cover it when run with a key from the linked organisation; that was not tested. The README and Setup must not say the credits pay for the app's reviews. https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/api-credits-for-subscribers
- **Enforcement in 2026.** In January, Anthropic said third-party harnesses using subscriptions are prohibited (https://venturebeat.com/technology/anthropic-cracks-down-on-unauthorized-claude-usage-by-third-party-harnesses). In March, OpenCode, a free open-source tool, removed its Pro/Max login after "anthropic legal requests", along with an Anthropic prompt file and other Anthropic references (https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/pull/18186). In April, third-party tools such as OpenClaw were moved off plan limits onto separately billed extra usage (https://techcrunch.com/2026/04/04/anthropic-says-claude-code-subscribers-will-need-to-pay-extra-for-openclaw-support/). In May and June, a separate billing plan for Agent SDK usage was announced and then paused. Searched through 2026-10-16: no report of action against an app that runs the unmodified binary with each user's own sign-in.
- **Asking Anthropic.** "Contact sales" leads to an Enterprise checkout with no form for individuals. A support request is a courtesy; silence is not approval.

### Claude Code pages re-read for setup (2026-10-19, `setup.13`)

Each page was read live on 2026-10-19; none carries a date of its own. Each bullet says what the page says now that bears on the app, then how the app stands against it. Information for the author, not legal advice. The installed Claude Code, 2.1.302, was asked only for its version and its help (Appendix C "No in-app sign-in launcher").

- **Legal and compliance** (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance). Nothing that bears on the app has changed since the 2026-10-16 reading above. The License section still puts Team, Enterprise and API users under the Commercial Terms and Free, Pro and Max users under the Consumer Terms. Running Claude Code in a product or service still needs the Commercial Terms unless agreed otherwise, on conditions: the binary is installed and run as Anthropic publishes it, no built-in sign-in method is removed, disabled or restricted, and no one pays for, resells or intermediates end users' usage; each end user signs in with their own API key, Claude plan or cloud provider credential, and is billed under their own agreement. The name-and-logo paragraph is unchanged. Under authentication: OAuth is for people who bought a Claude plan, for ordinary use of Claude Code and Anthropic's own apps; developers building products should use API keys; third-party developers may not offer Claude.ai login in their own applications or route requests through Free, Pro or Max credentials for their users, nor collect, store or intermediate Claude.ai credentials or session tokens, and sign-in completes through Anthropic's own flow; none of this stops an end user signing in to the unmodified Claude Code with their own subscription; Anthropic may enforce without notice. The OAuth bullet now links the Help Center's article on logging in. The app: as before (section 9 risk 8); Setup shows commands and starts no sign-in (Appendix C "No in-app sign-in launcher").
- **Authentication** (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/authentication). Which credential wins, highest first: a cloud provider chosen by `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_VERTEX` or `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_FOUNDRY`; `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, which a terminal session asks about once but `-p` always uses when it is there; an `apiKeyHelper`; `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`; Anthropic profile and federation credentials; the subscription sign-in from `/login`. On Windows the login is kept in `%USERPROFILE%\.claude\.credentials.json`, or under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when that is set. A login within three days of its end warns at start-up in a terminal session; once it has ended and cannot be renewed, every request fails with "Login expired · Please run /login". A Console sign-in without an API key (2.1.242 and later) keeps an Anthropic profile outside the configuration folder (`%APPDATA%\Anthropic` on Windows, auth mode `user_oauth`), ranked below a working `/login`, and signs Claude Code out of any claude.ai login stored on the machine (that last point read again for the `setup.13` review, 2026-10-19). The app: the README's API key route, and Setup's line that the key is used in place of the plan, hold, since reviews run with `-p`; `apiKeyHelper` lives in a settings file, which the app does not load, as Setup's troubleshooting says (11.3 c); "Login expired" is read as `claude-login-expired` by `classifyClaudeFailure`'s expiry rule, so Setup shows the sign-in-ended view. An `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` was tried with a dummy token on 2026-10-19 (`setup.17`, Appendix C "An ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN is the token, not a Claude plan"): 2.1.302's `auth status --json` says `loggedIn` true, `authMethod` `oauth_token` and no key, and its `initialize` reply names `tokenSource` `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` with no email, plan or key, with an empty configuration folder and over this machine's Max sign-in alike, which the token displaces (Claude Code then warns that the claude.ai connectors are off); beside an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` both answers name the key's source too and `auth status` still says `oauth_token`. The check read the token as a Claude plan, through `oauth_token`, until `setup.17`, and now names it. Not tried: how they describe a profile sign-in; one with an email and no API key would probably read as a Claude plan, and Test the connection's note would then say it counts against the plan. `release.13` tries a keyless Console sign-in just before `setup.14`'s sign-in, which brings back the claude.ai login the Console sign-in removes; until then the README does not offer `claude auth login --console`.
- **CLI reference, `auth status`** (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference). `claude auth status` prints JSON, or prose with `--text`, and exits with 0 when logged in and 1 when not. The JSON's `authMethod` is one of `none`, `claude.ai`, `oauth_token`, `api_key`, `api_key_helper` and `third_party`, and from 2.1.268 the JSON also names the configuration folder in `configDirectory`. `claude auth login` takes `--email`, `--sso` and `--console`. The app: `credentialFromAuthMethod` maps exactly those five methods beside `none`; `runClaudeCli` keeps the answer of an exit code 1, since signed out is an answer; `parseAuthStatus` keeps only its six fields, so the folder's path is never kept or logged. The page also lists every flag of 11.3 (q) with the meaning the app relies on, `--max-turns` among them (print mode only, no limit by default), and warns that `claude --help` does not list every flag: 2.1.302's `--help` leaves out `--max-turns`, which 2.1.266's listed, and which `setup.8`'s real test ran with on 2.1.302 (C.63). `release.10`'s per-release check of the flags reads this page for a flag `--help` leaves out.
- **Troubleshoot installation and login** (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/troubleshoot-install). On Windows the PowerShell installer puts `claude.exe` in `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin`, and when that folder is not on PATH it says so, with the fix, in its "Setup notes", without changing PATH itself; CMD has an installer line of its own. WinGet (`winget install Anthropic.ClaudeCode`) and Homebrew (`brew install --cask claude-code`, the stable channel, about a week behind; `claude-code@latest` for the newest) do not update themselves. An npm install on Windows meets PowerShell's execution policy at `claude.ps1`, and `claude.cmd` is the way round it. An older Claude Desktop can register a `Claude.exe` in the `WindowsApps` folder that comes first on PATH. A self-update that failed can leave no `claude.exe`, only a `claude.exe.old.*` backup to rename back (before 2.1.281 the backup could be deleted too). For sign-in: when no browser opens, `claude auth login` prints the link and reads a pasted code from the terminal, which is also the fallback on Windows when pasting into the interactive prompt fails; an expired token is cured by signing in again; an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` left from an old employer or project overrides a subscription and can fail with "This organization has been disabled". The app: the install line signs in through the full path and the full-path forms are right; Troubleshooting's "open a new terminal" helped only when the folder was already on PATH, so since `setup.17` its answer leads with the full-path form where Setup shows one and names the installer's folder where it does not (5.6 item 5); the npm answer stands; a `WindowsApps` alias is never taken by the lookup, which leaves that folder out and whose `canRun` refuses an alias anyway, but a terminal runs it when it comes first on PATH, which Troubleshooting now answers on the Windows tab (5.6 items 1 and 5, `setup.17`); the disabled organisation is `API_KEY_REFUSED_MESSAGE`'s case; a `claude.exe` lost in an update reads as not installed, and the not-installed install line puts it back.
- **Model configuration** (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config). Default is Opus 5.5 on Pro, Max, Team, Enterprise and the API since 2.1.280, and was Sonnet 5 on Pro and Team Standard before (Appendix C "Default is Opus 5.5 on Pro since CLI 2.1.280", with the order of the choices that decide it). On the Anthropic API `opus` became Opus 5.5 at 2.1.280, `sonnet` Sonnet 5.5 at 2.1.284 and `haiku` Haiku 5.5 at 2.1.293; `fable` is Fable 5.1 from 2.1.257. A `/model` choice made in `-p` mode lasts for that session only. The app: `RECOMMENDED_CLAUDE_CODE`, 2.1.283, was set for Opus 5.5 and predates Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5, so a copy between 2.1.283 and 2.1.292 gets no update note while it lacks one of the newest models; `release.10` moves the yardstick each release.

### OpenAI (research dated 2026-09-11, Phase 5c)

- OpenAI's documented route for a third-party app is an API key from a Platform account, billed per token to that account. That is what the OpenAI provider uses. The SDK reads `OPENAI_API_KEY` by default and the app passes a stored key explicitly. https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/structured-outputs and https://platform.openai.com/docs/api-reference/responses
- Signing in with a ChatGPT subscription (Plus, Pro, Team) inside a third-party app has no stated permission. The only subscription sign-in OpenAI documents is for its own Codex tools; public requests for a "Sign in with ChatGPT" for other apps are still open feature requests, and routing a Codex login through another app is described in the community as unsupported. So the app offers no ChatGPT sign-in and says in the dialog that OpenAI reviews are billed per token, not to a ChatGPT subscription. Sources seen: https://github.com/openai/codex/issues/10974 (404 when re-checked on 2026-10-16) , https://community.openai.com/t/login-with-chatgpt-allow-users-to-use-their-own-plus-subscription-in-3rd-party-apps/1378506 , https://community.openai.com/t/api-chatgpt-subscription-cannot-use-api-with-chatgpt-subscription/875542
- Prices for the cost estimate, standard tier: https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing (copied into `src/shared/ai/pricing.ts` on 2026-09-11).
